2023年6月

第 1 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,364 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）288 词 · 12 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) affirmedB) approximatelyC) completelyD) differentiateE) distinctF) domainG) formidableH) outperformedI) overstatedJ) pledgeK) previouslyL) prospectiveM) proveN) surpassO) volume

Scientists recently examined studies on dog intelligence and compared them with research into the minds of other intelligent animals.

科学家们最近对有关犬类智力的研究进行了梳理，并将其与其他智能动物心智方面的研究作了比较。

The researchers found that dogs are among the more intelligent carnivores, social hunters and domestic animals, but that their intelligence does not surpass other intelligent animals in any of those categories.

研究人员发现，犬类在食肉动物、社会性猎食动物和家养动物中属于较为聪明的，但其智力在上述任何一类动物中都未超越其他智能动物。

Though a significant body of research has examined dog cognition previously, the authors of this new study found little to warrant the volume of work that has been devoted to the topic.

尽管此前已有大量研究考察过犬类认知，但这项新研究的作者发现，几乎没有什么依据足以支撑围绕这一课题所投入的巨大研究量。

Stephen Lea, lead author of the new study, argues that many researchers seem to have designed their studies to prove how clever dogs are, rather than simply to study dogs' brains.

这项新研究的第一作者斯蒂芬·利指出，许多研究人员似乎从设计之初就是为了证明狗有多聪明，而不是单纯地研究狗的大脑。

Lea and a colleague examined more than 300 studies of dog cognition, comparing the studies' results with those from research into other animals.

利和一位同事审阅了300多项犬类认知研究，并将这些研究的结果与其他动物的研究结果进行了比较。

The researchers made specific comparisons between the different species in different categories of intelligence.

研究人员针对不同物种在不同智力类别上进行了具体比较。

These comparisons affirmed that dogs are intelligent, but their intelligence is not as formidable as some researchers might have believed.

这些比较证实了狗确实具有智力，但其智力并不像某些研究人员所认为的那样强大。

In many areas, though, comparisons were not possible.

不过，在许多方面，比较无法进行。

For example, the researchers noted that both dogs and cats are known to be able to recognize and differentiate human voices.

例如，研究人员指出，众所周知，狗和猫都能识别和分辨人声。

But the investigators could not find any data to indicate which species can remember a greater number of distinct human voices, so it was impossible to compare the two on that front.

但调查人员找不到任何数据来说明哪一物种能够记住更多不同的声音，因此无法在这一方面对两者进行比较。

However, not all researchers agree completely with the findings of this study.

然而，并非所有研究人员都完全认同这项研究的结论。

Zachary Silver, an American researcher, believes the authors of the new study overstated the idea that an excessive amount of research has been devoted to dogs, as the field of dog cognition is young, and there is much to be learned about how dogs think.

美国研究人员扎卡里·西尔弗认为，新研究的作者夸大了对犬类研究投入过多这一观点，因为犬类认知这一研究领域尚属新兴，对于狗如何思考仍有许多未知之处有待探索。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1179 词 · 53 句

A

Gratitude may be more beneficial than we commonly suppose.

感恩的益处可能远超我们通常的想象。

One recent study asked participants to write a note of thanks to someone and then estimate how surprised and happy the recipient would feel—an impact that they consistently underestimated.

最近的一项研究请参与者给某人写一封感谢信，然后估计收信人会感到多么惊喜和高兴——结果他们始终低估了这份影响。

Another study assessed the health benefits of writing thank-you notes.

另一项研究评估了写感谢信对健康的益处。

The researchers found that writing as few as three weekly thank-you notes over the course of three weeks improved life satisfaction, increased happy feelings and reduced symptoms of depression.

研究人员发现，在三周的时间里每周写区区三封感谢信，就能提升生活满意度、增加幸福感并减轻抑郁症状。

B

While this research into gratitude is relatively new, the principles involved are anything but.

尽管关于感恩的研究起步较晚，但其中蕴含的原则却绝非新鲜事物。

Students of mine in a political philosophy course at Indiana University are reading Daniel Defoe's 300-year-old Robinson Crusoe, often regarded as the first novel published in English.

我在印第安纳大学政治哲学课上的学生们正在阅读丹尼尔·笛福三百年前写就的《鲁滨逊漂流记》，该书常被视为英语世界出版的第一部小说。

Left alone on an unknown island with no apparent prospect of rescue or escape, Crusoe has much to lament.

独自困在一座荒岛上，既看不到获救的希望，也找不到逃生的可能，鲁滨逊有太多可以哀叹的事。

But instead of giving in to despair, he makes a list of things for which he is grateful, including the fact that he is the sole survivor from the shipwreck and has been able to salvage many useful items from the wreckage.

但他没有屈服于绝望，而是列出了一份感恩清单，其中包括：他是海难中唯一的幸存者，并且已从残骸中打捞出许多有用的物品。

C

Defoe's masterpiece, which is often ranked as one of the world's greatest novels, provides a portrait of gratitude in action that is as timely and relevant today as it has ever been.

笛福的这部杰作常被评为世界最伟大的小说之一，它为我们描绘了一幅将感恩付诸行动的图景，其意义在今天与以往任何时候一样及时而切中时弊。

It is also one with which contemporary psychology and medicine are just beginning to catch up.

这同时也是当代心理学和医学才刚刚开始追赶的一个领域。

Simply put, for most of us, it is far more helpful to focus on the things in life for which we can express gratitude than those that incline us toward resentment and lamentation.

简而言之，对我们大多数人来说，把注意力放在生活中值得感恩的事物上，远比纠结那些让我们怨愤和哀叹的事情更有益。

D

When we focus on the things we regret, such as failed relationships, family disputes, and setbacks in career and finance, we tend to become more regretful.

当我们一味关注令人遗憾的事情，比如失败的恋情、家庭纷争以及事业和财务上的挫折时，我们往往会变得更加懊悔。

Conversely, when we focus on the things we are grateful for, a greater sense of happiness tends to spread through our lives.

相反，当我们把注意力放在值得感恩的事物上时，更强烈的幸福感往往会渗透到我们生活的方方面面。

And while no one would argue for cultivating a false sense of blessedness, there is mounting evidence that counting our blessings is one of the best habits we can develop to promote mental and physical health.

虽然没有人会主张培养一种虚假的幸福感，但越来越多的证据表明，学会感恩是我们所能养成的、有助于促进身心健康的最佳习惯之一。

E

Gratitude has long enjoyed a privileged position in many of the world's cultural traditions.

在世界诸多文化传统中，感恩长期以来都享有特殊的地位。

For example, some ancient Western philosophers counsel gratitude that is both enduring and complete, and some Eastern thinkers portray it as not merely an attitude but a virtue to be put into practice.

例如，一些西方古代哲学家主张感恩应持久而彻底，一些东方思想家则将其描述为一种不仅停留在态度层面、更应付诸实践的美德。

F

Recent scientific studies support these ancient teachings.

近年来的科学研究印证了这些古老教诲。

Individuals who regularly engage in gratitude exercises, such as counting their blessings or expressing gratitude to others, exhibit increased satisfaction with relationships and fewer symptoms of physical illness.

经常进行感恩练习的人，例如列举自己的福分或向他人表达谢意，在人际关系中会表现出更高的满意度，身体疾病的症状也更少。

And the benefits are not only psychological and physical.

而感恩的好处并不局限于心理和身体层面。

They may also be moral—those who practice gratitude also view their lives less materialistically and suffer from less envy.

还可能体现在道德层面——那些践行感恩的人对生活的物质追求也更淡然，嫉妒心理也更少。

G

There are multiple explanations for such benefits of gratefulness.

关于感恩为何能带来这些益处，有多种解释。

One is the fact that expressing gratitude encourages others to continue being generous, thus promoting a virtuous cycle of goodness in relationships.

其中一个原因是，表达感激会鼓励他人继续保持慷慨，从而在人际关系中形成善意的良性循环。

Similarly, grateful people may be more likely to reciprocate with acts of kindness of their own.

同样，懂得感恩的人可能也更愿意以善意的行为回馈他人。

Broadly speaking, a community in which people feel grateful to one another is likely to be a more pleasant place to live than one characterized by mutual suspicion and resentment.

从整体上看，一个人与人之间相互感激的社区，往往会比一个充斥着相互猜疑和怨恨的社区更令人愉悦。

The beneficial effects of gratitude may extend even further.

感恩的益处可能远不止于此。

For example, when many people feel good about what someone else has done for them, they experience a sense of being lifted up, with a corresponding enhancement of their regard for humanity.

例如，当许多人因别人为自己所做的事而心怀感激时，他们会感到精神为之一振，同时也更加尊重人类。

Some are inspired to attempt to become better people themselves, doing more to help bring out the best in others and bringing more goodness into the world around them.

有些人因此受到激励，努力让自己成为更好的人，更多地帮助他人发挥其最美好的一面，并为周围的世界带来更多善意。

H

Gratitude also tends to strengthen a sense of connection with others.

感恩还往往能增强人与人之间的联结感。

When people want to do good things that inspire gratitude, the level of dedication in relationships tends to grow and relationships seem to last longer.

当人们乐于做那些能激发感恩之事时，人际关系中的投入程度往往会加深，关系也似乎更加持久。

And when people feel more connected, they are more likely to choose to spend their time with one another and demonstrate their feelings of affection in daily acts.

而当人们感到彼此更加亲近时，他们更可能选择共度时光，并在日常举动中表达关爱之情。

I

Of course, acts of kindness can also foster discomfort.

当然，善举也可能带来不安。

For example, if people feel they are not worthy of kindness or suspect that some ulterior motive lies behind it, the benefits of gratitude will not be realized.

例如，如果人们觉得自己不配得到善待，或怀疑善举背后另有隐情，感恩的益处便无法实现。

Likewise, receiving a kindness can give rise to a sense of indebtedness, leaving beneficiaries feeling that they must now pay back whatever good they have received.

同样，接受善意也会让人产生负债感，使受惠者觉得必须回报所受之恩。

Gratitude can flourish only if people are secure enough in themselves and sufficiently trusting to allow it to do so.

只有当人们内心足够安全、足够信任他人时，感恩才能真正滋长。

Another obstacle to gratitude is often called a sense of entitlement.

感恩的另一个障碍通常被称为理应得利感。

Instead of experiencing a benefaction as a good turn, people sometimes regard it as a mere payment of what they are owed, for which no one deserves any moral credit.

人们有时并不把受助视为别人的一番好意，而是将其看作对方在偿还所欠之债，因而无人值得任何道德上的褒奖。

J

There are a number of practical steps anyone can take to promote a sense of gratitude.

任何人都可以采取一些切实可行的做法来培养感恩之心。

One is simply spending time on a regular basis thinking about someone who has made a difference, or perhaps writing a thank-you note or expressing such gratitude in person.

其中之一，就是定期花些时间想想那个曾对自己产生重要影响的人，或者写一封感谢信，抑或当面向对方表达谢意。

Others are found in ancient religious disciplines, such as reflecting on benefactions received from another person or actually praying for the health and happiness of a benefactor.

另一些做法见于古老的宗教修习，比如默想他人给予自己的恩惠，或者切实地为恩人的健康与幸福祈祷。

In addition to benefactions received, it is also possible to focus on opportunities to do good oneself, whether those acted on in the past or hoped for in the future.

除了感恩所受的恩惠，也可以把注意力放在自己行善的机会上，无论这些机会是过去已经把握的，还是将来有望实现的。

Some people are most grateful not for what others have done for them but for chances they enjoyed to help others.

有些人最感激的，不是别人为他们做了什么，而是自己曾有过帮助他人的机会。

In regularly reflecting on the things in his life he is grateful for, Defoe's Crusoe believes that he becomes a far better person than he would have been had he remained in the society from which he originally set out on his voyage.

笛福笔下的鲁滨逊·克鲁索经常反思生活中值得感恩的事物，他相信，与留在当初启程远航时所在的那个社会相比，自己因此成为了远为更好的人。

K

Reflecting on generosity and gratitude, the great basketball coach John Wooden once offered two counsels to his players and students.

伟大的篮球教练约翰·伍登在谈及慷慨与感恩时，曾向他的球员和学生提出两条忠告。

First, he said, "It is impossible to have a perfect day unless you have done something for someone who will never be able to repay you."

他说，第一条是："除非你为某个永远无力回报你的人做些什么，否则你不可能拥有完美的一天。"

In saying this, Wooden sought to promote purely generous acts, as opposed to those performed with an expectation of reward.

说这番话，伍登意在倡导纯粹出于慷慨的行为，而不是那些指望回报才做的行为。

Second, he said, "Give thanks for your blessings every day."

他说，第二条是："每天都要为你的福分感恩。"

L

Some faith traditions incorporate such practices into the rhythm of daily life.

有些信仰传统把这类做法融入日常生活之中。

For example, adherents of some religions offer prayers of thanksgiving every morning before rising and every night before lying down to sleep.

例如，某些宗教的信徒每天早晨起床前和每天晚上入睡前都要做感恩祈祷。

Others offer thanks throughout the day, such as before meals.

另一些人则一天中多次表达感恩，比如在饭前。

Other less frequent special events, such as births, deaths and marriages, may also be heralded by such prayers.

其他不太常见的特殊事件，如出生、死亡和结婚等，也可能伴随着这样的祈祷。

M

When Defoe depicted Robinson Crusoe making thanksgiving a daily part of his island life, he was anticipating findings in social science and medicine that would not appear for hundreds of years.

当笛福笔下的鲁滨逊·克鲁索把感恩作为荒岛日常生活的一部分时，他其实预见了数百年后才出现的社会科学和医学研究发现。

Yet he was also reflecting the wisdom of religious and philosophical traditions that extend back thousands of years.

然而，他也同时反映了绵延数千年的宗教与哲学传统中的智慧。

Gratitude is one of the healthiest and most nourishing of all states of mind, and those who adopt it as a habit are enriching not only their own lives but also the lives of those around them.

感恩是最健康、最有益于心灵滋养的心态之一，那些将其养成习惯的人，不仅在丰富自己的生活，也在丰富身边人的生活。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1447 词 · 22 句

P1

Technology is never a neutral tool for achieving human ends.

技术从来都不是实现人类目的的中立工具。

Technological innovations reshape people as they use these innovations to control their environment.

人们在利用这些创新成果来控制自身环境的同时，技术创新也在重塑着他们。

Artificial intelligence, for example, is altering humanity.

例如，人工智能正在改变着人性本身。

P2

While the term AI conjures up anxieties about killer robots or catastrophic levels of unemployment, there are other, deeper implications.

虽然一提到人工智能，人们便会联想到杀人机器人或灾难性失业水平等令人焦虑的图景，但它还有其他更深层的含义。

As AI increasingly shapes the human experience, how does this change what it means to be human?

随着人工智能日益塑造着人类的经验体验，这将如何改变"何为人类"的内涵？

Central to the problem is a person's capacity to make choices, particularly judgments that have moral implications.

这一问题的核心在于人做出选择的能力，尤其是做出具有道德含义的判断的能力。

P3

Aristotle argued that the capacity for making practical judgments depends on regularly making them—on habit and practice.

亚里士多德认为，做出实践判断的能力取决于经常做出这类判断——取决于习惯与实践。

We see the emergence of machines as substitute judges in a variety of everyday contexts as a potential threat to people learning how to effectively exercise judgment themselves.

我们看到，机器作为替代性裁判出现在各种日常场景中，这可能会威胁到人们学习如何有效行使自身判断力。

P4

In the workplace, managers routinely make decisions about who to hire or fire and which loan to approve, to name a few.

在职场中，管理者例行性地决定录用或解雇谁、批准哪笔贷款等等，不一而足。

These are areas where algorithmic prescription is replacing human judgment, and so people who might have had the chance to develop practical judgment in these areas no longer will.

这些正是算法规定正在取代人类判断的领域，因此，那些原本可能有机会在这些领域中培养实践判断力的人，将不再拥有这样的机会。

P5

Recommendation engines, which are increasingly prevalent intermediaries in people's consumption of culture, may serve to constrain choice and minimize luck.

推荐引擎作为人们文化消费中日益普遍的中介，可能会限制人们的选择、减少偶然性。

By presenting consumers with algorithmically selected choices of what to watch, read, stream and visit next, companies are replacing human taste with machine taste.

企业通过向消费者呈现算法筛选出的下一步观看、阅读、流媒体播放和访问的内容，正以机器的品味取代人的品味。

In one sense, this is helpful.

从某种意义上说，这是有帮助的。

After all, machines can survey a wider range of choices than any individual is likely to have the time or energy to do on their own.

毕竟，机器能够审视的选择范围，比任何个人凭自己的时间和精力可能做到的都要广。

P6

At the same time, though, this selection is optimizing for what people are likely to prefer based on what they've preferred in the past.

然而与此同时，这种筛选依据的是人们过去所偏好的东西，来优化他们未来可能偏好的选择。

We think there is some risk that people's options will be constrained by their past in a new and unanticipated way.

我们认为，人们的可选项有可能会以一种全新而始料未及的方式被自身过去所限定，这存在一定风险。

P7

As machine learning algorithms improve and as they train on more extensive data sets, larger parts of everyday life are likely to become utterly predictable.

随着机器学习算法不断改进，且用更庞杂的数据集加以训练，日常生活的更大部分很可能变得完全可预测。

The predictions are going to get better and better, and they will ultimately make common experiences more efficient and pleasant.

预测会越来越准确，最终将使常见体验变得更高效、更令人愉悦。

P8

Algorithms could soon—if they don't already—have a better idea about which show you'd like to watch next and which job candidate you should hire than you do.

算法可能很快——若非已然如此——就比你自己更清楚你接下来想看什么节目、该录用哪位求职者。

One day, humans may even find a way for machines to make these decisions without some of the biases that humans typically display.

终有一天，人类甚至可能找到办法，让机器做出这些决定，同时避免人类通常表现出的某些偏见。

P9

But to the extent that unpredictability is part of how people understand themselves and part of what people like about themselves, humanity is in the process of losing something significant.

然而，就不可预测性既是人们理解自我的一部分、也是人们喜爱自身之处的一部分而言，人类正在失去某种意义重大的东西。

As they become more and more predictable, the creatures inhabiting the increasingly AI-mediated world will become less and less like us.

随着它们变得越来越可预测，栖居于这个日益由人工智能中介的世界中的生灵，将变得越来越不像我们人类。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2450 词 · 28 句

P1

Phonics, which involves sounding out words syllable by syllable, is the best way to teach children to read.

自然拼读法（即逐个音节拼读单词）是教孩子阅读的最佳方法。

But in many classrooms, this can be a dirty word.

但在许多课堂上，它却成了一个禁忌。

So much so that some teachers have had to sneak phonics teaching materials into the classroom.

以至于有些教师不得不把自然拼读的教学材料偷偷带进课堂。

Most American children are taught to read in a way that study after study has found to be wrong.

大多数美国孩子被教导阅读的方式，一项又一项研究已经证明是错误的。

P2

The consequences of this are striking.

这种情况的后果触目惊心。

Less than half of all American adults were proficient readers in 2017.

2017年，美国成年人中阅读能力达到熟练水平的不足一半。

American fourth graders rank 15th on the Progress in International Literacy Study, an international exam.

在国际读写素养进展研究这一国际考试中，美国四年级学生排名第15位。

P3

America is stuck in a debate about teaching children to read that has been going on for decades.

美国深陷于一场关于儿童阅读教学的争论之中，这场争论已经持续了几十年。

Some advocate teaching symbol-sound relationships (the sound k can be spelled as c, k, ck, or ch), known as phonics.

一些人主张教授符号与发音之间的对应关系（例如/k/这个音可以拼写为c、k、ck或ch），这就是所谓的自然拼读法。

Others support an immersive approach (using pictures of a cat to learn the word cat), known as "whole language".

另一些人则支持沉浸式方法（例如用猫的图片来学习cat这个单词），这就是所谓的“整体语言”教学法。

Most teachers today, almost three out of four according to a survey by the Ed Week Research Centre in 2019, use a mix called "balanced literacy".

如今的大多数教师——据《教育周刊》研究中心2019年的一项调查，近四分之三——采用的是一种被称为“均衡读写”的混合方法。

This combination of methods is ineffective.

这种把各种方法混合在一起的做法并无实效。

"You can't sprinkle in a little phonics," says Tenette Smith, executive director of elementary education and reading at Mississippi's education department.

“你不能只在教学中零星掺入一点自然拼读法，”密西西比州教育部小学教育与阅读执行主任特内特·史密斯说。

"It has to be systematic and explicitly taught."

“它必须系统化，并且明确地教给学生。”

P4

Mississippi, often behind in social policy, has set an example here.

密西西比州在社会政策方面常常落在后面，却在这件事上树立了榜样。

In a state once notorious for its low reading scores, the Mississippi state legislature passed new literacy standards in 2013.

在一个曾因阅读成绩低下而臭名昭著的州，密西西比州议会于2013年通过了新的读写能力标准。

Since then Mississippi has seen remarkable gains.

自那以来，密西西比州取得了显著进步。

Its fourth graders have moved from 49th (out of 50 states) to 29th on the National Assessment of Educational Progress, a nationwide exam.

在全美教育进步评估（一项全国性考试）中，该州四年级学生的排名从第49位（在50个州中）升至第29位。

In 2019 it was the only state to improve its scores.

2019年，它是唯一一个分数有所提高的州。

For the first time since measurement began, Mississippi's pupils are now average readers, a remarkable achievement in such a poor state.

自开始测量以来，密西西比州的学生如今首次达到了平均阅读水平，对于这样一个贫困州而言，这是一项了不起的成就。

P5

Mississippi's success is attributed to implementing reading methods supported by a body of research known as the science of reading.

密西西比州的成功归功于采用了得到一批被称为“阅读科学”的研究支持的阅读教学方法。

In 1997 Congress requested the National Institute of Child Health and Human Development and the Department of Education to convene a National Reading Panel to end the "reading wars" and synthesize the evidence.

1997年，国会要求美国国家儿童健康与人类发展研究院和教育部召集成立一个全国阅读小组，以终结“阅读战争”并综合相关证据。

The panel found that phonics, along with explicit instruction in phonemic awareness, fluency and comprehension, worked best.

该小组发现，自然拼读法配合对音素意识、流利度和理解力的明确教学，效果最好。

P6

Yet over two decades on, "balanced literacy" is still being taught in classrooms.

然而二十多年过去了，“平衡读写教学法”仍在课堂上被使用。

But advances in statistics and brain imaging have disproved the whole-language method.

然而，统计学和脑成像技术的进步已经推翻了全语言教学法。

To the teacher who is a proficient reader, literacy seems like a natural process that requires educated guessing, rather than the deliberate process emphasized by phonics.

对于阅读熟练的教师而言，识字似乎是一个自然而然的过程，需要的是有根据的推测，而不是拼读法所强调的那种刻意的练习过程。

Teachers can imagine that they learned to read through osmosis when they were children.

教师们可能会以为自己小时候是潜移默化地学会阅读的。

Without proper training, they bring this to classrooms.

如果缺乏适当的培训，他们就会把这种观念带进课堂。

第 2 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,371 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）285 词 · 11 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) attributesB) comprisedC) concededD) conservationE) diminishingF) farG) inclinedH) indulgingI) innumerableJ) mortalityK) oddsL) plightsM) regulatoryN) stillO) unmatchable

Imagine sitting down to a big dinner: a massive steak, a large portion of fried potatoes, and cake for dessert.

想象一下坐下来享用一顿丰盛的大餐：一大块牛排、一大份炸土豆，还有作为甜点的蛋糕。

After eating so much, you should be too full to eat another bite.

吃了这么多之后，你应该已经撑得一口也吃不下了。

But some people experience a powerful urge to keep eating, even after indulging in a huge meal, a behavior that makes little sense, as most adults are well-versed in the dangers of obesity, which researchers have shown correlates with innumerable health issues and is even linked to increased mortality risk.

然而，有些人即使在大吃一顿之后，仍会产生强烈的继续进食的冲动——这种行为令人费解，因为大多数成年人都深知肥胖的危害，而研究人员已证实，肥胖与无数健康问题相关，甚至会增加死亡风险。

But some people still keep eating long after they should stop, a phenomenon Dr. Susan Thompson calls "insatiable hunger."

但有些人仍然在本该停止进食之后继续大快朵颐，苏珊·汤普森博士将这种现象称为"永不满足的饥饿"。

It is characterized by two main attributes: not being satisfied by eating, and having a desire to stay sedentary.

它具有两大特征：一是进食无法带来满足感，二是倾向于久坐不动。

This is at odds with how humans are biologically programmed.

这与人类的生理本能背道而驰。

When there was a great deal of food available, ancient people would gorge on calories; this massive calorie intake was accompanied by an urge to get active.

当食物充足时，古人会大量摄入热量；而这种热量的大量摄入往往伴随着想要活动身体的冲动。

Humans were also programmed for something called "compensation," which is the brain's regulatory mechanism for preventing the accumulation of excess weight.

人类的本能中还包含一种名为"补偿"的机制，即大脑为防止体重过度增加而进行的调节机制。

With compensation, if you eat one large meal in the morning, you are naturally inclined to eat less for the rest of the day.

在补偿机制的作用下，如果你早上吃了一顿大餐，一天中剩下的时间自然会倾向于少吃。

But recent studies show that 70% of American adults have lost the ability to naturally compensate for the calories they consume; worse still, a significant number of them report diminishing hunger halfway through an eating session, but, by the end of the meal, they feel the same or higher levels of hunger than when they sat down.

但近期研究表明，70%的美国成年人已经丧失了对摄入热量进行自然补偿的能力；更糟糕的是，其中相当一部分人反映，在进食过程中饥饿感会逐渐减弱，但到了用餐结束时，他们的饥饿感却与刚坐下时持平甚至更强烈。

Dr. Thompson argues that the main cause of this phenomenon is the modern diet, which is comprised of food high in sugar, carbohydrates and calories.

汤普森博士认为，造成这一现象的主要原因是现代饮食——它由高糖、高碳水化合物和高热量的食物构成。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1187 词 · 54 句

A

When psychologist Jessica Pryor lived near an internationally renowned university, she once saw a student walking into a library holding a sleeping bag and a coffee maker.

心理学家杰西卡·普赖尔曾住在一所国际知名大学附近，有一次她看到一名学生背着睡袋、端着咖啡机走进图书馆。

She has heard of graduate students spending 12 to 18 hours at a time in the lab.

她听说过有的研究生一次在实验室里待上12到18个小时。

Their schedules are meant to be literally punishing: If they are scientists-in-training, they won't allow themselves to watch Netflix until their experiments start generating results.

他们的日程安排简直就是自我折磨：如果他们是正在接受训练的科学家，他们就不会允许自己在实验开始出结果之前看Netflix。

"Relationships become estranged—people stop inviting them to social gatherings or dinner parties, which leads them to spend even more time in the lab," Pryor told me.

"人际关系变得疏远——人们不再邀请他们参加社交聚会或晚宴，这反过来又让他们在实验室里花更多时间，"普赖尔告诉我。

B

Along with other therapists, Pryor, who is now with the Family Institute at Northwestern University, is trying to sound the alarm about a tendency among young adults and college students to strive for perfection in their work—sometimes at any cost.

与普赖尔现在任职于西北大学家庭研究所，她与其他心理治疗师一起，正在就年轻人和大学生中一种追求工作完美（有时不惜一切代价）的倾向发出警报。

Though it is often portrayed as a positive trait, Pryor and others say extreme perfectionism can lead to depression, anxiety, and even suicide.

虽然追求完美常被描绘成一种积极的品质，但普赖尔等人指出，极端的完美主义可能导致抑郁、焦虑甚至自杀。

C

What's more, perfectionism seems to be on the rise.

更重要的是，完美主义似乎正在加剧。

In a study of thousands of American, Canadian, and British college students published earlier this year, Thomas Curran of the University of Bath and Andrew Hill of York St. John University found that today's college students report higher levels of perfectionism than college students did during the 1990s or early 2000s.

巴斯大学的托马斯·柯伦和约克圣约翰大学的安德鲁·希尔在今年早些时候发表的一项针对数千名美国、加拿大和英国大学生的研究中发现，当今大学生报告的完美主义程度高于20世纪90年代或21世纪初的大学生。

They measured three types of perfectionism: self-oriented, or a desire to be perfect; socially prescribed, or a desire to live up to others' expectations; and other-oriented, or holding others to unrealistic standards.

他们测量了三种类型的完美主义：自我导向型，即渴望做到完美；社会规定型，即渴望达到他人的期望；以及他人导向型，即以不切实际的标准要求他人。

From 1989 to 2016, they found, self-oriented perfectionism scores increased by 10 percent, socially prescribed scores rose by 33 percent, and other-oriented perfectionism increased by 16 percent.

他们发现，从1989年到2016年，自我导向型完美主义得分上升了10%，社会规定型得分上升了33%，他人导向型完美主义上升了16%。

D

A person living with an other-oriented perfectionist might feel criticized by the perfectionist spouse for not doing household chores exactly the "right" way.

与一个他人导向型完美主义者共同生活的人，可能会因为家务没有按照完全"正确"的方式做而受到完美主义配偶的批评。

"One of the most common things couples argue about is the proper way of loading the dishwasher," says Amy Bach, a psychologist in Providence, Rhode Island.

"夫妻之间最常见的争吵之一，就是往洗碗机里放碗碟的正确方式，"罗德岛州普罗维登斯的心理学家艾米·巴赫说。

E

Curran describes socially prescribed perfectionism as "My self-esteem is contingent on what other people think."

柯伦将社会规定型完美主义描述为：“我的自尊取决于别人怎么看我。”

His study didn't examine the causal reasons for its rise, but he assumes that the rise of both standardized testing and social media might play a role.

他的研究并未探究这一现象加剧的因果原因，但他认为标准化考试和社交媒体的兴起可能起到了一定作用。

These days, LinkedIn alerts us when our rival gets a new job, and Instagram can let us know how well "liked" our lives are compared with a friend's.

如今，当竞争对手获得新工作时，领英会提醒我们，而Instagram则能让我们知道，与朋友相比，我们的生活获得了多少“点赞”。

In an opinion piece earlier this year, Curran and Hill argue that society has also become more dog-eat-dog.

在今年早些时候的一篇评论文章中，柯伦和希尔指出，社会也变得更加弱肉强食。

"Over the last 50 years, public interest and civic responsibility have been progressively eroded," they write, "replaced by a focus on self-interest and competition in a supposedly free and open marketplace."

“过去50年间，公共利益和公民责任不断遭到侵蚀，”他们写道，“取而代之的是，在一个所谓的自由开放的市场中，对自身利益和竞争的追逐。”

We strive for perfection, it seems, because we feel we must in order to get ahead.

我们之所以追求完美，似乎是因为觉得唯有如此才能出人头地。

Michael Brustein, a clinical psychologist in Manhattan, says when he first began practicing in 2007, he was surprised by how prevalent perfectionism was among his clients, despite how little his graduate training had focused on the phenomenon.

曼哈顿的临床心理学家迈克尔·布鲁斯坦说，2007年他刚开业时，曾惊讶于完美主义在他的来访者中如此普遍，而他的研究生课程却很少涉及这一现象。

He sees perfectionism in, among others, clients who are entrepreneurs, artists, and tech employees.

他发现，来访者中有创业者、艺术家和科技公司员工等，都存在完美主义倾向。

"You're in New York because you're ambitious, you have this need to strive," he says.

“你来纽约是因为你有雄心，你有这种拼搏的需要，”他说。

"But then your whole identity gets wrapped into a goal."

“但之后，你的整个身份认同都被捆绑在一个目标上了。”

F

Perfectionism can, of course, be a positive force.

当然，完美主义也可以成为一种积极的力量。

Think of professional athletes, who train aggressively for ever-higher levels of competition.

想想职业运动员吧，他们为追求更高水平的竞技而积极训练。

In well-adjusted perfectionism, someone who doesn't get the gold is able to forget the setback and move on.

在适应性完美主义中，未能摘金的人能够忘却挫折，继续前行。

In maladaptive perfectionism, meanwhile, people make an archive of all their failures.

而在适应不良的完美主义中，人们会把所有的失败都记录归档。

They revisit these archives constantly, thinking, as Pryor puts it, "I need to make myself feel terrible so I don't do this again."

他们不断重温这些失败记录，正如普赖尔所说，心想：“我必须让自己感到难受，这样才不会再犯同样的错误。”

Then they double down, "raising the expectation bar even higher, which increases the likelihood of defeat, which makes you self-critical, so you raise the bar higher, work even harder," she says.

接着他们会变本加厉，“把期望值抬得更高，这会增加失败的可能性，失败又让你自我苛责，于是你把标准提得更高，工作得更加拼命，”她说道。

Next comes failure, shame, and pushing yourself even harder toward even higher and more impossible goals.

接下来就是失败、羞愧，以及逼迫自己朝着更高、更不可能实现的目标更加拼命地努力。

Meeting them becomes an "all or nothing" premise.

实现这些目标变成了一种“全有或全无”的前提。

Pryor offered this example: "Even if I'm an incredible attorney, if I don't make partner in the same pacing as one of my colleagues, clearly that means I'm a failure."

普赖尔举了这样一个例子：“即使我是一名出色的律师，如果我不能像某位同事那样按同样的节奏晋升为合伙人，那显然就意味着我是个失败者。”

G

Brustein says his perfectionist clients tend to devalue their accomplishments, so that every time a goal is achieved, the high lasts only a short time, like "a gas tank with a hole in it."

布鲁斯坦说，他的完美主义来访者往往贬低自己的成就，因此每当实现一个目标，那种兴奋感只能维持很短的时间，就像“一个有漏洞的油箱”。

If the boss says you did a great job, it's because he doesn't know anything.

如果老板说你干得不错，那是因为他什么都不懂。

If the audience likes your work, that's because it's too stupid to know what good art actually is.

如果观众喜欢你的作品，那是因为他们太愚蠢，根本不懂什么是真正的优秀艺术。

But, therapists say, there are also different ways perfectionism manifests.

但是，治疗师们表示，完美主义的表现方式也各不相同。

Some perfectionists are always pushing themselves forward.

有些完美主义者总是不断鞭策自己前进。

But others actually fall behind on work, unable to complete assignments unless they are, well, perfect.

但另一些人确实在工作上落后，除非任务做到“完美”，否则根本无法完成。

Or they might handicap their performance ahead of time.

或者，他们可能会提前给自己的表现设下障碍。

They're the ones partying until 2 a.

这类人会在期末考试前夜派对到凌晨两点，

m. the night before the final, so that when the grade C rolls in, there's a ready excuse.

这样一来，当成绩得C时，便有了现成的借口。

H

While educators and parents have successfully convinced students of the need to be high performing and diligent, the experts told me, they haven't adequately prepared them for the inevitability of failure.

教育专家告诉我，尽管教育工作者和家长已经成功地让学生们认识到高分表现和勤奋刻苦的必要性，但他们并没有充分地让学生为失败的不可避免做好准备。

Instead of praises like "You're so smart," parents and educators should say things like "You really stuck with it," Pryor says, to emphasize the value of perseverance over intrinsic talent.

Pryor说，家长和教育者不应说“你真聪明”之类夸奖的话，而应该说“你真的很坚持”，以此强调坚持努力比天赋更重要。

Pryor notes that many of her clients are wary she'll "turn them into some degenerate couch potato and teach them to be okay with it."

Pryor指出，她的许多来访者担心她会“把他们变成某种堕落的沙发土豆，并教他们安于现状”。

Instead, she tries to help them think through the parts of their perfectionism they'd like to keep, and to lose the parts that are ruining their lives.

相反，她会帮助来访者梳理自己的完美主义，思考哪些部分想保留，哪些破坏了他们生活的部分应该摒弃。

I

Bach, who sees many students from Brown University, says some of them don't even go out on weekends, let alone weekdays.

Bach接待了大量布朗大学的学生，她说其中一些人周末甚至不出门，更不用说工作日了。

She tells them, "Aim high, but get comfortable with good enough."

她告诉他们：“目标要高，但要学会接受‘足够好’。”

When they don't get some award, she encourages them to remember that "one outcome is not a basis for a broad conclusion about the person's intelligence, qualifications, or potential for the future."

当他们没有获得某项奖励时，她会鼓励他们记住：“一次结果并不能作为对其智力、资历或未来潜力作出全面结论的依据。”

J

The treatment for perfectionism might be as simple as having patients keep logs of things they can be proud of, or having them behave imperfectly in small ways, just to see how it feels.

治疗完美主义的方法可能很简单：让患者记录下自己可以引以为豪的事情，或者让他们故意在小事上表现得不完美，看看感觉如何。

"We might have them hang the towels crooked or wear some clothing inside out," says Martin Antony, a professor in the department of psychology at Ryerson University in Toronto.

"我们可能会让他们把毛巾挂歪，或者把衣服反着穿，"多伦多瑞尔森大学心理学系教授马丁·安东尼说。

K

Brustein likes to get his perfectionist clients to create values that are important to them, then try to shift their focus to living according to those values rather than achieving specific goals.

布鲁斯坦喜欢让他的完美主义来访者确立对自己而言重要的价值观，然后努力将注意力转向按照这些价值观去生活，而不是去达成特定的目标。

It's a play on the "You really stuck with it" message for kids.

这句话是对写给孩子的"你真的坚持下来了"这句鼓励语的巧妙化用。

In other words, it isn't about doing a headstand in yoga class; it's about going to yoga class in the first place, because you like to be the kind of person who takes care of herself.

换句话说，重点不在于在瑜伽课上完成倒立，而在于首先去上瑜伽课，因为你喜欢成为那种懂得照顾自己的人。

But he warns that some people go into therapy expecting too much—an instant transformation of themselves from a pathological perfectionist to a (still high-achieving) non-perfectionist.

但他警告说，有些人接受治疗时期望过高——期望自己立刻从一个病态的完美主义者脱胎换骨，变成一个（仍能取得高成就的）非完美主义者。

They try to be perfect, in other words, at no longer being perfect.

换句话说，他们试图在"不再追求完美"这件事上做到完美。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1449 词 · 22 句

P1

How on earth did we come to this?

我们究竟是怎么走到这一步的？

We protect our children obsessively from every harm; we scrutinize every carer, teacher or doctor with whom they come into contact.

我们不遗余力地保护孩子免受各种伤害；对每一位与他们接触的看护人、教师或医生，我们都仔细审查。

Yet despite all this, one group, which in no way has their best interests at heart, has almost unconstrained access.

然而，尽管如此，有一个群体几乎可以毫无限制地接近我们的孩子，而他们丝毫没有把孩子的最大利益放在心上。

P2

We seem to take it for granted that advertisers and marketers are allowed to condition even the youngest children.

我们似乎理所当然地认为，广告商和营销人员可以对最小的孩子进行诱导和塑造。

Before children have even developed a proper sense of their own identity, or learned to handle money, they're encouraged to associate status and self-worth with stuff, and to look to external things such as fame and wealth for validation.

在孩子们尚未形成正确的自我认同感、也还没学会理财之前，他们就被鼓励将身份地位和自我价值与物质挂钩，并从名利等外在事物中寻求认同。

We're turning out little consumers rather than young citizens who will value themselves for what they contribute to the society in which they live.

我们培养出来的是一个个小消费者，而不是会因自己对所处社会的贡献而珍视自身价值的年轻公民。

P3

We've reached this point so gradually that many of us have never questioned it.

我们是如此渐进地走到了这一步，以至于许多人从未对此提出过质疑。

It's crept up on us in the 60 years since advertisers started to target the young and found that they could recruit them to a commercial assault on their parents.

自从广告商开始以年轻人为目标，并发现可以利用他们向父母发起商业攻势以来，这种现象在60年间悄然蔓延到我们身上。

We've come to know it as "pester power" or the ability of children to pressure parents to make certain purchases.

我们将其称为'纠缠力量'，即孩子向父母施压、迫使其购买某些商品的能力。

P4

Many psychologists, child development experts and educators point to research suggesting that this emerging cradle-to-grave consumerism is contributing to growing rates of low self-esteem, depression and other forms of mental illness.

许多心理学家、儿童发展专家和教育工作者援引研究指出，这种从摇篮到坟墓的消费主义正在助长自卑、抑郁以及其他形式的心理疾病，其发病率不断攀升。

P5

Not all psychologists agree.

并非所有心理学家都持相同看法。

There're plenty working hand in glove with a ￡12bn-a-year industry that has turned the manipulation of adult emotions and desires into an art form—often literally.

不少心理学家与一个年产值120亿英镑的行业联手合作，该行业已将操控成年人的情感与欲望变成了一门艺术——往往还是字面意义上的艺术。

It's also one that's forever developing new ways to persuade our children to desire material possessions, and because of advertisements' viral effect they only need to infect a few to reach the many.

它还是一种不断推陈出新、想方设法诱使我们的孩子渴求物质占有之物的东西，而由于广告的病毒式传播效应，它们只需感染少数几个孩子，就能触及众多孩子。

Advertising and marketing can serve a useful purpose for children.

广告和营销对儿童也能发挥有益的作用。

Marketing may help socialize children as consumers, inform them about products, and help them carve out unique identities as they reach adulthood.

营销有助于儿童成长为消费者，让他们了解产品，并在他们步入成年之际帮助他们塑造独特的身份认同。

P6

Then, should we ban all advertising aimed at young children?

那么，我们是否应该禁止所有针对幼儿的广告？

I say yes.

我认为应该。

P7

Of course there'll be plenty of objections to an outright ban on advertising to the under-11s.

当然，全面禁止面向11岁以下儿童的广告会招来诸多反对。

There'll be those who argue that would be a breach of freedom of speech and infringe the rights of corporations to brainwash little children into demanding their products.

会有人争辩说，这将侵犯言论自由，并损害企业向小孩子洗脑、让他们要求购买其产品的权利。

P8

Most parents hate what advertising does to their children, but we do have the power to end it and let our children grow up free from many of the pressures of consumerism until they're old enough to make their own decisions.

大多数父母都厌恶广告对自己孩子的影响，但我们确实有能力终结这一切，让孩子在成长过程中免受消费主义的诸多压力，直到他们足以自主做出决定的年纪。

And though advertising is only part of an all-pervasive marketing culture we need to make a start somewhere.

尽管广告只是无孔不入的营销文化的一部分，我们仍需从某处着手。

Let's ban all advertising targeting children of primary school age and younger now.

让我们现在就禁止所有针对小学适龄及更年幼儿童的广告。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2450 词 · 22 句

P1

Many oppose workplace surveillance, because of the inherent dehumanizing effect it has and the relentless pressure it brings.

许多人反对职场监控，因为它固有的非人化效应，以及它带来的持续压力。

But it's on the rise around the world as firms look to become more efficient by squeezing more productivity from their workers.

但在全球范围内，随着企业试图通过压榨员工以榨取更多生产力来提高效率，这种做法正日益普遍。

More than half of companies with over $750m in annual revenue used "non-traditional" monitoring techniques on staff last year.

去年，年营收超过7.5亿美元的公司中，有一半以上对员工采用了"非传统"监控技术。

P2

Monitoring employee performance gives firms the ability to assess how their staff are performing and interacting, which can be good for both the firm and employees themselves.

监控员工表现使企业能够评估员工的工作与互动情况，这无论对企业还是员工自身都有益处。

A growing number of analytics companies offer this service.

越来越多的数据分析公司提供这项服务。

They gather "data exhaust" left by employees' email and instant messaging apps, and use name badges equipped with radio-frequency identification devices and microphones.

它们收集员工电子邮件和即时通讯应用留下的"数据尾迹"，并使用配备射频识别装置和麦克风的工牌。

These can check how much time you spend talking, your volume and tone of voice, even if you do not dominate conversations.

这些设备可以统计你的说话时长、音量和语调，即使你并未主导对话。

While this may sound intrusive, exponents argue that it can also protect employees against bullying and sexual harassment.

尽管这听起来可能令人不适，但倡导者认为它也可以保护员工免受欺凌和性骚扰。

P3

Some of this data analysis can produce unexpected results.

其中一些数据分析可能产生意想不到的结果。

For example, it was found that people who sat at 12-person lunch tables tended to interact, share ideas more and outperform those who regularly sat at four-person tables, a fact that would probably have gone undetected without such data analysis.

例如，研究发现，坐在12人餐桌旁的人往往互动更多、分享想法更频繁，表现优于经常坐在4人餐桌旁的人——若没有这样的数据分析，这一事实很可能永远不会被发现。

P4

Over the last few years a Stockholm co-working space called Epicenter has gone much further and holds popular "chipping parties", where people can have microchips implanted in their hands.

过去几年里，斯德哥尔摩一家名为Epicenter的联合办公空间走得更远，定期举办受欢迎的"芯片植入派对"，人们可以在那里将微型芯片植入手中。

They can use the implants to access electronically-controlled doors, or monitor how typing speed correlates with heart rate.

他们可以用植入物开启电子门禁，或者监测打字速度与心率之间的关联。

Implanted chips may seem extreme, but it is a relatively small step from ID cards and biometrics to such devices.

植入式芯片听起来或许有些极端，但从身份证和生物识别技术到这类设备，其实只是一小步。

P5

As long as such schemes are voluntary, there will probably be a growing number of convenience-oriented uses so that a substantial number of workers would opt to have a chip inserted.

只要这类方案是自愿参与的，出于便利考虑的用途就可能越来越多，从而会有相当数量的员工选择植入芯片。

But if implanted chips are used to reduce slack time or rest breaks, that could prove to be detrimental.

但如果植入式芯片被用来压缩怠工时间或休息时间，那就可能产生负面影响。

And if surveillance tools take away autonomy, that's when they prove most unpopular.

而当监控工具剥夺了员工的自主权时，恰恰是它们最不受欢迎的时候。

A lot depends on how such monitoring initiatives are communicated and this could prevent possible revolts being staged.

很大程度上取决于此类监控措施如何与员工沟通，良好的沟通可能防止潜在的反抗发生。

P6

If bosses don't communicate effectively, employees assume the worst.

如果老板沟通不到位，员工往往会往最坏的方向想。

But if they're open about the information they're collecting and what they're doing with it, research suggests 46% of employees are generally okay with it.

但如果他们坦诚说明正在收集哪些信息以及如何使用这些信息，研究显示有46%的员工总体上是可以接受的。

Although many such monitoring schemes use anonymised data and participation is voluntary, many staffers remain sceptical and fear an erosion of their civil liberties.

尽管许多此类监控方案使用的是匿名数据且参与是自愿的，许多员工仍持怀疑态度，担心公民自由受到侵害。

P7

So workplace surveillance could be empowering for staff and useful for companies looking to become more efficient and profitable.

因此，职场监控既可能赋予员工更多主动权，也有助于希望提高效率和盈利的公司。

But implemented in the wrong way, it could also become an unpopular tool of oppression that proves counterproductive.

但如果实施方式不当，它也可能沦为不得人心的压迫工具，最终适得其反。

第 3 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,382 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）279 词 · 14 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) activatedB) articulatesC) assessmentD) consecutiveE) cuesF) denyG) discrepancyH) probablyI) probesJ) randomK) recallL) relativelyM) saturatedN) symptomsO) terminate

You might not know yourself as well as you think.

你对自己的了解，可能并没有你以为的那么深。

According to a new study, people are relatively accurate judges of only some of their behaviors.

一项新的研究发现，人们对自身行为的判断只有部分是相对准确的。

While most previous studies on how well people know themselves have been done on long-term personality traits, this new study probes how well people understand how they are acting from one moment to the next.

以往关于人们自我认知准确程度的研究大多针对长期的人格特质，而这项新研究则考察人们对自己一时一刻的行为表现了解得有多准确。

Researchers asked participants to wear audio recorders that automatically activated every 9.5 minutes between 7 a.

研究人员要求参与者佩戴录音设备，该设备在每天上午7点到

m. and 2 a.

次日凌晨2点之间每隔9.5分钟自动启动一次，

m. to record 30 seconds of audio.

录下30秒的音频。

These participants were then emailed surveys four times a day asking them to recall how outgoing, agreeable, or conscientious they were during a particular hour of the day.

随后，研究人员每天四次向这些参与者发送问卷，请他们回想自己在一天中某一特定时段里表现得有多外向、多随和或多尽责。

The study used data from 248 participants, all of whom answered questions about their behavior for two consecutive weeks and wore the audio device for one of those weeks.

这项研究使用了248名参与者的数据，他们均连续两周回答了有关自身行为的问题，并在其中的一周佩戴了录音设备。

Six laboratory assistants rated each participant's audio clips to see how their observations compared with people's assessment of themselves.

六名实验室助手对每位参与者的音频片段进行评分，以考察他们的观察结果与人们的自我评价吻合到什么程度。

The six assistants were generally in agreement with one another about how the people they were observing acted.

这六名助手对被观察者的行为表现的判断总体上彼此一致。

Further, participants' ratings of their own behaviors agreed with observers' for how outgoing and how conscientious they were being.

此外，参与者对自身外向程度和尽责程度的自我评价，也与观察者的评价相吻合。

But the agreement between participants and outside observers was much smaller for agreeableness.

但在随和程度上，参与者与外部观察者之间的意见一致程度要低得多。

Some of this discrepancy could be because the observers used only audio clips, and thus could not read cues like body language, but there are probably other explanations, as people should be able to hear when a participant is being kind versus being rude.

这种差异部分可能是因为观察者仅凭音频片段，因而无法读取肢体语言等线索，但可能还有其他原因，因为人们理应能听出参与者是在表达善意还是在粗鲁无礼。

The weak agreement between how participants thought they were acting and what observers heard could be because people would rather deny rude behavior.

参与者自认为的表现与观察者所听到的表现之间之所以缺乏共识，可能是因为人们更愿意否认自己的粗鲁行为。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1218 词 · 57 句

A

We're experiencing the rebirth of smallness.

我们正在经历一场对‘小’的重新回归。

Farmers markets, tiny homes, and brew pubs all exemplify our love of smallness.

农贸市场、微型住宅和精酿啤酒馆，都体现了我们对‘小’的喜爱。

So do charter schools, coffee shops, and local bookstores.

特许学校、咖啡馆和社区书店亦是如此。

Small is often (but not always) more affordable, healthier, and sustainable, but its finest characteristic, the one that turns charm into love, is that going small allows us to be more fully who we are.

小巧往往（但并非总是）更实惠、更健康、更可持续，但它最出色的特质——也是将魅力化为热爱的那一特质——在于，选择‘小’让我们能够更充分地做自己。

B

In higher education the trend is mostly in the opposite direction: Universities with 20,000 or 30,000 students are considered "mid-sized".

在高等教育领域，这一趋势却大体相反：拥有两三万名学生的大学已算是‘中等规模’。

The nation's largest university, Arizona State University, has 80,000 students on campus and aims to enroll another 100,000 students online.

全美规模最大的亚利桑那州立大学在校学生达八万人，还计划再招收十万名在线学生。

At the other end of the spectrum is a handful of colleges that have fewer than a hundred students on campus and no online courses: colleges such as Sterling College, Thomas More College of Liberal Arts, and Deep Springs College.

而另一端，则是少数几所校园内学生不足百人且不设在线课程的学院，例如斯特林学院、托马斯莫尔文理学院和深泉学院。

These colleges are so small that they can only be called "tiny."

这些学院小到只能用‘微小’来形容。

C

Tiny colleges focus not just on a young person's intellect, but on the young person as a whole.

微小的学院不仅关注年轻人的才智，更关注年轻人的全面发展。

Equally important, tiny colleges ask, "How can education contribute to human flourishing and the well-being of the world?"

同样重要的是，微小的学院会追问：‘教育如何能促进人的蓬勃成长与世界的福祉？’

And they shape a college experience to address that question.

并且他们据此设计大学生活，以回应这个问题。

They replace concerns about institutional growth with attention to the growth of students as fully developed participants in their communities.

他们不再着眼于机构规模的扩张，转而关注学生成长为社区中全面发展的参与者。

D

I've had the privilege of teaching at three different institutions of higher learning during my career—a small liberal arts college and two mid-sized public universities.

在我的职业生涯中，我有幸在三所不同的高等院校任教——一所小型文理学院和两所中等规模的公立大学。

I've also been profoundly disappointed in each of these institutions, and in many of my colleagues, especially when it comes to helping students and preparing them for the many responsibilities of adulthood.

然而，我对这三所院校以及我的许多同事都深感失望，尤其是在帮助学生、为他们承担成年后的诸多责任做准备这方面。

Administrators focus on the business of running a university, and most faculty focus on their scholarship and teaching their discipline.

管理者专注于大学的运营事务，而大多数教师则专注于学术研究和本专业的教学。

Little deliberate attention is given to how students mature as individuals and social beings.

很少有人刻意关注学生作为个体和社会成员的成长过程。

E

Having just retired from teaching at a public university, I'm now returning to my hometown of Flagstaff, Arizona, to establish a tiny college—Flagstaff College.

我刚从一所公立大学退休，现在正回到家乡亚利桑那州弗拉格斯塔夫，创办一所微型学院——弗拉格斯塔夫学院。

I'm convinced there's a need for another type of education, one devoted to helping students come into their own and into this beautiful and troubled world.

我深信，我们需要另一种教育，一种致力于帮助学生实现自我、融入这个美丽而又充满问题的世界的教育。

Young people need an education that will provide them with meaning, hope, courage, and passion, as well as information and skills.

年轻人需要的教育，不仅要传授知识和技能，更要赋予他们意义、希望、勇气和热情。

Large institutions, I believe, are particularly ill-suited to this type of education.

我认为，大型院校尤其不适合开展这种教育。

F

There's no "best of" list when it comes to tiny colleges, at least not yet.

目前还没有关于微型学院的“最佳”排行榜，至少现在还没有。

But around the country people are creating new colleges that provide an alternative to small liberal arts colleges, large public universities, and online education.

但在全国各地，人们正在创办新的学院，为小型文理学院、大型公立大学和在线教育提供一种替代选择。

G

With only 26 students, Deep Springs is the smallest college in the country and, quite likely, the most atypical.

深泉学院只有26名学生，是全国最小的学院，也很可能是最非同寻常的学院。

Located on a working cattle ranch on the California-Nevada border, Deep Springs is a private, residential, two-year college for men, committed to educating students for "a life of service to humanity."

深泉学院位于加利福尼亚州与内华达州交界处的一个牧场内，是一所私立的寄宿制男子两年制学院，致力于培养学生“为人类服务的人生”。

Founded by the electricity tycoon L.L. Nunn in 1917, Deep Springs "curriculum" revolves around academics, labor, and self-governance.

深泉学院由电力大亨L.L.纳恩于1917年创办，其"课程"围绕学业、劳动和自治三个核心展开。

In addition to their courses, students are charged with running the 155-acre ranch and overseeing the functioning of the college.

除了上课之外，学生们还肩负着经营这155英亩牧场和管理学院运转的责任。

Students chair both the admissions and the curriculum committees.

招生委员会和课程委员会均由学生担任主席。

H

"Living in close community with one's teachers and fellow students, and being forced to take on adult responsibilities, makes for one's growth as a person," says William Hunt, who graduated last year.

去年毕业的威廉·亨特说："与老师和同学共同生活在紧密的社群中，被迫承担成年人的责任，这些都有助于一个人的成长。"

"To exist for very long in a community like that, you have to get over the question of whether you're sufficiently talented or principled and get started worrying about how you can stretch yourself and your peers, how much you can manage to learn with them."

"要在这样一个社群中长久地生存下去，你必须超越'自己是否有足够的才华或信念'这个问题，转而开始思考如何超越自己和同伴，如何与他们一起尽可能多地学到东西。"

I

Sterling College, in Craftsbury Common, Vermont, is also very small—fewer than 100 students.

位于佛蒙特州克拉夫茨伯里康蒙的斯特林学院同样规模极小——学生人数不足100人。

Unlike Deep Springs, Sterling focuses its curriculum on environmental and social justice issues, but like Deep Springs it places a high value on personal responsibility and manual labor.

与深泉学院不同，斯特林学院的课程侧重于环境和社会公正问题，但与深泉学院一样，它高度重视个人责任和体力劳动。

According to its catalog, a college education at Sterling combines "rigorous academics, roll-up-your-sleeves challenges, and good old hard work."

据该校招生手册介绍，斯特林学院的大学教育将"严谨的学术训练、挽起袖子的挑战和踏踏实实的辛勤劳动"融为一体。

J

The average tuition at a small liberal-arts college is $30,000 to $40,000 a year, not including the cost of living on campus, as compared to $8,000 to $10,000 a year for tuition alone at a public university.

一所小型文理学院的平均学费为每年3万至4万美元（不含住宿生活费），相比之下，公立大学仅学费一项每年就需8千至1万美元。

Of the tiny colleges, only Deep Springs doesn't charge tuition or room and board; students pay only for books and the cost of traveling to and from college.

在这些微型学院中，只有深泉学院不收学费和食宿费；学生只需支付书本费和往返学校的交通费用。

If tiny schools are to become a player on the higher education scene, they will need to find a way to be truly affordable.

如果微型学校要在高等教育领域占有一席之地，就必须找到真正负担得起的方式。

K

Doing so may not be that difficult so long as they do not pattern themselves too closely on existing norms.

只要它们不过于拘泥于现有的模式，做到这一点也许并不那么困难。

We've come to believe that a good college should have many academic programs and excellent facilities, posh dorms, an array of athletic programs, and a world-class student activity center.

我们逐渐形成这样一种观念：一所好的大学应当拥有众多的学术项目、一流的设施、豪华的宿舍、各式各样的体育项目，以及一个世界级的学生活动中心。

Imagine a good college without a climbing wall!

想象一下，一所好大学竟然没有攀岩墙！

We also have accepted the idea that college presidents, and their many vice-presidents, should be paid like their counterparts in the business world and that higher education requires an elaborate, up-to-date technology infrastructure.

我们也接受了这样一种看法：大学校长及其众多副校长应当像商界同行那样领取薪酬；而高等教育则需要一套精密、先进的技术基础设施。

All of this drives up the cost of education.

所有这些都推高了教育成本。

L

The "trick" to making tiny colleges affordable, if that's the right word, is simplicity.

让微型大学变得负担得起的“诀窍”——如果这个词用得恰当的话——就在于简约。

At its core, education is a human-to-human interaction.

教育的核心，是人与人之间的互动。

Reflecting on his own college education, President Garfield once commented that an ideal college would consist of nothing more than the legendary teacher Mark Hopkins on one end of a log and a student on the other.

加菲尔德总统回顾自己的大学教育时曾评论道，一所理想的大学不过是：一根圆木的一端坐着传奇教师马克·霍普金斯，另一端坐着一名学生，仅此而已。

The economics of a tiny college, in other words, might be similar to that of a tiny house.

换句话说，微型大学的运作方式可能与微型住宅相似。

Because it is small, a tiny house costs less to build and less to furnish, insure, and maintain.

由于面积小，微型住宅的建造成本较低，添置家具、投保和维护的费用也更低。

But the economic benefits of a small house don't end there.

但小房子带来的经济好处不止于此。

Tiny homes discourage homeowners from buying stuff that they really don't need, because there's no place to put it.

微型住宅让房主不再购买那些他们其实并不需要的东西，因为根本没有地方存放。

M

I'm a late convert to the idea of tiny colleges, and I fully understand the need for many diverse types of educational institutions.

我是很晚才转而认同微型大学这一理念的，而且我完全理解，社会需要多种多样的教育机构类型。

Academic research and job training are important, but tiny colleges aren't suited for either.

学术研究和职业培训固然重要，但规模很小的大学并不适合承担其中任何一项。

The educational needs of a complex society are themselves complex, and no single model can meet all of these needs.

复杂社会的教育需求本身也是复杂的，没有任何单一模式能够满足所有这些需求。

But I'm now convinced there's an educational need that's now going almost completely unmet: namely, the need to help young people transition into adulthood.

但我现在确信，有一种教育需求几乎完全被忽视了，那就是帮助年轻人顺利过渡到成年阶段。

Tiny colleges can do this better than any other type of educational institution.

规模很小的大学在这件事上比任何其他类型的教育机构都做得更好。

N

The ultimate justification for a tiny college is the conviction that each of us comes into our full humanity by close interaction with those who know and care for us, and that one of the basic purposes of higher education is social.

规模很小的大学存在的最根本理由，是这样一种信念：我们每个人只有通过与了解我们、关心我们的人密切互动，才能充分实现自己作为人的价值；而高等教育的基本目的之一是社会性的。

Although we give lip service to the idea that a college education will make us better people, when all's said and done, we think of higher education primarily in economic terms.

尽管我们口口声声说大学教育会让我们成为更好的人，但归根结底，我们主要还是从经济角度来理解高等教育的。

We've come to think of higher education as a means to make a living rather than make a life.

我们已渐渐把高等教育看成谋生的手段，而非成就人生的方式。

We've also come to see higher education as a private good rather than a public one.

我们还渐渐把高等教育看成一种私人商品，而非公共产品。

Tiny colleges are not the answer to all of our educational requirements, but they're an answer to one of our most basic educational necessities; the need to produce thoughtful, engaged, and compassionate human beings.

规模很小的大学并非能满足我们所有教育需求的答案，但它却回答了我们最基本的教育需求之一：培养有思想、有担当、有同情心的人。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1448 词 · 23 句

P1

If you're someone who has turned to snacking on junk food more in the pandemic, you're not alone.

如果你在疫情期间比以往更多地依赖垃圾食品来充当零食，那么有这种习惯的并非只有你一人。

Investigative reporter Michael Moss says processed food is engineered to hook you, not unlike alcohol, cigarettes, or other harmful substances.

调查记者迈克尔·莫斯指出，加工食品经过精心设计以使人上瘾，这一点与酒精、香烟或其他有害物质并无二致。

His 2013 book, Salt Sugar Fat, explored food companies' aggressive marketing of those products and their impact on our health.

他在2013年出版的《盐糖脂肪》一书，探讨了食品公司对这些产品的激进营销及其对我们健康的影响。

In his new book, Hooked, Moss updates the food giants' efforts to keep us eating what they serve, and how they're responding to complaints from consumers and health advocates.

在新书《上瘾》中，莫斯更新了食品巨头为让我们持续食用他们所提供的产品而做出的努力，以及他们如何回应消费者和健康倡导者的批评。

P2

Processed food is inexpensive, it's legal, and it's everywhere.

加工食品价格低廉，合法合规，而且无处不在。

Companies' advertising is cueing us to remember those products and we want those products constantly.

企业的广告在不断提示我们去回忆那些产品，让我们时刻想要购买它们。

So the food environment is one of those key things that makes food even more problematic for so many people.

因此，食品环境是使食品对如此多的人造成更大问题的关键因素之一。

Memory, nostalgia in particular, plays a big role in the foods we crave.

记忆，尤其是怀旧情绪，在我们渴望的食物中扮演着重要角色。

Soda companies discovered that if they put a soda in the hands of a child when they're at a ball game with their parents, that soda will forever be associated with that joyous moment.

汽水公司发现，如果在孩子和父母一起看球赛时把一瓶汽水递到孩子手中，这瓶汽水就会永远与那个欢乐时刻联系在一起。

Later in life, when that child wants to experience a joyous moment, they're going to think of soda.

日后当那个孩子想要重温欢乐时刻时，他们就会想到汽水。

Many people seek comfort in the snacks they remember from childhood.

许多人从童年记忆中的零食中寻求慰藉。

P3

Moss examines the way companies capitalize on our memories, cravings and brain chemistry to keep us snacking.

莫斯剖析了企业如何利用我们的记忆、渴望和大脑化学机制，来让我们不停地吃零食。

P4

One of the reasons I came to think that some of these food products are even more powerful, more troublesome than drugs can be is memory.

我之所以开始认为某些食品甚至比药物更强大、更麻烦，原因之一在于记忆。

What we eat is all about memory.

我们吃什么，与记忆息息相关。

And we begin forming memories for food at a really early age.

而我们对食物形成记忆的时间非常早。

And we keep those memories for a lifetime.

并且我们会将这些记忆保持一生。

Knowing this, the food industry spends lots of time trying to shape the memories that we have for their products.

深知这一点，食品工业花费大量时间，试图塑造消费者对它们产品的记忆。

One of the features of addiction that scientists studying drug addiction discovered back in the 1990s was that the faster a substance hits the brain, the more apt we are as a result to act impulsively.

研究药物成瘾的科学家在20世纪90年代发现，成瘾的特征之一是：一种物质越快作用于大脑，我们就越容易因此而冲动行事。

There's nothing faster than food in its ability to hit the brain.

就作用于大脑的速度而言，没有什么能比食物更快。

For Moss, this puts the notion of "fast food" in an entirely new light as this isn't limited to fast food chains-almost 90% of food products in grocery stores are processed foods.

在莫斯看来，这让"快餐"的概念呈现出全新的面貌，因为这并不局限于快餐连锁店——杂货店里近90%的食品都是加工食品。

Everything in the industry is about speed, from manufacturing to packaging.

这个行业的一切都围绕着速度，从生产到包装皆是如此。

P5

Overall, Moss outlines the industry's dependence on making their products inexpensive, super delicious, and incredibly convenient for consumers.

总体而言，莫斯指出，这个行业的运作依赖于让自己的产品价格低廉、极其美味，并为消费者提供极大的便利。

Now that more and more people care about what they put in their bodies and are wanting to eat healthier, these companies are finding it really difficult to meet that new demand because of their own addiction to making these convenience foods.

如今，越来越多的人关注自己摄入体内的食物，希望吃得更健康，而这些公司因为自身对生产这类方便食品的依赖，发现很难满足这一新需求。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2437 词 · 26 句

P1

Chimpanzees, human beings' closest animal relatives, share up to 98% of our genes.

黑猩猩是与人类亲缘关系最近的动物，与我们共享高达98%的基因。

Yet humans and chimpanzees lead very different lives.

然而，人类和黑猩猩的生活方式却大相径庭。

Fewer than 300,000 wild chimpanzees live in a few forested corners of Africa today, while humans have colonized every corner of the globe.

如今，野生黑猩猩的数量不足30万，仅栖息在非洲少数几处森林地带，而人类却已遍布地球的每一个角落。

At more than 7 billion, human population dwarfs that of nearly all other mammals—despite our physical weaknesses.

人类人口超过70亿，规模远超几乎所有其他哺乳动物——尽管我们身体并不强壮。

P2

What could account for our species' incredible evolutionary successes?

是什么造就了我们物种惊人的进化成就？

P3

One obvious answer is our big brains.

一个显而易见的答案是我们的大脑发达。

It could be that our raw intelligence gave us an unprecedented ability to think outside the box, innovating solutions to thorny problems as people migrated across the globe.

可能是我们的原始智力赋予了我们前所未有的创造性思维能力，使我们在全球迁徙的过程中不断革新，为棘手的难题找到解决方案。

P4

But a growing number of cognitive scientists and anthropologists are rejecting that explanation.

然而，越来越多的认知科学家和人类学家正在否定这一解释。

They think that, rather than making our living as innovators, we survive and thrive precisely because we don't think for ourselves.

他们认为，我们之所以能够生存并繁荣昌盛，恰恰不是因为我们是创新者、靠自己的头脑思考，而是正因为我们不自行思考。

Instead, people cope with challenging climates and ecological contexts by carefully copying others.

相反，人类通过仔细模仿他人来应对严峻的气候和复杂的生态环境。

P5

In a famous study, psychologists Victoria Horner and Andrew Whiten showed two groups of test subjects—children and chimpanzees—a mechanical box with a treat inside.

在一项著名的研究中，心理学家维多利亚·霍纳和安德鲁·惠滕向两组实验对象——儿童和黑猩猩——展示了一个内置奖励品的机械盒子。

In one condition, the box was opaque, while in the other it was transparent.

在一种实验条件下，盒子是不透明的；在另一种条件下，盒子是透明的。

The experimenters demonstrated how to open the box to retrieve the treat, but they also included the irrelevant step of tapping on the box with a stick.

实验人员示范了如何打开盒子取出零食，但他们还在过程中加入了一个无关的步骤——用棍子敲击盒子。

P6

Oddly, the children carefully copied all the steps to open the box, even when they could see that the stick had no practical effect.

奇怪的是，孩子们仔细地模仿了打开盒子的每一个步骤，即使他们明明看到那根棍子并没有任何实际作用。

That is, they copied irrationally: Instead of doing only what was necessary to get their reward, children slavishly imitated every action they'd witnessed.

也就是说，他们不加分辨地照搬了所有动作：为了拿到奖励，他们并非只做必要的步骤，而是机械地模仿了他们所看到的每一个动作。

P7

Of course, that study only included three—and four-year-olds.

当然，那项研究只涉及三岁和四岁的幼儿。

But additional research has shown that older children and adults are even more likely to mindlessly copy others' actions, and infants are less likely to over-imitate—that is, to precisely copy even impractical actions.

但后续研究表明，年龄较大的儿童和成年人反而更容易盲目地模仿他人的行为，而婴幼儿则较少出现过度模仿——即精确模仿那些甚至毫无实用性的动作。

P8

By contrast, chimpanzees in the study only over-imitated in the opaque condition.

相比之下，研究中的黑猩猩只在不透明盒子的条件下出现了过度模仿。

In the transparent condition—where they saw that the stick was mechanically useless—they ignored that step entirely.

在透明盒子的条件下——它们能看到那根棍子在机械结构上毫无用处——便会完全忽略那个步骤。

Other research has since supported these findings.

此后，其他研究也证实了这些发现。

P9

When it comes to copying, chimpanzees are more rational than human children or adults.

在模仿这件事上，黑猩猩比人类儿童和成年人更为理性。

P10

Where does the seemingly irrational human preference for over-imitation come from?

人类这种看似不理性的过度模仿倾向究竟从何而来？

Anthropologist Joseph Henrich points out that people around the world rely on technologies that are often so complex that no one can learn them rationally.

人类学家约瑟夫·亨里奇指出，世界各地的人们依赖的技术往往极其复杂，任何人都无法凭借理性将其学会。

Instead, people must learn them step by step, trusting in the wisdom of more experienced elders and peers.

相反，人们必须一步一步地学习这些技术，信赖更有经验的长辈和同伴的智慧。

P11

So the next time you hear someone arguing passionately that everyone should embrace nonconformity and avoid imitating others, you might laugh a little bit.

因此，下次你听到有人慷慨激昂地鼓吹每个人都应该标新立异、避免随波逐流时，你不妨会心一笑。

We're not chimpanzees, after all.

毕竟，我们又不是黑猩猩。
