Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）305 词 · 16 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) alleviateB) clarityC) cognitiveD) composersE) hurtF) inhibitingG) interrogationH) intrinsicallyI) looseJ) majesticK) mandatoryL) recoverM) significantlyN) soothingO) wards

It's quite remarkable how different genres of music can spark unique feelings, emotions, and memories.

不同类型的音乐能激发独特的感受、情感和记忆，这一点颇为奇妙。

Studies have shown that music can reduce stress and anxiety before surgeries and we are all attracted toward our own unique life soundtrack.

研究表明，音乐可以在手术前缓解压力和焦虑，而我们每个人也都会被自己专属的生命配乐所吸引。

If you're looking to alleviate stress, you might want to give classical music a try.

如果你想要缓解压力，不妨试试古典音乐。

The sounds of classical music produce a calming effect letting loose pleasure-inducing dopamine in the brain that helps control attention, learning and emotional responses.

古典音乐的声音能产生镇静效果，促使大脑释放带来愉悦感的多巴胺，有助于控制注意力、学习和情绪反应。

It can also turn down the body's stress response, resulting in an overall happier mood.

它还能降低身体的压力反应，使人整体心情更加愉悦。

It turns out a pleasant mood can lead to clarity in a person's thinking.

事实证明，愉悦的心情能让人思维更加清晰。

Although there are many great composers of classical music like Bach, Beethoven and Handel, none of these artists' music seems to have the same health effects as Mozart's does.

尽管古典音乐有巴赫、贝多芬和亨德尔等众多杰出的作曲家，但这些音乐家的作品似乎都没有莫扎特的音乐那样显著的健康功效。

According to researchers, listening to Mozart can increase brain wave activity and improve cognitive function.

研究人员指出，听莫扎特的音乐能增强脑电波活动，改善认知功能。

Another study found that the distinctive features of Mozart's music trigger parts of the brain that are responsible for high-level mental functions.

另一项研究发现，莫扎特音乐的独特特征能激活大脑中负责高级心理功能的区域。

Even maternity wards use Mozart to help newborn babies adapt to life outside of the mother's belly.

甚至产房也会播放莫扎特的音乐，帮助新生儿适应母体之外的生活。

It has been found that listening to classical music significantly reduces a person's blood pressure.

研究发现，听古典音乐能显著降低人的血压。

Researchers believe that the calming sounds of classical music may help your heart recover from stress.

研究人员认为，古典音乐的舒缓音韵有助于心脏从压力中恢复。

Classical music can also be a great tool to help people who have trouble sleeping.

古典音乐也可以成为帮助睡眠困难者的一大利器。

One study found that students who had trouble sleeping slept better while they were listening to classical music.

一项研究发现，有睡眠困难的学生在听古典音乐时睡得更好。

Whether classical music is something that you listen to on a regular basis or not, it wouldn't hurt to take time out of your day to listen to music that you find soothing.

无论你平时是否经常听古典音乐，每天抽出一些时间听听让你感到舒缓的音乐，总是有益无害的。

You will be surprised at how good it makes you feel and the potentially positive change in your health.

你会发现它带给你的良好感受以及可能给健康带来的积极变化，都会让你感到惊喜。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1230 词 · 39 句

A

In the city of Athens, Georgia, there exists a rather curious local landmark—a large white oak that is almost universally stated to own itself.

在佐治亚州的雅典市，有一处颇为奇特的当地地标——一棵巨大的白橡树，几乎所有人都说它归自己所有。

Because of this, it is considered one of the most famous trees in the world.

正因如此，它被认为是世界上最著名的树木之一。

So how did this tree come to own itself and the land around it?

那么，这棵树是如何拥有自身及周边土地的呢？

B

Sometime in the 19th century a Georgian called Colonel William Jackson reportedly took a liking to the said tree and endeavored to protect it from any danger.

据传，19世纪某时，一位名叫威廉·杰克逊上校的佐治亚人看中了这棵树，并竭力保护它免受任何危害。

As to why he loved it so, the earliest documented account of this story is an anonymously written front page article in the Athens Weekly Banner published on August 12, 1890.

至于他为何如此钟爱这棵树，关于此事最早的有文字记载见于1890年8月12日《雅典周报》头版上一篇匿名文章。

It states, "Col. Jackson had watched the tree grow from his childhood, and grew to love it almost as he would a human. Its luxuriant leaves and sturdy limbs had often protected him from the heavy rains, and out of its highest branches he had many a time gotten the eggs of the feathered singers. He watched its growth, and when reaching a ripe old age he saw the tree standing in its magnificent proportions, he was pained to think that after his death it would fall into the hands of those who might destroy it."

文中写道："杰克逊上校从小看着这棵树长大，对它的喜爱几乎如同对人一般。它茂盛的枝叶和粗壮的树干常为他遮挡暴雨，他还多次从最高的枝头取下那些鸣禽的鸟蛋。他注视着它的成长，到了垂暮之年，看着这棵树亭亭如盖、气势非凡，一想到自己身后它可能落入那些会毁掉它的人手中，便深感痛心。"

C

Towards this end, Jackson transferred by means of a deed ownership of the tree and a little land around it to the tree itself.

为此，杰克逊通过一纸契约，将这棵树及周围一小片土地的所有权转让给了这棵树本身。

The deed read, "W. H. Jackson for and in consideration of the great affection which he bears the said tree, and his great desire to see it protected has conveyed unto the said oak tree entire possession of itself and of all land within eight feet of it on all sides."

契约写道："W. H. 杰克逊出于对该树的深厚感情，以及迫切希望看到它受到保护，特将该橡树自身的完整所有权及其四周各八英尺范围内所有土地的完整所有权，转让予该橡树。"

D

In time, the tree came to be something of a tourist attraction, known as The Tree That Owns Itself.

随着时间推移，这棵树逐渐成了一处旅游景点，被称为"拥有一切的树"（The Tree That Owns Itself）。

However, in the early 20th century, the tree started showing signs of its slow death, with little that could be done about it.

然而，到了20世纪初，这棵树开始显现出缓慢衰亡的迹象，人们却无能为力。

Father time comes for us all eventually, even our often long lived, tall and leafy fellow custodians of Earth.

时间终将带走一切，哪怕是这些常年茂盛、高大葱郁、与我们共同守护地球的树木。

Finally, on October 9, 1942, the over 30 meter tall and 200-400 year old tree fell, rumor has it, as a result of a severe windstorm and/or via having previously died and its roots rotted.

最终，1942年10月9日，这棵高逾30米、树龄约200至400年的巨树轰然倒下，据传是猛烈的风暴所致，也可能是先前已经死亡、根系腐烂所致。

E

About four years later, members of the Junior Ladies Garden Club (who'd tended to the tree before its unfortunate death) tracked down a small tree grown from a nut taken from the original tree.

大约四年后，少年淑女园艺俱乐部的成员（她们曾在那棵树不幸死去之前照料过它）找到了一棵由原树所结坚果培育而成的小树苗。

And so it was that on October 9, 1946, under the direction of Professor Roy Bowden of the College of Agriculture at the University of Georgia, this little tree was transplanted to the location of its ancestor.

于是，1946年10月9日，在佐治亚大学农学院罗伊·鲍登教授的指导下，这棵小树被移栽到了它祖先所在的位置。

A couple of months later, an official ceremony was held featuring none other than the Mayor of Athens, Robert L McWhorter, to commemorate the occasion.

两个月后，一场隆重的官方典礼举行了，雅典市市长罗伯特·L·麦霍特亲自出席，以纪念这一时刻。

F

This new tree became known as The Son of the Tree That Owns Itself and it was assumed that, as the original tree's heir, it naturally inherited the land it stood on.

这棵新树被称为'自有其树之子'，人们认为，作为原树的继承者，它自然也继承了脚下的土地。

Of course, there are many dozens of other trees known to exist descending from the original, as people taking a nut from it to grow elsewhere was a certainty.

当然，已知还有数十棵其他树木是原树的后代，因为人们从原树上取坚果到别处培育是必然的事。

That said, to date, none of the original tree's other children have petitioned the courts for their share of the land, so it seems all good.

话虽如此，迄今为止，原树的其他'子女'没有一个向法院申请过属于自己的那部分土地，所以看来一切都好。

In any event, The Son of the Tree That Owns Itself still stands today, though often referred to simply as The Tree That Owns Itself.

无论如何，'自有其树之子'至今仍矗立着，尽管人们常常简称它为'自有其树'。

G

This all brings us around to whether Jackson ever actually gave legal ownership of the tree to itself in the first place and whether such a deed is legally binding.

这一切把我们带回了一个问题：杰克逊当初是否真的将树的所有权授予了树本身，以及这样的契据是否具有法律效力。

H

Well, to begin with, it turns out Jackson only spent about three years of his life in Athens, starting at the age of 43 from 1829 to 1832, sort of dismissing the idea that he loved the tree from spending time under it as a child and watching it grow, and then worrying about what would happen to it after he died.

嗯，首先，事实证明杰克逊一生中只在雅典居住了大约三年，即1829年至1832年，从43岁开始——这基本推翻了他从小在树下玩耍、看着它长大，因而深爱这棵树，又担心自己去世后树的命运的说法。

Further, an extensive search of land ownership records in Athens does not seem to indicate Jackson ever owned the land the tree sits on.

此外，对雅典土地所有权记录的广泛查阅似乎并未表明杰克逊曾拥有这棵树所在的土地。

I

He did live on a lot of land directly next to it for those three years, but whether he owned that land or not isn't clear.

那三年间，他确实居住在紧邻该树的一大片土地上，但那片土地是否属于他并不清楚。

Whatever the case, in 1832 a four acre parcel, which included the land the tree was on and the neighboring land Jackson lived on, among others, was sold to University professor Malthus A Ward.

无论情况如何，1832年，一块四英亩的土地（包括树所在的土地以及杰克逊居住的邻近土地等）被卖给了大学教授马尔萨斯·A·沃德。

In the transaction, Ward was required to pay Jackson a sum of $1,200 (about $31, 000 today), either for the property itself or simply in compensation for improvements Jackson had made on the lot.

在这笔交易中，沃德需向杰克逊支付1200美元（约合今天的31000美元），这笔钱要么是用于购买该地产本身，要么就是单纯作为对杰克逊在该地块上所做改良的补偿。

In the end, whether he ever owned the neighboring lot or was simply allowed to use it while he allegedly worked at the University, he definitely never owned the lot the tree grew on, which is the most important bit for the topic at hand.

最终，无论他是否曾拥有相邻地块，或只是在据称于大学工作期间获准使用该地块，他都从未拥有过那棵树所在的地块——而这才是当前话题最关键的一点。

J

After Professor Ward purchased the land, Jackson and his family purchased a 655 acre parcel a few miles away and moved there.

沃德教授购得该土地后，杰克逊及其家人在几英里外购买了一块655英亩的地块并搬到了那里。

Ten years later, in 1844, Jackson seemed to have come into financial difficulties and had his little plantation seized by the Clarke County Sheriff's office and auctioned off to settle the mortgage.

十年后的1844年，杰克逊似乎陷入了财务困境，他的小种植园被克拉克县治安官办公室查封，并被拍卖以清偿抵押贷款。

Thus, had he owned some land in Athens itself, including the land the tree sat on, presumably he would have sold it to raise funds or otherwise had it taken as well.

因此，如果他在雅典市内拥有任何土地，包括那棵树所在的土地，想必他也会将其出售以筹集资金，或者同样被没收。

K

And whatever the case there, Jackson would have known property taxes needed to be paid on the deeded land for the tree to be truly secure in its future.

而无论情况如何，杰克逊本应知道，必须为已登记产权的土地缴纳财产税，那棵树的未来才能真正得到保障。

Yet no account or record indicates any trust or the like was set up to facilitate this.

然而，没有任何记载或记录显示曾设立过信托或类似安排来促成此事。

L

On top of all this, there is no hard evidence such a deed ever existed, despite the fact that deed records in Athens go back many decades before Jackson's death in 1876 and that it was supposed to have existed in 1890 in the archives according to the original anonymous news reporter who claims to have seen it.

除此之外，也没有确凿证据表明这样一份契据曾存在过，尽管雅典的契据记录可追溯至杰克逊1876年去世前数十年，且据那位声称见过它的匿名新闻记者所言，该契据在1890年时本应存于档案中。

M

As you might imagine from all of this, few give credit to this side of the story.

从上述种种情况不难想象，几乎没有人相信故事的这一版本。

So how did all of this come about then?

那么，这一切究竟是怎么来的呢？

N

It is speculated to have been invented by the imagination of the said anonymous author at the Athens Weekly Banner in the aforementioned 1890 front page article titled "Deeded to Itself", which by the way contained several elements that are much more easily proved to be false.

据推测，这可能是《雅典周报旗帜报》那位匿名作者的想象产物，出自前述1890年那篇题为《自行受让》的头版文章——顺带一提，该文包含若干更容易被证伪的元素。

As to why the author would do this, it's speculated perhaps it was a 19th century version of a click-bait thought exercise on whether it would be legal for someone to deed such a non-conscious living thing to itself or not.

至于作者为何要这样做，有人推测这或许是19世纪版的“标题党”式思想实验，探讨将此类无意识的生物以契据形式转让给其自身是否合法。

O

Whatever the case, the next known instance of the Tree That Owns Itself being mentioned wasn't until 1901 in the Centennial Edition of that same paper, the Athens Weekly Banner.

无论如何，下一次有记载提到“拥有自身的树”已是1901年，刊登在同一家报纸《雅典旗帜周报》的百年纪念特刊上。

This featured another account very clearly just copying the original article published about a decade before, only slightly reworded.

这篇报道又讲了一段叙述，很显然是照抄了大约十年前发表的那篇原文，只是措辞稍作改动。

The next account was in 1906, again in the Athens Weekly Banner, again very clearly copying the original account, only slightly reworded, the 19th century equivalent of re-posts when the audience has forgotten about the original.

下一次报道出现在1906年，同样登在《雅典旗帜周报》上，同样明显是抄录原始报道、仅略作改动，堪称19世纪版的“转发”——趁读者已把原作忘得差不多时再炒一遍冷饭。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1446 词 · 25 句

P1

It is irrefutable that employees know the difference between right and wrong.

毋庸置疑，员工都分得清是非对错。

So why don't more employees intervene when they see someone exhibiting at-risk behavior in the workplace?

那么，为什么当员工在工作场所看到有人表现出危险行为时，更多的人不出面干预呢？

P2

There are a number of factors that influence whether people intervene.

有诸多因素会影响人们是否选择出面干预。

First, they need to be able to see a risky situation beginning to unfold.

首先，他们需要能够察觉到风险情境正在形成。

Second, the company's culture needs to make them feel safe to speak up.

其次，企业文化需要让他们敢于发声而无后顾之忧。

And third, they need to have the communication skills to say something effectively.

再次，他们需要具备有效沟通的技巧，才能把话说得有理有据。

P3

This is not strictly a workplace problem; it's a growing problem off the job too.

这严格来说并非仅仅是工作场所的问题；在工作之外，这一问题也日益凸显。

Every day people witness things on the street and choose to stand idly by.

每天都有人在街头目睹种种事件，却选择袖手旁观。

This is known as the bystander effect—the more people who witness an event, the less likely anyone in that group is to help the victim.

这就是所谓的旁观者效应——目睹事件的人越多，其中任何一人伸出援手的可能性就越小。

The psychology behind this is called diffusion of responsibility.

其背后的心理机制被称为责任分散。

Basically, the larger the crowd, the more people assume that someone else will take care of it—meaning no one effectively intervenes or acts in a moment of need.

简而言之，人群越大，就越多人以为别人会去处理——结果在需要有人挺身而出的时刻，没有人真正干预或采取行动。

P4

This crowd mentality is strong enough for people to evade their known responsibilities.

这种从众心理之强，足以让人们逃避自己心知肚明的责任。

But it's not only frontline workers who don't make safety interventions in the workplace.

然而，不进行安全干预的并不只是一线员工。

There are also instances where supervisors do not intervene either.

有时主管也不会出面干预。

P5

When a group of employees sees unsafe behavior not being addressed at a leadership level it creates the precedent that this is how these situations should be addressed, thus defining the safety culture for everyone.

当一群员工看到不安全行为没有在管理层得到处理时，就等于树立了一个先例，让人认为这类情况本该如此处理，从而为所有人定义了安全文化。

P6

Despite the fact that workers are encouraged to intervene when they observe unsafe operations, this happens less than half of the time.

尽管企业鼓励员工在发现不安全操作时进行干预，但实际做到的情况不足一半。

Fear is the ultimate factor in not intervening.

恐惧是不进行干预的首要因素。

There is a fear of penalty, a fear that they'll have to do more work if they intervene.

人们害怕受到处罚，也担心一旦干预就要干更多的活。

Unsuccessful attempts in the past are another strong contributing factor to why people don't intervene—they tend to prefer to defer that action to someone else for all future situations.

过去干预失败的经历也是人们不愿干预的另一大原因——在今后的所有情形中，他们往往倾向于把这项行动推给别人去做。

P7

On many worksites, competent workers must be appointed.

在许多工地，必须指定具备资质的人员。

Part of their job is to intervene when workers perform a task without the proper equipment or if the conditions are unsafe.

其职责之一，就是在员工未使用适当设备或作业条件不安全时进行干预。

Competent workers are also required to stop work from continuing when there's a danger.

当出现危险时，具备资质的人员还必须叫停作业。

P8

Supervisors also play a critical role.

主管同样扮演着关键角色。

Even if a competent person isn't required, supervisors need a broad set of skills to not only identify and alleviate workplace hazards but also build a safety climate within their team that supports intervening and open communication among them.

即便不要求配备具备资质的人员，主管也需要具备广泛的技能，不仅要识别和消除工作场所的隐患，还要在团队中营造一种安全氛围，鼓励大家主动干预、彼此坦诚沟通。

P9

Beyond competent workers and supervisors, it's important to educate everyone within the organization that they are obliged to intervene if they witness a possible unsafe act, whether you're a designated competent person, a supervisor or a frontline worker.

除了合格的员工和主管之外，还必须让组织内的每一个人都明白，一旦目击到可能的不安全行为，他们就有义务予以干预——无论你是被指定的胜任人员、主管，还是一线员工。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2446 词 · 22 句

P1

The term "environmentalist" can mean different things.

"环保主义者"这个术语可以有不同的含义。

It used to refer to people trying to protect wildlife and natural ecosystems.

它过去指的是那些努力保护野生动物和自然生态系统的人。

In the 21st century, the term has evolved to capture the need to combat human-made climate change.

到了21世纪，这个术语的内涵已经演变为涵盖应对人为气候变化的需要。

P2

The distinction between these two strands of environmentalism is the cause of a split within the scientific community about nuclear energy.

环保主义的这两大分支之间的分歧，正是科学界内部在核能问题上产生分裂的原因。

P3

On one side are purists who believe nuclear power isn't worth the risk and the exclusive solution to the climate crisis is renewable energy.

一方是纯粹主义者，他们认为核能不值得冒这个险，解决气候危机的唯一出路是可再生能源。

The opposing side agrees that renewables are crucial, but says society needs an amount of power available to meet consumers' basic demands when the sun isn't shining and the wind isn't blowing.

对立面则认同可再生能源至关重要，但指出社会需要一定量的电力供应，以便在没有阳光和风力的时刻满足消费者的基本用电需求。

Nuclear energy, being far cleaner than oil, gas and coal, is a natural option, especially where hydroelectric capacity is limited.

核能远比石油、天然气和煤炭清洁，是一种自然而然的选择，在水电产能有限的地区尤其如此。

P4

Leon Clarke, who helped author reports for the UN's Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change, isn't an uncritical supporter of nuclear energy, but says it's a valuable option to have if we're serious about reaching carbon neutrality.

利昂·克拉克曾参与撰写联合国政府间气候变化专门委员会的报告，他并非核能的无条件支持者，但认为如果我们认真对待实现碳中和的目标，核能是一个值得保留的选项。

P5

"Core to all of this is the degree to which you think we can actually meet climate goals with 100% renewables," he said.

"这一切的核心在于，你认为我们究竟能否完全依靠可再生能源实现气候目标，"他说。

"If you don't believe we can do it, and you care about the climate, you are forced to think about something like nuclear."

"如果你不相信我们能做到这一点，而你又关心气候问题，那么你就不得不考虑核能之类的选项。"

P6

The achievability of universal 100% renewability is similarly contentious.

全面实现百分之百可再生能源是否可行，同样存在争议。

Cities such as Burlington, Vermont, have been "100% renewable" for years.

像佛蒙特州伯灵顿这样的城市，已经实现"百分之百可再生能源"供电多年。

But these cities often have small populations, occasionally still rely on fossil fuel energy and have significant renewable resources at their immediate disposal.

但这些城市往往人口稀少，有时仍依赖化石燃料能源，而可再生资源就取之于其近旁。

Meanwhile, countries that manage to run off renewables typically do so thanks to extraordinary hydroelectric capabilities.

与此同时，那些完全依靠可再生能源运行的国家，通常要归功于其非凡的水力发电能力。

P7

Germany stands as the best case study for a large, industrialized country pushing into green energy.

德国是大型工业化国家推进绿色能源的最佳研究案例。

Chancellor Angela Merkel in 2011 announced Energiewende, an energy transition that would phase out nuclear and coal while phasing in renewables.

2011年，总理安格拉·默克尔宣布了"能源转型"（Energiewende）计划，即逐步淘汰核电和煤电、同时大规模引入可再生能源的能源转型。

Wind and solar power generation has increased over 400% since 2010, and renewables provided 46% of the country's electricity in 2019.

自2010年以来，风力和太阳能发电量增长了400%以上，2019年可再生能源提供了德国46%的电力。

P8

But progress has halted in recent years.

但近年来进展陷入停滞。

The instability of renewables doesn't just mean energy is often not produced at night, but also that solar and wind can overwhelm the grid during the day, forcing utilities to pay customers to use their electricity.

可再生能源的不稳定性不仅意味着夜间往往无法发电，还意味着白天太阳能和风能可能过量涌入电网，迫使电力公司付费让客户用电。

Lagging grid infrastructure struggles to transport this overabundance of green energy from Germany's north to its industrial south, meaning many factories still run on coal and gas.

落后的电网基础设施难以将这些过剩的绿色能源从德国北部输送到南部的工业区，这意味着许多工厂仍在使用煤炭和天然气。

The political limit has also been reached in some places, with citizens meeting the construction of new wind turbines with loud protests.

在一些地方，政治承受力也已达到极限，民众对新建风力发电机组的抗议声浪此起彼伏。

P9

The result is that Germany's greenhouse gas emissions have fallen by around 11.5% since 2010—slower than the EU average of 13.5%.

结果是，德国的温室气体排放自2010年以来下降了约11.5%——低于欧盟13.5%的平均水平。

第 2 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,442 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）304 词 · 20 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) adoreB) appraiseC) assaultsD) chemistryE) coincidenceF) cracksG) deportedH) dismissesI) illegallyJ) lostK) perpetuallyL) prescribedM) shrewdN) skepticalO) strives

The Sun Is Also a Star is a truly lovely story of love, romance, fate, and destiny.

《The Sun Is Also a Star》是一个关于爱情、浪漫、缘分与命运的真正动人的故事。

Natasha is a Jamaican-born immigrant living illegally in America, not by choice exactly.

娜塔莎是出生在牙买加的移民，如今非法居留在美国——这并非完全出于她的本意。

Her parents brought her over and created the situation she strives to be out of.

是她的父母把她带到了美国，一手造成了她如今拼命想要摆脱的处境。

Daniel is an American born of Korean immigrants.

丹尼尔是韩裔移民在美国生下的孩子。

He believes in true love, fate, and all that other nonsense that Natasha dismisses through scientific reasoning.

他相信真爱、命运，以及所有那些娜塔莎用科学推理嗤之以鼻的无稽之谈。

Daniel and Natasha meet by coincidence on the streets of New York on the day that she is to be deported.

就在娜塔莎即将被遣返的那一天，两人在纽约街头偶遇。

She doesn't tell him that but does allow him to keep her company while he tries to get her to fall in love with him over the course of the day.

她没有把这件事告诉他，却任由他陪在自己身边；整整一天里，他都想方设法让她爱上自己。

Natasha is me.

娜塔莎就是我。

I found her so similar to myself.

我发现她和我是如此相像。

She's scientifically-minded, practical, somewhat cynical, and always skeptical.

她崇尚科学思维，务实，有些愤世嫉俗，而且总是满腹狐疑。

Her obsession with the universe through a scientific lens is infectious and I adore Daniel seeing that too.

她透过科学视角对宇宙的痴迷具有感染力，我也很欣赏丹尼尔同样能看到这一点。

Daniel is charming and passionate and has a way with words that even cracks Natasha's tough outer shell.

丹尼尔迷人而充满激情，能说会道，甚至敲开了娜塔莎坚硬的外壳。

By the end of the book I fell in love with both of them.

读到最后，我爱上了他们两个人。

I used to find romance stories to always be cheap or laughable.

我过去总觉得爱情故事不是廉价就是可笑。

I think now I can see the value in escaping into a story of pure optimism.

现在我想，我明白了沉浸在一个纯粹乐观的故事里的价值。

I got lost in The Sun Is Also a Star and finished it cover to cover in a weekend.

我沉醉于《太阳也是星星》，一个周末就从头到尾把它读完了。

I couldn't wait to get to what I hoped would be a happy ending.

我迫不及待地想读到那个我所期盼的圆满结局。

It's nice every once in a while to give in to magic.

偶尔向魔力缴械投降，感觉也挺好的。

It doesn't have to be a hard fantasy novel with actual spells, it can be the magic found between two people who just have that special something.

这种魔力不必是硬奇幻小说里货真价实的咒语，它也可以存在于两个人之间，那种彼此间独有的特别感觉所带来的魔力。

That chemistry that causes them to react and spark when they're near each other.

就是那种让他们一靠近就彼此反应、迸出火花的化学感应。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1244 词 · 69 句

A

According to recent research, behavioral change involves physical changes in the brain.

根据近期研究，行为的改变伴随着大脑的生理变化。

In the past decade, researchers have shown that when it comes to the duration of making a new behavior a deep-rooted habit there is not a simple answer.

过去十年间，研究人员发现，关于把新行为变成根深蒂固的习惯需要多长时间，并没有一个简单的答案。

Even for the most productive and disciplined among us, undoing something that has become an automatic part of who we are takes more than an overnight effort.

即使对于我们当中最高效、最有自律性的人来说，要摒弃已经成为我们自身自动组成部分的行为，也绝非一夜之间就能做到。

Once we've successfully made that change, we then have to make other adjustments to our lives to ensure that we continue to maintain it, which is often a whole other challenge in itself.

一旦成功实现了这一改变，我们还得对生活作出其他调整，以确保能够持续保持下去，而这本身往往又是另一大挑战。

B

At its core, success in changing and maintaining a behavior rarely occurs without the introduction of some sort of system.

从本质上讲，成功改变并维持一种行为，几乎离不开某种体系的引入。

When there isn't the right framework in place, we face a greater likelihood of derailing our hard-earned progress.

当缺少恰当的框架时，我们便更有可能让来之不易的进展半途而废。

To ensure success in changing and maintaining a behavior, we should stay away from some detrimental habits.

为了确保成功改变并保持一种行为，我们应当远离某些有害的习惯。

C

The first one to avoid is relying on willpower.

首先应当避免的就是依赖意志力。

Think about the last time you vowed to resist a temptation.

想想你上次发誓抵制某种诱惑的情景。

Perhaps you didn't want to check your phone every 15 minutes, or you were determined not to reach for a chocolate bar at 3 p.

也许你是不想每15分钟就看一次手机，或者你决心下午3点不去拿巧克力棒。

m.

（接上句 p.m.）

Think about how difficult it must have been not to glance at your phone when it was within reach, or not to walk to the vending machine when your afternoon slump hit.

想一想，当手机就在手边时忍住不瞄一眼有多难，或者下午困倦袭来时忍住不去自动售货机买零食有多难。

D

The research on whether we have finite or infinite willpower is inconclusive, but experts do generally agree that you can't change and sustain a habit if you rely on your willpower alone.

关于人的意志力是有限还是无限的研究尚无定论，但专家们普遍认为，如果你仅仅依靠意志力，就无法改变并坚持一个习惯。

The old military saying "You never rise to the occasion, you only sink to the level of training" also applies to behavior change.

军事上有一句老话：'你永远无法临场超常发挥，你只会达到训练水平'，这句话同样适用于行为改变。

The idea is simple—you repeat something so many times that it becomes automatic.

其原理很简单——你反复做某件事，直到它成为一种自动行为。

E

Think about what else you can change about your surrounding that makes it easier for you to perform this change on a daily basis.

想想在你的周围环境里，还有什么可以改变，能让你每天更容易地践行这一改变。

This is called your "cue."

这被称为你的'提示'。

Basically, it's a trigger to perform that particular habit.

基本上，它就是一个触发你执行该特定习惯的信号。

If you don't want to reach for a sugary treat at 3 p.

如果你不想在下午3点伸手去拿甜食，

m.

下午3点

, have a box of herbal tea ready at your desk.

，就在办公桌上准备好一盒花草茶。

When 3 p.

当下午3点

m.

到点时，

comes around, that's your cue to pour yourself a cup of hot water and drink that tea, instead of walking to the vending machine.

那就是提示你为自己倒一杯热水、喝那杯茶的信号，而不是走到自动售货机前去买零食。

F

The second one to avoid is focusing on negative goals.

第二个需要避免的是专注于负面目标。

Sometimes, it's not your process that lets you down, but the habit that you want to change in the first place.

有时候，让你失望的不是你所遵循的过程，而恰恰是你最初想要改变的那个习惯。

For starters, not eating chocolate to beat your afternoon slump is a harder goal than swapping chocolate for herbal tea when you reach the designated time.

举例来说，为了克服午后倦怠而不吃巧克力，比到了指定时间用花草茶代替巧克力这个目标要难得多。

Your brain wants to find routines that have succeeded in the past and allow you to repeat those actions again in the future without having to think about them explicitly.

你的大脑倾向于寻找过去行之有效的固定套路，并让你在未来无需刻意思考就能重复这些行为。

However, this habit-learning system isn't so effective when it comes to learning not to do something.

然而，这套习惯学习机制在学会"不做"某件事方面效果并不好。

That's why rather than giving up something, think about introducing something in its place.

因此，与其放弃某样东西，不如想想用什么来替代它。

Focus on actions you are going to take that will ultimately conflict with the behaviors you want to stop.

要专注于你将要采取的、最终会与你想要戒除的行为相冲突的行动上。

When your attention is on doing something new, you give your habit system a chance to operate.

当你把注意力放在做一件新事情上时，你就给了自己的习惯系统一个发挥作用的机会。

G

The third one to avoid is using the same strategies in different circumstances.

第三个需要避免的是在不同情境下使用相同的策略。

Because we are creatures of habit, it's natural to assume that when we do manage to adopt and sustain a desirable behavior, that same strategy will work when we want to make another behavior change.

因为我们是习惯的动物，所以很自然地会认为，既然我们成功养成并坚持了一种可取的行为，那么在想要改变另一种行为时，同样的策略也会奏效。

But that's not always the case.

但情况并非总是如此。

Sometimes, the system that got you to change one behavior might not work for another.

有时候，帮你改变了一种行为的那套方法，未必适用于另一种行为。

H

Sometimes we become accustomed to relying on our guts when it comes to decision-making.

有时，我们在做决定时会习惯于依赖直觉。

This serves us well in certain situations, but can hinder us in others especially when we need to consider metrics and data, rather than letting our instinct override everything.

这在某些情况下对我们颇有裨益，但在另一些情况下却可能成为阻碍，尤其是当我们需要考量指标和数据，而不是让直觉凌驾于一切之上时。

For example, if you want to stop checking your email first thing in the morning, you might decide to substitute another activity in its place.

例如，如果你想改掉早上一睁眼就查看电子邮件的习惯，你可能会决定用另一项活动来替代它。

But if you want to stop indulging in video games, simply deciding you will go for a run might not be as effective.

但如果你想戒掉沉迷电子游戏的习惯，仅仅决定去跑步可能就没有那么有效。

You might need to introduce another reinforcement, such as meeting a friend and booking an exercise class together.

你可能需要引入另一种强化机制，比如约上朋友一起报一个健身课。

I

The fourth one to avoid is not forgiving ourselves for slipping up.

要避免的第四点，就是因一时失误便不肯原谅自己。

Of course, even the best-laid plans fail sometimes.

当然，即使是计划得再周全的方案，有时也会失败。

You might have stuck to your screen-free nighttime routine for five days, and then a big project landed on your desk and you found yourself in bed with your laptop before you went to sleep.

你可能已经连续五天坚持睡前远离屏幕的作息，结果一个大项目突然落到你桌上，你发现自己又抱着笔记本电脑上了床。

Or you prepared meals on Sunday and stuck to eating healthy dinners at home, but by Friday you found yourself so exhausted and opted to order greasy takeout.

又或者你在周日就备好了餐食，坚持在家吃健康的晚餐，可到了周五你精疲力竭，最终选择了油腻的外卖。

Life happens and even if your behavior change is small, every single day can prove pretty inflexible, and at some point your luck may run out, even if just for a day.

生活中总有意外发生，即使你的行为改变幅度很小，每一天都可能充满变数，总有一天运气会用尽，哪怕只是一天也不例外。

The perfectionist in you might be screaming to abandon your goals altogether, but try to see it in the bigger picture.

你内心那个完美主义者可能正咆哮着让你干脆放弃目标，但请试着从大局来看待这件事。

Just because you might have temporarily strayed off course doesn't mean you can't start afresh the next day.

即便你一时偏离了轨道，也不意味着你不能在第二天重新开始。

J

The final one to avoid is discounting small progress.

最后一个要避免的误区是轻视微小的进步。

There's a habit that many perfectionists tend to fall into when they try to establish a behavior change.

许多完美主义者在尝试改变行为时，往往会陷入一种习惯。

They focus too much on the big goal and don't take the time to celebrate the small progress they make in the process.

他们过于关注宏大的目标，却不肯花时间去庆祝自己在此过程中取得的点滴进步。

Your brain responds to rewards.

大脑会对奖励作出反应。

The basal ganglia, the brain region linked to our performance of habits, is most active at the beginning of a behavior, when the habit is cued, and at the end, when it's rewarded.

基底神经节是大脑中与习惯行为执行相关的区域，它在行为开始（习惯被触发）和行为结束（获得奖励）时最为活跃。

Say your goal is to run five miles three times a week, and this week you ran one mile on Monday, Wednesday, and Friday.

假设你的目标是每周跑步三次、每次跑五英里，而这周你在周一、周三和周五各跑了一英里。

Rather than focusing on how far you've gone toward your goal, think about how you can reward yourself for the progress you've made.

与其盯着自己离目标还有多远，不如想想如何为自己已经取得的进步犒赏自己。

It doesn't have to be big or expensive; it can be something as simple as making your favorite fruit juice after your run.

奖励不必隆重或昂贵，可以简单到跑完步后榨一杯你最爱的果汁。

Whatever your reward, it has to be more than just the activity itself to get you going.

无论奖励是什么，它必须超越活动本身，才能真正激励你行动起来。

K

Initiating a new behavior usually seems like the hardest part of the process of change.

启动一种新的行为，通常是整个改变过程中最难的部分。

However, people often fail to adequately prepare for maintaining it.

然而，人们往往没有为如何坚持下去做充分准备。

One of the reasons for this is because we mistakenly believe the strategies we used to initiate the change will be equally effective in helping us continue the change.

原因之一在于，我们错误地以为，当初用来启动改变的策略，在帮助我们维持改变时同样有效。

But they won't.

但他们不会。

Where changing a strongly deep-rooted habit requires changing our belief about that habit that penetrates deeply into our lives, continually manifesting that wisdom requires that we maintain a positive outlook.

改变一个根深蒂固的习惯，需要改变我们对该习惯的信念——这种信念深深渗透进我们的生活；而持续展现这种智慧，则要求我们保持积极的心态。

If our mood is low, the wisdom to behave differently seems to disappear and we go back to eating more and exercising less.

如果我们情绪低落，做出不同行为的智慧似乎就会消失，我们便会重新回到吃得更多、运动更少的状态。

The key, then, to maintaining new behaviors is to be happy!

因此，维持新行为的关键就是保持快乐！

Which is why it's so hard to maintain new behaviors.

这正是新行为如此难以坚持的原因。

L

Remember, overcoming the behavioral inertia that prevents us from implementing new changes, like eating a healthy diet or exercising, can benefit us in the long run and can improve our physical and mental health.

请记住，克服阻碍我们落实新改变的行为惯性——比如健康饮食或坚持锻炼——从长远来看对我们有益，能够改善我们的身心健康。

No one was born with habits.

没有人是生来就有习惯的。

They were all learned, and can all, therefore, be unlearned.

所有习惯都是后天习得的，因此也都可以被戒除。

The question is: how badly do you really want to change?

问题在于：你究竟有多想改变？

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1446 词 · 21 句

P1

The "American Dream" promises that in the Land of Opportunity, any individual can climb the economic ladder and prosper through hard work and ambition alone.

“美国梦”许诺，在这片机遇之地，任何人只需凭借努力与雄心，便可攀登经济阶梯、走向成功。

And yet, young Americans today are struggling to earn more than their parents did at the same age, and upward mobility in the US actually compares unfavourably to that of other industrialised nations.

然而，如今的美国年轻人却难以挣得比父母同龄时更高的收入，而美国的社会向上流动性实际上比其他工业国家更为不利。

P2

So why does the idea of the American Dream persist?

那么，美国梦这一理念为何仍能经久不衰？

A new study in the American Journal of Political Science identifies one factor that has been overlooked: the influence of reality TV.

《美国政治科学杂志》的一项新研究指出了一个一直被忽视的因素：真人秀节目的影响。

P3

Reality shows have come to dominate US television over the past 20 years, notes Eunji Kim from Vanderbilt University.

范德堡大学的Eunji Kim指出，过去20年间，真人秀节目已主导了美国电视荧屏。

And the overwhelming majority of these have a "rags-to-riches" storyline: they feature ordinary Americans who work hard to achieve great economic success.

而这些节目中绝大多数都贯穿着“白手起家”的情节：它们讲述普通美国人通过辛勤劳动获得巨大经济成功的经历。

And while these programmes are regularly among the most-watched shows, news broadcasts—which paint a more realistic view of the economic hardship faced by millions of Americans—get a much smaller proportion of the viewership.

尽管这类节目常常位居收视榜前列，但新闻报道——后者如实描绘了数以百万计美国人所面临的经济困境——所占的收视份额却小得多。

P4

Rags-to-riches stories are ubiquitous on TV—but does watching these programmes actually convince people that economic mobility is easily attainable?

白手起家的故事在电视上无处不在——但观看这些节目真的会让人相信经济上的向上流动唾手可得吗？

To find out, Kim's team had participants watch a 5-minute clip from a reality show with a rags-to-riches storyline.

为查明这一点，Kim的团队让参与者观看了一段5分钟的真人秀片段，该片段讲述了一个白手起家的故事。

Control participants watched a clip from a reality show that didn't have a rags-to-riches story.

对照组的参与者则观看了一段没有白手起家情节的真人秀片段。

After watching the shows, participants rated how much they agreed with four statements relating to the American Dream.

看完节目后，参与者就四条与美国梦相关的陈述进行评分，表明自己的认同程度。

P5

The results showed that those who'd watched a rags-to-riches clip did indeed have a significantly greater belief in the American Dream.

结果显示，观看过白手起家片段的人确实对美国梦有着显著更强的信念。

Interestingly, when participants were separated by party affiliation, this effect was significant among Republicans but not Democrats, suggesting that the kind of messages implicit in these TV shows may play into people's existing socioeconomic beliefs.

有意思的是，当按党派归属对参与者进行划分后，这一效应在共和党人中显著，而在民主党人中则不显著，这表明这类电视节目所隐含的信息可能会迎合人们既有的社会经济观念。

P6

Kim also conducted a survey of 3,000 US residents.

金还对3000名美国居民进行了一项调查。

They also rated the extent to which they believed success in life is related to various internal factors (such as ambition) and external factors (such as family wealth).

他们还评估了自己在多大程度上认为人生的成功与各种内在因素（如抱负）和外在因素（如家庭财富）有关。

Finally, they read a list of TV programmes and indicated which they regularly watched.

最后，他们阅读了一份电视节目清单，并指出自己经常观看哪些节目。

P7

Participants who were heavy viewers of rags-to-riches programmes or frequent viewers had a stronger belief in the American Dream than those who never watched such shows.

那些经常观看或大量观看白手起家类节目的参与者，比从不观看此类节目的人更坚信“美国梦”。

P8

Kim concludes that "rags-to-riches entertainment media are an important cultural force that promotes and perpetuates beliefs in upward mobility".

金总结道，“白手起家题材的娱乐媒体是一股重要的文化力量，它宣扬并延续着对向上流动的信念。”

And here's the problem: if people mistakenly believe that hard work is all that is needed for individuals to make a better life for themselves, they may be less supportive of policies that could actually combat inequality.

问题在于：如果人们错误地认为个人只要努力工作就能过上更好的生活，他们可能就不太支持那些真正能够应对不平等的政策。

P9

"In this era of choice, entertainment media is what captures hearts and minds," Kim writes.

“在这个选择无处不在的时代，娱乐媒体才是真正俘获人心的东西，”金写道。

"Its political consequences are anything but trivial".

“其政治影响绝非微不足道。”

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2448 词 · 25 句

P1

When someone asks us 'what do you do?

当有人问我们“你是做什么的？

' we nearly always reply with our occupation.

我们几乎总是以自己的职业来作答。

Work, for many of us, is much more than a job.

对我们许多人来说，工作远不止一份职业那么简单。

It is the defining aspect of our identity.

它是定义我们身份的关键所在。

For many of us it is through our job that we can define ourselves.

对很多人而言，我们正是通过工作来界定自我。

P2

"Without my job I don't know who I am," is a sentence that has been uttered on more than a handful of occasions from my office chair.

“没有了工作，我就不知道自己是谁了。”——这样的话，我坐在办公室的椅子上，已经不知听过多少次。

Indeed, it can be one of the most challenging aspects I work on with clients who have lost or been forced into changing their jobs.

事实上，对于那些失去工作或被迫转行的来访者，这可能是我与他们共同面对的最具挑战性的问题之一。

This loss provokes an identity crisis much greater than the loss of the job itself.

这种丧失引发的身份危机，远比失去工作本身要严重得多。

P3

One of the things I have come to understand, however, is that our identity is much more complex than we recognise at first glance.

然而，我逐渐明白的一点是：我们的身份远比乍看之下要复杂得多。

If we take the time to reflect we might recognise that as well as our work we can also identify as a friend, a spouse, a son or daughter, a parent, a member of a sports team or religious community.

如果肯花时间反思，我们或许会意识到，除了工作，我们还可以是朋友、配偶、子女、父母、运动队成员或宗教团体的一员。

We may recognise that we feel and act differently in these roles and relationships than we do at work.

我们或许会察觉到，在这些角色和关系中，自己的感受和行为方式与工作时截然不同。

The passive daughter becomes an assertive leader at work.

那个在家中温顺的女儿，在职场上却是一位果敢的领导者。

Furthermore, our identities at work are not static.

此外，我们在职场中的身份并非一成不变。

They change over time.

它们会随时间而改变。

I myself have been a shop assistant, a waitress, a student, a graduate, and a clinical psychologist.

我本人曾做过店员、服务员、学生、毕业生和临床心理学家。

At each stage my ability to adapt to and develop my career identity has been crucial to my wellbeing.

在每一个阶段，我适应并发展职业身份的能力对我的幸福感都至关重要。

Whilst we like to eliminate uncertainty in our lives at some level we have to manage uncertainty, especially in today's volatile and ever-shifting job market.

虽然我们在某种程度上希望消除生活中的不确定性，但有时不得不应对不确定性，尤其是在当今动荡且瞬息万变的就业市场中。

P4

How we see ourselves is central to the issue of our identity.

我们如何看待自己，是身份认同问题的核心。

When we tell ourselves "I'm good at starting projects but not so great at seeing them through" it can become part of our belief system.

当我们告诉自己"我擅长启动项目，但不太擅长把它们做完"时，这种说法可能成为我们信念体系的一部分。

But if you have the unfortunate experience of an enforced job change you will need to examine those beliefs to see how grounded in reality they are.

但如果你不幸遭遇被迫的职业转变，你就需要审视这些信念，看看它们在多大程度上符合现实。

You will be required to ask yourself how helpful these beliefs are and consider personal change.

你需要扪心自问这些信念究竟有多大帮助，并考虑做出个人改变。

We can change our beliefs, behaviours and emotional experience at any time through experimentation, practice and conscious self-discipline.

我们随时都可以通过尝试、练习和有意识的自我约束来改变自己的信念、行为和情绪体验。

In an age where career progression may lead us into new sectors it is ever more important to challenge our sense of self and explore whether you can create a new experience of your identity by changing the beliefs you hold about yourself in order to expand your career options.

在职业发展可能引领我们进入新行业的时代，审视自我认知变得尤为重要，探索你是否能通过改变对自己的固有信念来创造新的身份体验，从而拓宽职业选择。

Ultimately it is you who define who you are.

归根结底，是你自己定义了你是谁。

You are only your job if you let it be so.

只有当你任由工作定义自己时，你才会仅仅等同于你的工作。

第 3 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,384 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）297 词 · 17 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) bouncesB) completelyC) dispersionD) eccentricE) hangingF) opticalG) originatesH) perceiveI) permeatesJ) ponderK) precedingL) recklesslyM) relationN) spectrumO) stands

A rainbow is a multi-colored, arc-shaped phenomenon that can appear in the sky.

彩虹是一种可以出现在天空中的多色弧形现象。

The colors of a rainbow are produced by the reflection and dispersion of light through water droplets present in the atmosphere.

彩虹的颜色是由光在大气中的水滴上发生反射和色散而产生的。

An observer may perceive a rainbow to be located either near or far away, but this phenomenon is not actually located at any specific spot.

观察者可能会觉得彩虹出现在近处或远处，但实际上这一现象并不位于任何特定的地点。

Instead, the appearance of a rainbow depends entirely upon the position of the observer in relation to the direction of light.

相反，彩虹的出现完全取决于观察者相对于光线方向所处的位置。

In essence, a rainbow is an optical illusion.

从本质上说，彩虹是一种光学错觉。

Rainbows present a spectrum made up of seven colors in a specific order.

彩虹呈现出由七种颜色按特定顺序组成的光谱。

In fact, school children in many English-speaking countries are taught to remember the name "Roy G. Biv" as an aid for remembering the colors of a rainbow and their order.

事实上，许多英语国家的学童都被教导记住“Roy G. Biv”这个名字，以此帮助记忆彩虹的颜色及其排列顺序。

"Roy G. Biv" stands for: red, orange, yellow, green, blue, indigo, and violet.

“Roy G. Biv”代表：红（red）、橙（orange）、黄（yellow）、绿（green）、蓝（blue）、靛（indigo）和紫（violet）。

The outer edge of the rainbow arc is red, while the inner edge is violet.

彩虹弧的外缘是红色，而内缘是紫色。

A rainbow is formed when light (generally sunlight) passes through water droplets hanging in the atmosphere.

当光（通常是阳光）穿过悬浮在大气中的水滴时，彩虹便形成了。

The light waves change direction as they pass through the water droplets, resulting in two processes: reflection and refraction.

光波在穿过水滴时改变方向，从而产生两个过程：反射和折射。

When light reflects off a water droplet, it simply bounces back in the opposite direction from where it originates.

当光从水滴上反射时，它只是朝着与入射方向相反的方向弹回。

When light refracts, it takes a different direction.

光发生折射时，其传播方向会发生改变。

Some individuals refer to refracted light as "bent light waves."

有些人把折射光称为“弯曲的光波”。

A rainbow is formed because white light enters the water droplet, where it bends in several different directions.

彩虹之所以形成，是因为白光进入水滴后，在其中向几个不同方向发生偏折。

When these bent light waves reach the other side of the water droplet, they reflect back out of the droplet instead of completely passing through the water.

当这些弯曲的光波到达水滴的另一侧时，它们会反射回来并射出水滴，而不是完全穿过水滴。

Since the white light is separated inside of the water, the refracted light appears as separate colors to the human eye.

由于白光在水滴内部被分离开来，折射后的光在人眼中就呈现出各种不同的颜色。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1202 词 · 51 句

A

Phyllis Hartman knows what it's like to make one's way through the depths of office meeting hell.

菲利斯·哈特曼深知在一场又一场令人窒息的会议室炼狱中摸爬滚打是何种滋味。

Managers at one of her former human resources jobs arranged so many meetings that attendees would fall asleep at the table or intentionally arrive late.

她曾在一家人力资源机构任职，那里的管理者安排了太多会议，以至于与会者在会议桌前打瞌睡，或故意迟到。

With hours of her day blocked up with unnecessary meetings, she was often forced to make up her work during overtime.

一天中大量时间被无关紧要的会议占据，她常常被迫在加班时补做本职工作。

"I was actually working more hours than I probably would have needed to get the work done," says Hartman, who is founder and president of PGHR Consulting in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania.

“我实际工作的时长，比完成这些工作原本所需的可能还要多。”哈特曼说道。她是匹兹堡PGHR咨询公司的创始人兼总裁，公司位于宾夕法尼亚州匹兹堡市。

B

She isn't alone in her frustration.

感到沮丧的并不只有她一人。

Between 11 million and 55 million meetings are held each day in the United States, costing most organisations between 7% and 15% of their personnel budgets.

美国每天召开的会议在1100万至5500万场之间，耗费了大多数组织7%至15%的人力预算。

Every week, employees spend about six hours in meetings, while the average manager meets for a staggering 23 hours.

每周，员工花费约六小时参加会议，而管理者的会议时间更是高达惊人的23小时。

C

And though experts agree that traditional meetings are essential for making certain decisions and developing strategy, some employees view them as one of the most unnecessary parts of the workday.

尽管专家一致认为，传统会议对于作出特定决策和制定战略不可或缺，但一些员工却视其为工作日中最没有必要的环节之一。

The result is not only hundreds of billions of wasted dollars, but an annoyance of what organisational psychologists call "meeting recovery syndrome (MRS)": time spent cooling off and regaining focus after a useless meeting.

其结果不仅是数千亿美元的浪费，还有组织心理学家所称的“会议恢复综合征（MRS）”所带来的困扰：即在一场毫无意义的会议之后，人们需要时间平复情绪、重新集中注意力。

If you run to the office kitchen to get some relief with colleagues after a frustrating meeting, you're likely experiencing meeting recovery syndrome.

如果一场令人沮丧的会议结束后，你跑去公司茶水间和同事聊聊天以求放松，那你很可能正在经历会议恢复综合征。

D

Meeting recovery syndrome is a concept that should be familiar to almost anyone who has held a formal job.

会议恢复综合征这一概念，对于几乎所有有过正式工作经历的人来说都不陌生。

It isn't ground-breaking to say workers feel fatigued after a meeting, but only in recent decades have scientists deemed the condition worthy of further investigation.

说员工在会议后感到疲惫并非什么开创性的观点，但直到近几十年来，科学家才认为这一状况值得进一步研究。

With its links to organisational efficiency and employee wellbeing, MRS has attracted the attention of psychologists aware of the need to understand its precise causes and cures.

由于与组织效率和员工身心健康密切相关，会议恢复综合征（MRS）已引起心理学家的关注，他们意识到有必要弄清其确切成因和对策。

E

Today, in so far as researchers can hypothesise, MRS is most easily understood as a slow renewal of finite mental and physical resources.

目前，在研究者所能提出的假设范围内，MRS最易被理解为有限的心智与体力资源的缓慢恢复过程。

When an employee sits through an ineffective meeting their brain power is essentially being drained away.

当员工熬过一场毫无成效的会议时，其脑力实际上正在被白白消耗。

Meetings drain vitality if they last too long, fail to engage employees or turn into one-sided lectures.

如果会议时间过长、无法调动员工参与，或沦为一言堂式的说教，就会损耗人的精力。

The conservation of resources theory, originally proposed in 1989 by Dr Stevan Hobfoll, states that psychological stress occurs when a person's resources are threatened or lost.

资源保存理论由斯蒂文·霍布福尔博士于1989年首次提出，该理论认为，当个人的资源受到威胁或丧失时，就会产生心理压力。

When resources are low, a person will shift into defence to conserve their remaining supply.

当资源不足时，人会转入防御状态，以保存剩余的资源储备。

In the case of office meetings, where some of employees' most valuable resources are their focus, alertness and motivation, this can mean an abrupt halt in productivity as they take time to recover.

就办公室会议而言，员工最宝贵的资源之一便是专注力、警觉性和积极性，这意味着生产力会骤然中断，因为他们需要时间来恢复。

F

As humans, when we transition from one task to another on the job—say from sitting in a meeting to doing normal work—it takes an effortful cognitive switch.

作为人类，在工作中从一项任务切换到另一项任务时——比如从开会转到正常工作——需要付出努力来完成认知转换。

We must detach ourselves from the previous task and expend significant mental energy to move on.

我们必须从上一项任务中抽离出来，并消耗大量脑力才能继续前行。

If we are already drained to dangerous levels, then making the mental switch to the next thing is extra tough.

如果我们已经精疲力竭到危险的程度，那么在心理上切换到下一项任务就会格外困难。

It's common to see people cyber-loafing after a frustrating meeting, going and getting coffee, interrupting a colleague and telling them about the meeting, and so on.

人们在经历一场令人沮丧的会议后上网摸鱼、去倒杯咖啡、打扰同事并跟他们聊会议的事等，都是很常见的现象。

G

Each person's ability to recover from horrible meetings is different.

每个人从糟糕的会议中恢复过来的能力各不相同。

Some can bounce back quickly, while others carry their fatigue until the end of the workday.

有些人能迅速恢复精力，而另一些人则会带着疲惫撑到工作日结束。

Yet while no formal MRS studies are currently underway, one can loosely speculate on the length of an average employee's lag time.

然而，尽管目前尚无正式的MRS（会议残留综合征）研究正在进行，人们还是可以大致推测一下普通员工的恢复时长。

Switching tasks in a non-MRS condition takes about 10 to 15 minutes.

在非MRS条件下切换任务大约需要10到15分钟。

With MRS, it may take as long as 45 minutes on average.

而在MRS条件下，平均可能需要长达45分钟。

It's even worse when a worker has several meetings that are separated by 30 minutes.

如果员工的几场会议之间仅间隔30分钟，情况就更糟了。

"Not enough time to transition in a non-MRS situation to get anything done, and in an MRS situation, not quite enough time to recover for the next meeting," says researcher Joseph Allen.

研究人员约瑟夫·艾伦说："在非MRS情况下，没有足够的时间完成任务切换；而在MRS情况下，也没有足够的时间为下一场会议恢复精力。"

"Then, add the compounding of back-to-back bad meetings and we may have an epidemic on our hands."

"再加上一场接一场糟糕会议的累积效应，我们可能正面临一场流行病。"

H

In an effort to combat the side effects of MRS, Allen, along with researcher Joseph Mroz and colleagues at the University of Nebraska-Omaha, published a study detailing the best ways to avoid common traps, including a concise checklist of do's and don'ts applicable to any workplace.

为了对抗MRS带来的副作用，艾伦与研究人员约瑟夫·姆罗兹以及内布拉斯加大学奥马哈分校的同事们合作，发表了一项研究，详细阐述了避免常见陷阱的最佳方法，其中包括一份适用于任何工作场所的简明"宜""忌"清单。

Drawing from around 200 papers to compile their comprehensive list, Mroz and his team may now hold a remedy to the largely undefined problem of MRS.

姆罗兹及其团队参考了约200篇论文来编制这份全面的清单，如今他们或许已经找到了针对这一在很大程度上尚未被定义的MRS问题的解决之道。

I

Mroz says a good place to start is asking ourselves if our meetings are even necessary in the first place.

姆罗兹说，一个好的起点是扪心自问：我们的会议是否真的有必要。

If all that's on the agenda is a quick catch-up, or some non-urgent information sharing, it may better suit the group to send around an email instead.

如果议程上只是简短的沟通交流，或是分享一些非紧急的信息，那么发一封电子邮件可能更适合这个团队。

"The second thing I would always recommend is keep the meeting as small as possible," says Mroz.

姆罗兹说："我总是建议的第二点，就是尽可能让会议规模保持小。"

"If they don't actually have some kind of immediate input, then they can follow up later. They don't need to be sitting in this hour-long meeting."

如果他们实际上并没有什么当场需要提供的意见，那么他们可以稍后再跟进。他们没必要坐在那儿听整整一小时的会议。

Less time in meetings would ultimately lead to more employee engagement in the meetings they do attend, which experts agree is a proven remedy for MRS.

减少会议时间，最终会让员工在真正参加的会议上更加投入；专家们一致认为，这是治疗MRS（会议后遗症）的可靠良方。

J

Employees also feel taxed when they are invited together to meetings that don't inspire participation, says Cliff Scott, professor of organisational science.

组织科学教授克利夫·斯科特说，当员工被一并召集去参加那些令人提不起参与兴致的会议时，也会感到不堪重负。

It takes precious time for them to vent their emotions, complain and try to regain focus after a pointless meeting—one of the main traps of MRS. Over time as employees find themselves tied up in more and more unnecessary meetings—and thus dealing with increasing lag times from MRS—the waste of workday hours can feel insulting.

一场毫无意义的会议之后，他们还要耗费宝贵的时间去发泄情绪、抱怨，并努力重新集中注意力——这正是MRS的主要陷阱之一。长此以往，当员工发现自己被卷入越来越多的无谓会议，因而要承受MRS越来越长的余波时，这种对工作时间的浪费会让人感到被冒犯。

K

Despite the relative scarcity of research behind the subject, Hartman has taught herself many of the same tricks suggested in Mroz's study, and has come a long way since her days of being stuck with unnecessary meetings.

尽管围绕这一主题的研究相对匮乏，哈特曼还是自学了许多与姆罗兹研究中所建议的相同技巧，与当初被无谓会议困住的日子相比，她已大有改观。

The people she invites to meetings today include not just the essential employees, but also representatives from every department that might have a stake in the issue at hand.

如今她邀请来开会的，不仅有关键员工，还有每一个可能与当前议题利害相关的部门的代表。

Managers like her, who seek input even from non-experts to shape their decisions, can find greater support and cooperation from their workforce, she says.

她说，像她这样的管理者，即便非专业人士的意见也会听取，以此来完善决策，因而能从员工那里获得更多的支持与配合。

L

If an organisation were to apply all 22 suggestions from Mroz and Allen's findings, the most noticeable difference would be a stark decrease in the total number of meetings on the schedule, Mroz says.

姆罗兹说，如果一个组织把姆罗兹与艾伦研究中的22条建议全部付诸实施，最明显的变化将是日程表上会议总数的大幅减少。

Less time in meetings would ultimately lead to increased productivity, which is the ultimate objective of convening a meeting.

减少会议时间最终会带来更高的生产力，而这正是召开会议的终极目的。

While none of the counter-MRS ideas have been tested empirically yet, Allen says one trick with promise is for employees to identify things that quickly change their mood from negative to positive.

艾伦说，尽管这些对抗MRS的办法尚未经过实证检验，但其中一项颇有希望的做法，是让员工找出那些能迅速让自己心情由阴转晴的事物。

As simple as it sounds, finding a personal happy place, going there and then coming straight back to work might be key to facilitating recovery.

听起来再简单不过，但找到属于自己的那片快乐天地，去那里待上一会儿，然后马上回到工作中，或许正是促进恢复的关键。

M

Leaders should also see themselves as "stewards of everyone else's valuable time", adds Steven Rogelberg, author of The Surprising Science of Meetings.

《会议的惊人科学》一书作者史蒂文·罗格尔伯格补充说，领导者也应把自己视为"他人宝贵时间的守护者"。

Having the skills to foresee potential traps and treat employees' endurance with care allows leaders to provide effective short-term deterrents to MRS.

领导者若具备预见潜在陷阱的技能，并能体恤员工的承受力，就能为应对MRS（会议恢复综合征）提供有效的短期预防手段。

N

Most important, however, is for organisations to awaken to the concept of meetings being flexible, says Allen.

然而，艾伦指出，最重要的是让组织意识到会议应当具有灵活性。

By reshaping the way they prioritise employees' time, companies can eliminate the very sources of MRS in their tracks.

通过重新调整对员工时间的优先排序方式，企业可以从源头上根除MRS的诱因。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1442 词 · 25 句

P1

Sarcasm and jazz have something surprisingly in common: You know them when you hear them.

讽刺和爵士乐有一个出人意料的共同点：一听就知道。

Sarcasm is mostly understood through tone of voice, which is used to portray the opposite of the literal words.

讽刺主要靠语调来理解，语调所传达的往往与字面意思相反。

For example, when someone says, "Well, that's exactly what I need right now," their tone can tell you it's not what they need at all.

比如，当有人说“嗯，这正是我现在所需要的”时，从他的语调你能听出，他根本不需要这个。

P2

Most frequently, sarcasm highlights an irritation or is, quite simply, mean.

讽刺最常用来表达恼怒，或者说，纯粹就是刻薄。

P3

If you want to be happier and improve your relationships, cut out sarcasm.

如果你想更快乐、改善人际关系，就别再说讽刺话了。

Why?

为什么？

Because sarcasm is actually hostility disguised as humor.

因为讽刺其实是披着幽默外衣的敌意。

P4

Despite smiling outwardly, many people who receive sarcastic comments feel put down and often think the sarcastic person is rude, or contemptible.

尽管表面上在笑，许多遭到讽刺的人却感到被贬低，并且常常认为讽刺者粗鲁或可鄙。

Indeed, it's not surprising that the origin of the word sarcasm derives from the Greek word "sarkazein" which literally means "to tear or strip the flesh off."

事实上，“讽刺”（sarcasm）一词源自希腊语“sarkazein”，字面意思是“撕扯或剥离皮肉”，这并不令人意外。

Hence, it's no wonder that sarcasm is often preceded by the word "cutting" and that it hurts.

因此，讽刺常被冠以“尖刻”（cutting）一词，而且会伤人，也就不足为奇了。

P5

What's more, since actions strongly determine thoughts and feelings, when a person consistently acts sarcastically it may only serve to heighten their underlying hostility and insecurity.

更重要的是，由于行为在很大程度上决定思想和感受，一个人若总是表现得尖酸刻薄，反而只会加剧其内心深处的敌意和不安全感。

After all, when you come right down to it, sarcasm can be used as a subtle form of bullying—and most bullies are angry, insecure, or cowardly.

毕竟说到底，讽刺可以被用作一种隐蔽的霸凌方式——而大多数霸凌者都是愤怒、缺乏安全感或懦弱的人。

P6

Alternatively, when a person stops voicing negative comments, especially sarcastic ones, they may soon start to feel happier and more self-confident.

反之，当一个人不再发表负面评论，尤其是带有讽刺意味的评论时，他往往会很快变得更快乐、更自信。

Also, other people in their life benefit even more because they no longer have to hear the emotionally hurtful language of sarcasm.

此外，他身边的其他人受益更大，因为他们再也不必听到那些带有情感伤害的讽刺语言了。

P7

Now, I'm not saying all sarcasm is bad.

当然，我并不是说所有讽刺都不好。

It may just be better used sparingly—like a potent spice in cooking.

讽刺或许只是少用为妙——就像烹饪中的一种烈性香料。

Too much of the spice, and the dish will be overwhelmed by it.

香料放得太多，整道菜就会被它的味道盖过。

Similarly, an occasional dash of sarcastic wit can spice up a chat and add an element of humor to it.

同样，偶尔来一点讽刺的妙语，能让闲谈更有滋味，增添几分幽默。

But a big or steady serving of sarcasm will overwhelm the emotional flavor of any conversation and can taste very bitter to its recipient.

但若大量或持续地使用讽刺，就会淹没任何对话的情感韵味，而且会让听者感到苦涩难当。

P8

So, tone down the sarcasm and work on clever wit instead, which is usually without any hostility and thus more appreciated by those you're communicating with.

所以，少一些讽刺，多在机智风趣上下功夫。机智风趣通常不带任何敌意，因而更受交谈对象的欣赏。

In essence, sarcasm is easy while true, harmless wit takes talent.

说到底，讽刺容易做到，而真正的、无伤大雅的机智却需要才华。

P9

Thus, the main difference between wit and sarcasm is that, as already stated, sarcasm is often hostility disguised as humor.

因此，机智与讽刺的主要区别在于——正如前面所说——讽刺常常是伪装成幽默的敌意。

It can be intended to hurt and is often bitter and biting.

讽刺可能有意伤人，而且往往尖酸刻薄。

Witty statements are usually in response to someone's unhelpful remarks or behaviors, and the intent is to untangle and clarify the issue by emphasizing its absurdities.

机智的言辞通常是针对他人无益的言论或行为而作出的回应，其意图是通过凸显事情的荒谬之处来理清并阐明问题。

Sarcastic statements are expressed in a cutting manner; witty remarks are delivered with undisguised and harmless humor.

讽刺性话语措辞尖刻；机智妙语则以坦率无害的幽默来表达。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2443 词 · 24 句

P1

Variability is crucially important for learning new skills.

变异性对学习新技能至关重要。

Consider learning how to serve in tennis.

以学习网球发球为例。

Should you always practise serving from the exactly same location on the court, aiming at the same spot?

你是否应该总是在球场上的同一位置练习发球，并瞄准同一个落点？

Although practising in more variable conditions will be slower at first, it will likely make you a better tennis player in the end.

尽管在更多变的条件下练习起初会较慢，但最终很可能让你成为更好的网球选手。

This is because variability leads to better generalisation of what is learned.

这是因为变异性有助于对所学内容实现更好的泛化。

P2

This principle is found in many domains, including speech perception and learning categories.

这一原理存在于许多领域，包括语音感知和类别学习。

For instance, infants will struggle to learn the category "dog" if they are only exposed to Chihuahuas, instead of many different kinds of dogs.

例如，如果婴儿只接触吉娃娃而不接触多种不同的狗，他们将难以学会"狗"这一类别。

P3

"There are over ten different names for this basic principle," says Limor Raviv, the senior investigator of a recent study.

近期一项研究的首席研究员利莫尔·拉维夫（Limor Raviv）表示："这一基本原理有十多种不同的名称。"

"Learning from less variable input is often fast, but may fail to generalise to new stimuli."

"从变异性较低的输入中学习往往很快，但可能无法泛化到新的刺激上。"

P4

To identify key patterns and understand the underlying principles of variability effects, Raviv and her colleagues reviewed over 150 studies on variability and generalisation across fields, including computer science, linguistics, categorisation, visual perception and formal education.

为了识别关键模式并理解变异性效应背后的原理，拉维夫和同事们回顾了来自计算机科学、语言学、分类、视觉感知和正规教育等领域的150多项关于变异性与泛化的研究。

P5

The researchers discovered that, across studies, the term variability can refer to at least four different kinds of variability, such as set size and scheduling.

研究人员发现，在各项研究中，"变异性"一词至少可以指代四种不同的变异类型，例如集合规模和时间安排。

"These four kinds of variability have never been directly compared—which means that we currently don't know which is most effective for learning," says Raviv.

拉维夫说："这四种变异类型此前从未被直接比较过——这意味着我们目前还不知道哪种对学习最有效。"

P6

The impact of variability depends on whether it is relevant to the task or not.

变异性的影响取决于它是否与任务相关。

But according to the 'Mr. Miyagi principle', practising seemingly unrelated skills may actually benefit learning of other skills.

但根据“宫城先生原则”，练习看似无关的技能，实际上可能有助于其他技能的学习。

P7

But why does variability impact learning and generalisation?

然而，变异性为何会影响学习与泛化呢？

One theory is that more variable input can highlight which aspects of a task are relevant and which are not.

一种理论认为，更具变异性的输入能够凸显任务中哪些方面是相关的、哪些是无关的。

P8

Another theory is that greater variability leads to broader generalisations.

另一种理论认为，变异性越大，泛化的范围就越广。

This is because variability will represent the real world better, including atypical examples.

这是因为变异性能更好地呈现真实世界，其中也包括非典型例子。

P9

A third reason has to do with the way memory works: when training is variable, learners are forced to actively reconstruct their memories.

第三个原因与记忆的运作方式有关：当训练具有变异性时，学习者被迫主动重建记忆。

P10

"Understanding the impact of variability is important for literally every aspect of our daily life. Beyond affecting the way we learn language, motor skills, and categories, it even has an impact on our social lives,"

“理解变异性的影响，对我们日常生活的方方面面都至关重要。它不仅影响我们学习语言、运动技能和类别的方式，甚至对我们的社交生活也有影响。”

explains Raviv.

拉维夫解释道。

"For example, face recognition is affected by whether people grew up in a small community or in a larger community. Exposure to fewer faces during childhood is associated with diminished face memory."

“例如，人脸识别会受到人们是成长于小社区还是大社区的影响。童年时期接触到的面孔越少，面孔记忆能力就越弱。”

P11

"We hope this work will spark people's curiosity and generate more work on the topic," concludes Raviv.

“我们希望这项工作能激发人们的好奇心，并推动更多关于这一课题的研究。”拉维夫总结道。

"Our paper raises a lot of open questions. Can we find similar effects of variability beyond the brain, for instance, in the immune system?"

“我们的论文提出了许多悬而未决的问题。比如，我们能否在大脑之外，例如在免疫系统中，发现类似的变异性效应？”

第 1 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,372 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）283 词 · 13 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) appealB) extravagantlyC) intrinsicallyD) ironyE) laymanF) literallyG) majesticH) maliciousI) meadowJ) mingleK) predatorL) rageM) scrapeN) shrewdO) thrifty

With the rapid progress in the economy, it is natural that people's ideology is bound for change.

随着经济的飞速发展，人们的思想观念必然会随之改变，这是再自然不过的事。

Being thrifty is no longer a virtue to some people, and they are not alone in holding this view.

在有些人看来，节俭已不再是美德，而持这种看法的人绝非少数。

Each might have different viewpoints on how to lead one's lifestyle: some prefer simplicity, some love luxuries, some spend extravagantly, some accumulate as much as they can like a country mouse.

每个人对于生活方式各有不同的选择：有人崇尚简朴，有人贪恋奢华，有人挥金如土，有人像乡下老鼠一样拼命积攒。

As I was born poor and in the countryside, the simple rural life seldom loses its appeal to me, but as my station changes, I have an opportunity to mingle with the newly rich, an experience which has revolutionized my view of lifestyle.

我出身贫寒，生于农村，质朴的乡村生活对我始终不乏吸引力；但随着身份地位的变化，我有机会与新富阶层交往，这段经历彻底改变了我对生活方式的看法。

It is evil or sinful to spend little and to save much, an idea only recently being revealed to me.

少花钱、多储蓄反倒是邪恶或有罪的——这种观念直到最近才映入我的眼帘。

Actually the whole world is in a rage to borrow and to spend, with the USA leading the trend.

事实上，整个世界都在狂热地借贷和消费，美国更是引领了这一潮流。

The USA is still the richest and most powerful country in the world, but the irony is that they borrow money from us.

美国仍然是世界上最富裕、最强大的国家，但颇具讽刺意味的是，他们却向我们借钱。

Though a layman of economics, at least I know that investing with borrowed money will turn the borrower into the predator rather than the decent and kind lender.

尽管我是经济学的门外汉，但至少我明白：用借来的钱去投资，会把借款人变成掠夺者，而不是体面而善良的出借人。

An apparent case is real estate.

最明显的例子就是房地产。

Those who are shrewd and have got loans from the bank become relatively rich by selling their purchased home at a price several folds higher.

那些精明能干、从银行贷到款的人，以高出数倍的价格转手卖掉所购房产，从而变得相对富有。

Even education is not immune from this theory.

就连教育也未能幸免于这套理论。

Many of those poor parents who have the vision to send their children to college have benefited from this investment, even though they have to scrape a living.

许多有远见、倾力供子女上大学的贫困父母都从这项投资中获益，尽管他们不得不勉强糊口。

It is reasonable to conclude that spending is praiseworthy, supposing it is not beyond your means.

可以合理地得出这样的结论：只要不超出自己的经济能力，花钱消费是值得肯定的。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1194 词 · 63 句

A

On a small fruit farm near the Straits of Malacca, Lim Kok Ann is down to just one tree growing kedondong, a crunchy, sour berry that Malaysians mostly use in pickles and salads.

在马六甲海峡附近的一座小果园里，林国安（Lim Kok Ann）只剩下最后一种果树——结着kedondong（一种口感脆爽、味道酸涩的浆果）的树。马来西亚人大多用它来做腌菜和沙拉。

"It's not very well-known," says the 45-year-old, who is instead focusing on longan berries and pineapples, which have bigger markets.

“它不怎么出名，”这位45岁的果农说。他转而主攻龙眼和菠萝，因为这两种水果的市场更大。

"We have to grow what is profitable," he says.

“我们得种赚钱的东西，”他说。

B

But less than an hour away in the Malaysian countryside, inside three giant, silver domes, scientists are trying to change the future of food.

然而，不到一小时车程之外的马来西亚乡村，科学家们正在三个巨大的银色穹顶之内，试图改变食物的未来。

They're pushing the boundaries of what humans eat by growing and processing so-called "alternative" crops—such as kedondong.

他们通过种植和加工所谓的“替代”作物——比如kedondong——来拓展人类食用的边界。

At the headquarters of global research centre Crops For the Future (CFF) this particular under-used fruit has been turned into a sugar-free juice, high in vitamin C and getting top marks in sensory evaluations.

在全球研究中心“未来作物”（Crops For the Future，简称CFF）的总部，这种长期被忽视的水果已被制成无糖果汁，维生素C含量高，并在感官评价中获得最高分。

"Anything you see here is a forgotten crop," says Sayed Azam-Ali of the abundant plants weaving through the gardens of CFF outside Malaysia's capital Kuala Lumpur.

“你在这里看到的每一种植物，都是被遗忘的作物。”赛义德·阿扎姆-阿里（Sayed Azam-Ali）指着CFF园区内缠绕交错的繁茂植物说。该中心位于马来西亚首都吉隆坡郊外。

C

Prof Azam-Ali explains that just four crops—wheat, maize, rice and soybean—provide two-thirds of the world's food supply.

阿扎姆-阿里教授解释说，仅小麦、玉米、水稻和大豆这四种作物，就提供了全世界三分之二的食物供应。

"We're dependent on these four," he says.

“我们的食物供应依赖于这四种作物，”他说。

"But actually there're 7,000 crops we've been farming for thousands of years. We ignore all of those."

“但实际上，我们耕种了几千年的作物多达7000种，而我们却对它们视而不见。”

Researchers are trying to unlock the potential of these ignored crops—plants they describe as forgotten, under-used or "alternative" as they are displaced by increasingly uniform diets fuelled by processed ingredients from the major crops.

研究人员正试图释放这些被忽视作物的潜力——由于主要作物的加工配料催生出越来越单一的饮食结构，这些植物被挤出主流，因而被人们描述为被遗忘、未充分利用或“替代”作物。

D

It's a timely quest.

这是一次恰逢其时的探索。

The food sector is already responsible for nearly a third of global greenhouse gas emissions.

食品行业目前占全球温室气体排放量的近三分之一。

By 2050 it estimates the world must produce 50% more food to feed the projected global population of 10 billion.

该报告预计，到2050年，全球粮食产量必须增加50%，才能养活预计达到100亿的世界人口。

Meeting this demand without contributing to climate change calls for urgent solutions.

要在不加剧气候变化的前提下满足这一需求，亟需找到解决方案。

E

Forgotten crops hold key answers.

被遗忘的作物蕴含着关键答案。

By investing in neglected local plants, countries can reduce their reliance on imported crops and their carbon-heavy supply chains.

通过投资开发被忽视的本地作物，各国可以减少对进口作物及其高碳排放供应链的依赖。

Bringing back the variety of crops humans once ate also boosts food security at a time warming climates threaten existing crops.

恢复人类曾经食用过的作物多样性，还能在气候变暖威胁现有作物之际增强粮食安全。

On top of that forgotten crops are among the most climate-resilient and nutritious, argues Azam-Ali.

阿扎姆-阿里指出，除此之外，被遗忘的作物还是最具气候韧性和营养价值的作物之一。

His summary is plain: "Dietary diversification is critical to the future of humanity."

他的总结简明扼要："饮食多样化对人类的未来至关重要。"

F

Food security experts agree.

粮食安全专家对此表示认同。

"There is no food insecurity in the world, there is food ignorance," says Cecilia Tortajada, a senior research fellow at the Institute of Water Policy at the National University of Singapore.

新加坡国立大学水政策研究所高级研究员塞西莉亚·托尔塔哈达说："世界上不存在粮食不安全的问题，存在的只是对粮食的无知。"

"Whenever we have native crops we tend to disregard them as if they were not valuable but they are," she adds.

她补充道："每当我们拥有本土作物时，往往视而不见，仿佛它们毫无价值，但实际上它们价值非凡。"

G

Azam-Ali knows that scepticism firsthand.

阿扎姆-阿里对这种质疑深有体会。

He came across alternative crops in the 1980s through the work of women farmers he met in Niger.

20世纪80年代，他在尼日尔结识了几位女农人，通过她们的工作首次接触到了替代作物。

The then-PhD student remembers marvelling at the crops they grew in their backyards, without the benefits of technology, to feed their families when the big crops failed.

这位当时的博士生回忆说，那些女农人在自家后院种植的作物令他惊叹不已——没有借助任何技术手段，她们靠这些作物养活家人，度过大田作物歉收的难关。

He saw a tremendous opportunity to build alternative food systems.

他看到了构建替代粮食体系的巨大机遇。

But "the resistance was enormous," he recalls.

但据他回忆，"当时的阻力非常大。"

H

Undeterred, he ploughed on.

他毫不气馁，坚持推进。

Project after project helped prove these crops were viable in different environments as alternatives to the staple ones.

一个又一个项目陆续开展，证明了这些作物能够在不同环境中种植，可作为主粮的替代品。

But the question of whether these crops would be marketable remained.

但这些作物能否打开市场，始终是个悬而未决的问题。

"That's the critical thing," he says.

他说："这是最关键的一点。"

In one of the centre's domes, food technologist Tan Xinlin uses powdered moringa leaves in place of some wheat flour to bake a cake lower in gluten and higher in nutrients.

在该中心的一座穹顶温室内，食品技术员谭歆林（Tan Xinlin）用辣木叶粉替代部分小麦粉来烘焙蛋糕，使蛋糕的麸质含量更低、营养更丰富。

Tan's job is to create recipes with these still-unfamiliar ingredients that will appeal to both local and international tastes.

谭歆林的工作，是用这些仍不为人熟知的食材研发食谱，使其同时迎合本地与国际的口味。

In recent years she has used some of the forgotten crops grown at CFF, such as moringa and bambara groundnut, to make everything from instant soup to Indian snacks.

近年来，她利用CFF种植的一些被遗忘的作物，如辣木和班巴拉豆，制作了从速食汤到印度小吃等各种食品。

"I try to modernise forgotten crops instead of using old recipes." says Tan, who is also a trained chef.

谭歆林也是一名受过专业训练的厨师，她说："我致力于让被遗忘的作物焕发新生，而不是照搬老配方。"

It's a strategy to appeal to the world's growing middle classes who are increasingly turning to the fast and processed food industries.

这一策略旨在吸引全球日益壮大的中产阶级，他们正越来越多地转向快餐和加工食品产业。

It's also a way to help counter perceptions of local crops as "old or poor people's food" or as inferior "women's crops", adds Tan.

谭补充说，这也是一种有助于消除人们对本地作物成见的方式——人们往往视其为"穷人或老人吃的东西"，或低人一等的"妇女作物"。

I

The roots of these connotations about local foods can run deep.

这些关于本地食物的负面联想可能根深蒂固。

The bambara groundnut, a protein-rich native crop of sub-Saharan Africa that is also grown in parts of southeast Asia, can trace its marginalisation to colonial rule.

班巴拉花生是撒哈拉以南非洲的一种高蛋白本土作物，东南亚部分地区也有种植，其被边缘化的历史可追溯至殖民统治时期。

"African women who grew bambara groundnut were actually punished for growing it," says Azam-Ali.

阿扎姆-阿里说："种植班巴拉花生的非洲妇女实际上因此受到惩罚。"

"Colonial powers said you can't grow that because there's no oil. We can't get a market for it."

"殖民当局说你们不能种这个，因为它不产油，我们卖不出去。"

But today the bambara murukku is one of CFF's best reviewed foods and they are aiming to get it into grocery stores, pointing to the success of crops like quinoa to potential investors.

但如今，班巴拉穆鲁库（一种用班巴拉花生制成的传统零食）是CFF好评最多的食品之一，他们正设法将其推向杂货店，并以藜麦等作物的成功案例向潜在投资者游说。

Some 30 years ago, quinoa was virtually unheard of outside its native mountains in Bolivia and Peru.

大约30年前，藜麦在其原产地玻利维亚和秘鲁的山区之外几乎无人知晓。

Today the nutritious grain is found on the menus of lavish restaurants across the world.

如今，这种营养丰富的谷物已出现在全球豪华餐厅的菜单上。

J

Measuring crops by nutrition instead of yield is at the heart of the forgotten foods enterprise.

以营养价值而非产量来衡量作物，正是"被遗忘的食物"这一事业的核心理念。

Ever since the "green revolution" of the 1960s, high-yielding crops have dominated modern agriculture.

自20世纪60年代"绿色革命"以来，高产作物一直主导着现代农业。

That was in part a crucial response to devastating famines at a time when the world needed to increase its food supply.

在某种程度上，这是对当时毁灭性饥荒的一种关键应对，彼时世界亟需增加粮食供应。

Today "nutrition is becoming a time bomb", says Azam-Ali, as growing carbon dioxide levels strip crops of their minerals.

阿扎姆-阿里说，随着二氧化碳浓度不断升高，农作物中的矿物质被逐渐耗尽，“营养正成为一颗定时炸弹”。

Instead of bio-fortifying major crops we should be investing in those forgotten crops that are already more nutritious, he asserts.

他主张，与其对主要作物进行生物强化，不如投资于那些本就营养更丰富的被遗忘作物。

K

In the bowels of CFF's third dome, lab manager Gomathy Sethuraman opens a window into the centre's "crown jewels", revealing vines of winged beans growing under a bright yellow light.

在CFF第三个穹顶深处，实验室经理戈马蒂·塞图拉曼打开一扇窗，向人们展示了该中心的“镇馆之宝”——在明亮的黄光下生长的四角豆藤蔓。

It's one of multiple chambers where scientists are studying the impact of higher temperatures and carbon dioxide levels on the nutritional make-up of alternative crops.

这是众多实验舱之一，科学家们在此研究升温和二氧化碳浓度升高对替代作物营养构成的影响。

This research is "the game changer", says Azam-Ali, ensuring that "future crops" are also the healthiest ones in warmer climates.

阿扎姆-阿里说，这项研究是“游戏规则的改变者”，它确保“未来的作物”在更温暖的气候中同样是最健康的。

L

There is a growing global momentum around forgotten foods, says Danielle Nierenberg, president of Food Tank, a US-based think tank.

美国智库Food Tank（食物力量）总裁丹妮尔·尼伦伯格表示，全球对被遗忘食物的关注度正与日俱增。

Other than CFF, which bills itself as the world's first research centre dedicated solely to under-utilised crops, there are other key groups championing agricultural diversity including Crop Trust, Slow Food, Icrisat and Bioversity International.

除了CFF——该中心自称是全球首家专门致力于利用不足作物的研究中心——还有其他多个关键组织在推动农业多样性，包括作物信托基金、慢食运动、国际半干旱热带作物研究所（Icrisat）和国际农业生物多样性应用研究机构（Bioversity International）。

Add to that more middle-income consumers searching for nutritious foods and others eager to try the unprocessed foods their grandparents once ate, she says.

她说，此外还有越来越多的中等收入消费者在寻找营养食品，以及一些人渴望尝试祖辈曾食用的未加工食品。

M

But the rising interest in forgotten foods in some quarters is overtaken by the global spread of Western-style diets heavy in sugar, fat and processed foods in others.

然而，一些人对被遗忘食物日益增长的兴趣，正被另一些地区西方饮食模式的全球扩张所盖过——这些饮食富含糖分、脂肪和加工食品。

N

A key obstacle to promoting fading local crops in Malaysia, for example, is "the obsession with imported products", says Jenifer Kuah, co-founder of a restaurant that champions locally-sourced food in an affluent suburb of Kuala Lumpur.

例如，吉隆坡一个高档社区的本土食材餐厅联合创始人珍妮弗·库阿表示，马来西亚推广本土日渐式微作物的一大障碍是“对进口产品的痴迷”。

Customers at Sitka, regarded as a pioneer in the country's small farm-to-table dining scene, still seek foreign ingredients as a "status symbol", she says.

她说，Sitka餐厅被视为该国小型农场直供餐饮的先驱，但其顾客仍把外国食材视为一种“身份象征”。

O

The argument for forgotten foods feels intuitive.

支持食用被遗忘食物的理由似乎不言自明。

Some analysts say it is in fact inevitable.

一些分析人士称，这其实是不可避免的。

"Climate change is going to mean almost certainly tastes are going to be forced to change," says Tim Lang, professor of food policy at City, University of London.

伦敦大学城市学院食品政策教授蒂姆·朗表示：“气候变化几乎注定会迫使人们的口味发生改变。”

We "have to get used to eating other crops" as yields of staple crops fall, he says.

他说，随着主要农作物产量下降，我们“不得不习惯食用其他作物”。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1447 词 · 26 句

P1

Imagine you're an alien sent to Earth to document the behaviour of the mammals inhabiting the planet.

想象一下，你是一名被派往地球的外星人，任务是记录这颗星球上哺乳动物的行为。

You stumble into a movie theatre that's showing the latest Hollywood horror film.

你误打误撞走进一家电影院，里面正在放映最新的好莱坞恐怖片。

P2

Several dozen humans are gathered together in a dark, undecorated room.

几十个人类聚集在一间昏暗、毫无装饰的房间里。

They're all staring at a rectangular area on which patterns of light change rapidly.

他们都盯着墙上的一个矩形区域，那上面的光影图案在飞速变幻。

P3

They are clearly in a state of high arousal.

他们显然处于高度兴奋的状态。

Their heart rate is elevated, they occasionally glance around nervously, and they sometimes jump collectively in their seats, and emit high-pitched warning calls.

他们的心率加快，不时紧张地四下张望，有时还会集体从座位上跳起来，发出尖锐的警报声。

P4

Eventually, the lights come up and the rectangular screen goes black.

终于，灯光亮起，那块矩形屏幕暗了下去。

The humans stand up and leave the room, chatting and laughing, and showing signs of pleasure.

人类站起身来离开房间，一边聊天一边欢笑，露出了愉悦的神情。

P5

Why do these humans voluntarily expose themselves to what appears to be a deeply unpleasant experience?

这些人类为什么自愿让自己经历一场看似极其难受的体验？

And why do they react so strongly to those patterns of light on a screen?

他们又为什么会对屏幕上那些光影图案做出如此强烈的反应？

P6

I am fascinated with the paradox of horror—the strange fact that many people seek out scary entertainment.

恐怖带来的悖论令我着迷——许多人竟会主动寻求惊吓式的娱乐，这一事实着实奇特。

P7

I think the answer to the puzzle lies in human nature.

我认为这个谜题的答案就在于人性。

P8

My research suggests that we humans evolved to find pleasure in situations that allow us to experience negative emotions in a safe context.

我的研究表明，人类在进化中形成了一种倾向：在安全的情境中体验负面情绪时，会感到愉悦。

You can see these elements of horror in children's games.

你可以在儿童游戏中看到恐怖的这些要素。

Take hide-and-seek for example, which is a simulation of a predator-prey interaction.

以捉迷藏为例，它模拟的正是捕食者与猎物之间的互动。

The kid hides and the adult pretends to be a predator, searching for the child while howling like a dangerous beast.

孩子躲藏起来，成年人则扮演捕食者，一边像危险的野兽般嚎叫，一边搜寻孩子。

P9

This simulation gives the child crucial information about how to avoid becoming prey, and children tend to find that kind of activity deeply satisfying, presumably because it gives them a safe experience of a potentially catastrophic scenario.

这种模拟向孩子传递了如何避免成为猎物的关键信息，而孩子们往往会觉得这类活动极具满足感，大概是因为它让孩子在安全的环境中体验了潜在的灾难性情境。

P10

They find it pleasurable, and pleasure is evolution's way of motivating us toward adaptive behaviour.

他们从中获得愉悦感，而愉悦正是进化用以激励我们采取适应性行为的方式。

P11

Horror is pleasurable to many people because it lets us play with negative emotions and develop coping strategies.

恐怖之所以让许多人感到愉悦，是因为它让我们得以把玩负面情绪，并发展出应对策略。

We learn what it feels like to be truly afraid, and we learn how to handle negative emotion.

我们得以体会真正恐惧时是什么感受，也学会如何应对负面情绪。

P12

How, then, does horror work?

那么，恐怖究竟是如何起作用的？

My research suggests that horror works by exploiting an ancient set of biological defence mechanisms—an evolved fear system, which we share with other animals.

我的研究表明，恐怖的运作方式是利用一套古老的生物防御机制——一套与其他动物共有的、经过进化的恐惧系统。

But humans are uniquely imaginative, and we use our evolved imagination to travel into virtual worlds that are full of danger.

但人类具有独特的想象力，我们运用进化而来的想象力，进入充满危险的虚拟世界。

P13

There are good reasons for watching a horror film, even if you're not a loyal horror fan.

即使你并非恐怖片的忠实拥趸，看恐怖片也有充分的理由。

If you make it through the film in one piece, you'll probably experience a strong sense of mastery, a sense that you were able to make it through an appalling experience.

如果你能完整地看完这部电影，你很可能会获得一种强烈的掌控感，一种你成功挺过了一段可怕经历的感觉。

Anyway, watching a horror film makes you better at handling your own fear, and who knows when that will become critically relevant?

总之，看恐怖片能让你更善于应对自己的恐惧，而谁又知道这种能力何时会变得至关重要呢？

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2448 词 · 22 句

P1

An awakening has been taking place in the physical world against the beauty model that has been dictated to us for years.

在现实世界中，一场觉醒运动正悄然兴起，反对多年来强加给我们的审美标准。

But in the digital arena, social media determines what is considered beautiful.

但在数字领域，社交媒体决定了什么才是美的标准。

P2

The two opposing struggles are taking place in parallel.

这两种对立的斗争正在同步进行。

In the physical world, the struggle goes against the underlying pressure exerted on women to conform to an unrealistic beauty ideal.

在现实世界中，这场斗争针对的是社会强加于女性、要求她们遵从不切实际的审美理想的深层压力。

As part of the struggle, various media outlets have presented women whose bodies don't correspond to the so-called ideal.

作为这场斗争的一部分，多家媒体报道了那些身材不符合所谓理想的女性。

All those women who had previously been excluded from the covers of magazines, television series and the public agenda, have become "legitimate".

所有这些曾经被排斥在杂志封面、电视剧和公共议题之外的女性，如今都成了"正当"的存在。

At the same time, a group of influencers have begun to upload to social media photos of themselves without makeup, and even photos in which they highlight supposed flaws.

与此同时，一批网红开始在社交媒体上传自己的素颜照片，甚至晒出那些所谓瑕疵的特写。

P3

Technology has reshaped our beauty ideal and is doing a great job communicating that message to the masses.

科技重塑了我们的审美理想，并且正在出色地将这一理念传播给大众。

One of the bizarre legacies of the past decade is the popularity of the "cyborg look", which illustrates what Americans will look like in 2050.

过去十年最离奇的遗产之一是"赛博格风格"的流行，这种风格展现了2050年美国人将会是什么样子。

P4

The cyborg look spread rapidly.

赛博格风格迅速蔓延开来。

Today, however, the Instagram face has become the new beauty ideal.

然而如今，Instagram脸已成为新的审美理想。

P5

The internalization of accepted beauty norms is much more effective when there is active involvement in the learning process.

当人们对审美规范的学习过程有主动参与时，对其的内化会更加有效。

The active involvement of users is reflected in the gamified interaction offered by the social media platforms—the ability to like, write a comment, compare, share.

用户的积极参与体现在社交媒体平台提供的游戏化互动上——点赞、发表评论、比较、分享等功能。

P6

Once the desired beauty ideal has been internalized, users are given tools or features to change their appearance to suit the accepted beauty ideal such as editing the image, choosing the ideal filter, the right background.

一旦理想的美貌标准被内化，用户便会获得各种工具或功能来改变自己的外貌，以迎合公认的审美理想，比如编辑图片、选择合适的滤镜和恰当的背景。

P7

A survey conducted in the United States revealed that more than 50% filter the images before posting them.

美国的一项调查显示，超过50%的人在发布照片前会对图片进行修饰。

And you will not be surprised to hear that the majority of them are women.

而当听到其中大多数是女性时，你并不会感到意外。

One of the significant consequences of obsessive filtering is the emerging tendency to treat oneself as an object to be observed and valued, in the same way another person observes and judges from the side.

过度滤镜的一个严重后果是，人们越来越倾向于把自己当作一个被观察和评价的对象，就像另一个人从旁审视和评判自己一样。

P8

The effect of the filters is already far beyond amiable amusement.

滤镜的影响早已远远超出了无伤大雅的娱乐。

The filters and the entire game played on the networks affect the mental health of the users.

滤镜以及网络上上演的整套把戏，正在影响用户的心理健康。

According to a study, apps like Instagram, Snapchat and FaceTune allow users to achieve a level of perfection that was previously only observed in beauty magazines.

一项研究表明，Instagram、Snapchat和FaceTune等应用让用户得以达到一种完美的程度，而这种完美以往只能在美容杂志上见到。

P9

Even though humanity has always cherished beauty, in the last decade our obsession with looks has reached an unprecedented peak.

尽管人类向来珍视美，但在过去十年里，我们对外貌的执念已达到前所未有的顶峰。

The time spent on social media creates an urge to achieve an impossible beauty ideal so powerful that the only thing that can fix it is not cosmetic intervention, but mental health care.

在社交媒体上花费的时间催生了对不可能实现的美貌理想的强烈渴望，其程度之强烈，以至于能解决它的不是医美手段，而是心理健康照护。

第 2 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,401 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）287 词 · 21 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) affectionateB) approximatelyC) elapsesD) erodeE) flatteredF) flipsG) haltH) overburdenedI) predominatesJ) propagateK) scenarioL) shadowM) tentativelyN) typicallyO) vigorous

Ever been talking about something important with someone you care about and found the discussion frustrating instead of fruitful?

你是否曾与在乎的人谈论重要话题，结果却发现交流令人沮丧而非富有成效？

Negative energy can then cast a shadow over your efforts to build a shared understanding.

这样一来，负面情绪就会给努力建立共识的过程蒙上阴影。

In fact collaborative problem-solving conversations require skills.

事实上，协作解决问题的对话需要技巧。

Strong listening skills are essential.

良好的倾听能力至关重要。

Sprinkling in positivity via appreciation, affectionate smiles, and warm comments helps.

适时穿插积极的表达——如表达欣赏、报以亲切的微笑、说些温暖的话语——会很有帮助。

So does the essential ability to keep your emotional tone in the calm zone.

同样重要的是，要能始终保持冷静的语气。

If the tone of a dialogue heats up and anger erupts, the dialogue flips from collaborative to adversarial, and progress is likely to come to an immediate halt.
