第 1 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,423 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）289 词 · 13 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) adolescentsB) arbitrarilyC) chronicD) cognitiveE) concedeF) disregardG) dropH) everI) maneuverJ) mortalityK) particularlyL) patternsM) refuteN) stereotypeO) vocal

By now, it is generally accepted that regular exercise comes with an assortment of health benefits for people who maintain the habit over time.

如今，人们普遍认为，长期保持运动习惯能给健康带来多方面的益处。

But is it ever too late to start?

但是，开始运动会不会为时已晚呢？

New research looked at people who increased their exercise levels after their 40s beyond levels they had when they were younger and those individuals showed declines in their risk of early mortality which were similar to those of people who exercised consistently throughout their lives.

一项新研究考察了那些在四十多岁后将运动量提升到超过年轻时水平的人群，结果发现，这些人的早亡风险有所下降，其降幅与一生坚持运动的人相当。

This was a drop of 32% to 35% compared to people who did not exercise.

与不运动的人相比，这一降幅为32%至35%。

Those who began exercising in middle age also saw reductions in their incidence of chronic diseases, such as heart disease and cancer, which were similar to the steady exercisers.

那些从中年才开始运动的人，其心脏病和癌症等慢性疾病的发病率也有所下降，降幅与长期坚持运动的人相近。

So why bother exercising consistently throughout your life?

那么，又何必一生都坚持运动呢？

The authors of the study concede that some might interpret the study's findings to mean that they can delay starting an exercise program but this would be a mistake.

该研究的作者承认，有些人可能会据此认为自己可以推迟开始锻炼计划，但这种想法是错误的。

There are other benefits, both mental and physical, to staying consistently active that we should not disregard.

坚持运动还有其他身心方面的好处，我们不应忽视。

These real health benefits include better cognitive functioning and lowered risk of gaining weight.

这些实实在在的健康益处包括更好的认知功能以及降低体重增加的风险。

The latter is particularly important given the global obesity epidemic.

考虑到全球肥胖流行的趋势，后一点尤为重要。

In addition, experts assert that starting younger is better because, if you make a habit of exercising when you are young, you are more likely to keep up those patterns later in life.

此外，专家指出，趁年轻开始运动更好，因为如果在年轻时养成运动习惯，日后更有可能坚持这一模式。

It is ideal, then, for people to start exercising when they are children or adolescents, and it is good to maintain an active lifestyle at all times regardless of your age.

因此，最好从小就养成运动习惯，而且无论年龄大小，始终保持积极的生活方式都是有益的。

However, this study does indicate that, if you have not been active, you can still benefit if you start becoming active in your 40s and 50s.

不过，这项研究确实表明，即使你过去一直不怎么活动，只要在四五十岁时开始积极运动，仍然能从中受益。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1231 词 · 64 句

A

Like most chefs, Flippy is not afraid of hard work.

和大多数厨师一样，Flippy 不怕辛苦。

Since last summer, that has meant 11 am until 7 pm shifts at Caliburger in Pasadena, California, as well as short assignments at Chick-N-Tots at Dodger Stadium in Los Angeles.

从去年夏天起，这意味着要在加州帕萨迪纳的 Caliburger 餐厅从上午11点工作到晚上7点，还要在洛杉矶道奇体育场的 Chick-N-Tots 餐厅完成一些短期任务。

These are long hours of repetitive work, where the boss demands absolute consistency.

这是漫长而重复的工作时间，老板要求绝对的一致性。

But you won't hear Flippy complain or say anything, in fact.

但你不会听到 Flippy 抱怨，事实上，它连一句话都不会说。

For Flippy is a robot — a cloud-connected mechanical arm with 3D thermal scanners for eyes — that can flip burgers or fry 80 baskets of food an hour, monitor that food and even clean up afterwards.

因为 Flippy 是一个机器人——一只接入云端的机械臂，眼睛是三维热成像扫描仪——它每小时能翻汉堡或炸80篮食物，监控食物的状态，甚至还能在事后清理打扫。

B

Flippy was created by Miso Robotics, part of the Cali Group, which is described by its CEO, John Miller, as: "A technology company that happens to sell cheeseburgers."

Flippy 由 Miso Robotics 创造，该公司隶属于 Cali Group，其首席执行官约翰·米勒这样形容这家公司："一家碰巧卖芝士汉堡的科技公司。"

Cali creates new machines that it road-tests in its Caliburger restaurants across the world, into which Flippy will be deployed this year.

Cali 开发新机器，并在其遍布全球的 Caliburger 餐厅进行实地测试，今年 Flippy 将在这些餐厅投入使用。

And Flippy is not alone.

而且 Flippy 并非孤例。

Also in California, Bear Robotics has developed a self-guiding robot, Penny, which has so far served 40,000 diners.

同样在加州，Bear Robotics 开发了一款自主导航机器人 Penny，迄今为止已为四万名顾客提供过服务。

C

Ten years ago, robot-chefs and waiters were pure science fiction.

十年前，机器人厨师和服务员还纯粹是科幻小说中的事物。

Today, they are a reality, and at prices that make them a plausible investment.

如今，它们已成为现实，而且价格合理，是一项值得考虑的投资。

From May, Penny will be shipped on a subscription basis that offers "an immediate return", says Bear Robotics' chief operating officer, Juan Higueros.

Bear Robotics 首席运营官胡安·伊格罗斯表示，从五月起，Penny 将以订阅方式推出，能够"立即带来回报"。

Flippy will cost a reported $60,000 to $100,000.

据报道，Flippy的售价在6万至10万美元之间。

Fully automated burger restaurants managed with minimal human oversight — where customers order at screens, pay electronically, and eat food cooked and delivered by robots — are now a possibility.

几乎无需人工监管的全自动汉堡餐厅如今已不再是幻想——顾客在屏幕上点餐、电子支付，食物由机器人烹饪和送达。

D

The technology exists; it just needs knitting together cost-effectively and in a way diners buy into.

技术已经具备，只是需要以经济高效的方式将各个环节整合起来，并让食客买账。

In an industry keen to slash labor costs and increase profits, further automation seems inevitable.

在这样一个急于削减人力成本、提高利润的行业中，进一步自动化似乎势在必行。

After an eight-month test at Kang Nam Tofu House in California, Bear Robotics credited Penny with driving a 28% increase in sales.

在加州康南豆腐馆（Kang Nam Tofu House）为期八个月的试用之后，Bear Robotics公司称Penny送餐机器人使餐厅销售额增长了28%。

Meanwhile, in US trials, the self-ordering screens that McDonald's is currently installing at its 1,300 UK restaurants yielded a reported 30% rise in order values.

与此同时，在美国的试用中，麦当劳目前正在其1300家英国餐厅安装的自助点餐屏幕，据称使每单消费金额提升了30%。

E

Personalisation is seen as key to the appeal of this technology, alongside its ability to reliably push so-called "upsells" (meal deals, extras, larger drinks) to customers.

这项技术的吸引力被认为关键在于个性化，以及它能够稳定地向顾客推销所谓的"加购项"（套餐、加料、大杯饮料）。

Soon when you log into a restaurant app, it will be able to use your data and purchasing history to recommend dishes to you — factoring in everything from the weather to, if you are ordering at a screen that can read your face, your mood.

不久的将来，当你登录一家餐厅的App时，它就能利用你的数据和购买记录为你推荐菜品——从天气到你的情绪，一切因素都会被纳入考量（如果你是在一台能识别人脸的屏幕前点餐的话）。

F

"Right now people compromise on choice. We're presented with a one-size-fits-all menu. Ultimately, technology can allow us to make personal choices. You love spicy food and chicken? Here's the dish people like you mostly order,"

"目前人们在选择上有所妥协，摆在我们面前的是一份千篇一律的菜单。而技术最终能让我们做出个性化选择。你爱吃辣，喜欢鸡肉？这是和你口味相似的人最常点的菜品。"

says Tom Weaver, CEO at the hospitality tech company Flyt.

餐饮科技公司Flyt的首席执行官汤姆·韦弗（Tom Weaver）说道。

He talks enthusiastically about the Helsinki pop-up Take-In, a full-service restaurant where diners ordered dishes in from various delivery services.

他兴致勃勃地谈起赫尔辛基的快闪餐厅Take-In，那是一家全服务餐厅，顾客可以点选多家外卖平台送来的菜品。

"For the first time in a few hundred years, digital is allowing us to create new versions of restaurants."

"几百年来第一次，数字技术让我们得以创造出全新形态的餐厅。"

G

If high-street fast food is automation-ready, its impact on high-end restaurants is likely to be subtler and slower.

如果说街头快餐已经具备了自动化条件，那么自动化对高端餐厅的影响可能会更微妙、也来得更慢。

"It'll probably be a very long time before we see a five-star robot-chef," says Noel Sharkey, professor of AI and robotics at the University of Sheffield.

谢菲尔德大学人工智能与机器人学教授诺埃尔·夏基说：“我们大概还要等很久，才能看到五星级的机器人厨师。”

"Apart from the AI creativity gap, great cooking involves a subtle understanding of ingredients and delicate cooking that would be enormously challenging for robots. Placing fragile foods on plates would be incredibly slow."

“除了人工智能在创造力上的差距之外，高超的烹饪还涉及对食材的细腻理解和精细烹调，这对机器人来说是极大的挑战。把易碎的食物摆放到盘子里，对它们来说会慢得惊人。”

H

Currently, robots have limited functionality.

目前，机器人的功能还很有限。

A human needs to load Penny with plates that diners lift off themselves.

仍需人工把餐盘装到“佩妮”机器人上，再由食客自行取下餐盘。

At the robot-powered US pizza-delivery company Zume, the more difficult jobs, such as topping pizzas, are still done by humans.

在美国机器人披萨配送公司Zume，难度较大的工作——比如给披萨加配料——目前仍由人工完成。

But Moley, which will launch soon, offers an idea of the sophistication to come.

但即将推出的Moley机器人厨师，让人得以一窥未来技术的精细程度。

Modelled on the movements of a MasterChef contest winner, Tim Anderson, its robotic arms are fixed over a stove and programmed to prepare dishes from raw ingredients.

它以《厨艺大师》比赛冠军蒂姆·安德森的动作作为原型，机械臂固定在炉灶上方，可按照程序从原料开始制作菜肴。

It is designed for domestic use, but a commercial version is planned that opens up the possibility of restaurant kitchens automating even highly technically challenging tasks.

它面向家用，但公司计划推出商用版本，这意味着餐厅厨房甚至有望将技术难度很高的任务也实现自动化。

I

For now, the kitchen technology that QSR Automations supplies to Mitchells & Butlers' pubs or KFC is less glamorous.

目前，QSR Automations公司为米其尔与巴特勒旗下的酒吧或肯德基提供的厨房技术就没那么光鲜了。

Rather than having a chef shouting out diners' orders, the QSR system automatically distributes those orders across screens.

QSR系统不需要厨师高声喊出食客的点单，而是自动把这些订单分发到各个屏幕上。

The kitchen screens then tell chefs when to start cooking each dish, so that your plate of fish, chips and peas comes together at the right time.

厨房屏幕随后会提示厨师何时开始烹饪每道菜，好让你那盘炸鱼、薯条和青豆能在恰当的时间一同上桌。

J

The restaurant industry is currently suffering a severe shortage of skilled chefs — a deficit of 11,000, according to the skills agency People 1st.

餐饮业目前正面临技术娴熟厨师严重短缺的问题——据技能机构 People 1st 称，缺口达 1.1 万名。

"Automation systems designed to make the process more idiot-proof are trying to bridge that gap," says Stefan Chomka, the editor of Restaurant magazine.

"旨在让整个流程更加'傻瓜化'的自动化系统正在试图填补这一缺口，"《餐厅》杂志主编斯特凡·乔姆卡说。

K

In terms of simplifying or enhancing the customer experience, online restaurant booking has been the easily-won success of the internet age.

就简化或提升顾客体验而言，网上订餐早已是互联网时代唾手可得的成功。

Automated at-table ordering and payment is seen as the next landmark.

餐桌自助点餐与自动支付被视为下一个里程碑。

Until then, early adopters, such as the Manchester bar owner Andy Smith, who uses the Ordoo app at his venue 33 Oldham Street, may well experience growing but "lower than expected" acceptance.

在此之前，像曼彻斯特酒吧老板安迪·史密斯这样的早期采用者——他在自己的店铺 33 Oldham Street 使用 Ordoo 应用——很可能面临接受度不断增长却"低于预期"的局面。

L

Embarrassing failures in restaurant tech frequently hit the headlines.

餐饮科技领域令人尴尬的失败屡屡登上新闻头条。

The TGI Fridays drone that injured a Brooklyn Daily photographer; US burger chain Shake Shack having to retreat from plans for entirely cashless ordering sites.

TGI Fridays 的无人机弄伤了《布鲁克林日报》的一名摄影师；美国汉堡连锁品牌 Shake Shack 也不得不放弃全面推行无现金点餐门店的计划。

But, says Chomka, such technology could miss the mark if it only offers "solutions to problems that don't really exist".

但乔姆卡说，如果这类技术只是提供"解决那些并不真正存在的问题的方案"，那它就可能偏离方向。

"Payments being automatic" could be useful, he adds.

"自动付款"可能会有用，他补充道。

But, in an expensive restaurant, "how quick do you need dinner to be?"

但在高档餐厅，"你需要晚餐上得多快呢？"

M

That attachment to the human art of hospitality will be heartening for staff who see automation as a threat to jobs.

对人性化待客之道的这份执着，会让那些将自动化视为就业威胁的员工感到欣慰。

"Unite is concerned that without safeguards, any benefits will bypass workers in low-paying jobs, such as waiters," says the union's London regional officer, Dave Turnbull.

"Unite 联合工会担心，如果没有保障措施，任何好处都不会惠及服务员等低薪岗位的劳动者，"该工会伦敦地区官员戴夫·特恩布尔说。

"We're urging Members of Parliament to ensure automation does not just benefit a wealthy few like previous industrial revolutions."

我们正敦促议会成员确保自动化不会像以往的工业革命一样，仅仅让少数富人受益。

N

The industry principle is that automation will allow it to redeploy staff more effectively.

该行业的原则是，自动化将使其能够更有效地重新调配员工。

Jobs will change, if not immediately decrease in number.

工作岗位将会改变，即便其数量不会立即减少。

"Self-order screens mean more demand for people front-of-house. Last year, we recruited 1,000 new managers,"

"自助点餐屏幕意味着对前厅人员的需求增加。去年，我们新招募了1000名经理，"

says McDonald's COO, Jason Clark.

麦当劳首席运营官贾森·克拉克说道。

Caliburger has a programme to retrain staff as robot engineers or "chef techs".

Caliburger有一个将员工再培训为机器人工程师或"厨师技师"的项目。

O

But ultimately the chance to reduce overheads by employing fewer people or at lower rates will be what attracts big brands to technology.

但归根结底，通过雇佣更少的人或以更低的薪酬来降低运营成本的机会，才是吸引大品牌采用技术的真正原因。

Invariably, over the next 10 years, jobs will go in conventional bricks-built restaurants, not least because automation will boost their rapidly growing rival: delivery.

未来十年间，传统砖砌餐厅的岗位无疑将会减少，尤其是因为自动化会助推其迅速发展的竞争对手——外卖配送。

Companies such as Domino and Just Eat have both experimented with robot and drone delivery, which is already legal in Reykjavik, Iceland.

Domino和Just Eat等公司都已尝试机器人和无人机配送，这种配送方式在冰岛雷克雅未克已经合法。

"Robot food deliveries are the future," says Noel Sharkey.

诺尔·沙基说道："机器人送餐是未来的趋势。"

"Small, 4 mph ground robots are safe and will work 24 hours a day."

"时速4英里的小型地面机器人安全可靠，可以一天24小时工作。"

P

Combined with machine learning of anticipated order volumes and the minute-by-minute cooking data generated by systems such as QSR, that robot fleet could deliver almost instantly.

再加上对预计订单量的机器学习，以及QSR等系统生成的逐分钟烹饪数据，这个机器人车队几乎可以实现即时配送。

"Restaurants will prepare food ahead of orders coming in," predicts Just Eat's director of engineering, Daniel Richardson.

“餐厅会提前备好餐食，等订单一到就直接出餐，”Just Eat 工程总监丹尼尔·理查森预测道。

"I'll be able to say 'Let's have pizza' to my family, my intelligent assistant will hear and 10 minutes later the food will be at my door."

“我只需对家人说一句‘我们吃披萨吧’，我的智能助手就会听到，十分钟后餐食便会送到家门口。”

No one is putting an exact date on it yet.

目前还没有人给出确切的时间表。

But in the dining room and on your doorstep, the robots are coming.

但在餐厅和你家门口，机器人已经悄然而至。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1448 词 · 29 句

P1

Many see friendships as a comfort blanket: a shoulder to cry on, a reliable soul to confide in.

许多人把友谊看作一张安慰毯：一个可以依靠哭泣的肩膀，一个可以倾诉心声的可靠灵魂。

However, more often than not, in our eagerness to seek individual success, we lose friends along the way and that has become part of the process.

然而，我们往往在急于追求个人成功的过程中，在途中失去了朋友，而这已成为这一过程的一部分。

P2

The chaos and never-ending turmoil that accompanies everyday life is an aggressive obstacle in the pursuit of stable friendships.

日常生活中的混乱与永无休止的动荡，是追求稳定友谊的严重障碍。

As students, we use this excuse to shrug off losing people who we once thought to be loved ones.

作为学生，我们用这种借口来对失去曾经以为是至亲至爱的人不以为然。

P3

Instead of viewing our lives as a race, where everyone is on their own path to some form of success, we must relearn to value the connections we make along the track.

我们不应把生活看作一场竞赛，人人都在各自的道路上奔向某种成功，而必须重新学会珍视我们在途中建立的种种联系。

We must reevaluate the harm of radical, individual freedom.

我们必须重新审视激进的个人自由所带来的危害。

P4

Radical individualism elevates the freedom of the individual over the collective.

激进的个人主义将个人自由置于集体之上。

It argues, for instance, that universal healthcare, an example of collectivism, threatened the dream of many Americans.

例如，它认为全民医疗——一个集体主义的例子——威胁了许多美国人的梦想。

P5

Many of us have fallen victim to an individualistic culture.

我们很多人都已成为个人主义文化的受害者。

Society tends to celebrate personal achievement and commend it based on the assumption that winning or succeeding is a consequence of working harder than the next person.

社会往往颂扬个人成就并加以赞赏，其依据是这样一个假设：获胜或成功是比别人更加努力工作的结果。

P6

As a society, we idolize the concept of "losing friends on the road to success".

作为一个社会，我们把“在通往成功的路上失去朋友”这一观念奉为理想。

I remember hearing in high school, "Don't worry about losing friendships, just focus on study and getting into college. That's all that will matter in the long run,"

我记得上高中时听过这样的话：“别担心失去友谊，只管专心学习、考上大学。从长远来看，那才是唯一重要的事。”

a sentiment that is not unique to my experience.

这一感受并非我的独有经历。

It's preached in many success stories, and it's embedded in the way we see growth.

它被反复宣讲于众多成功故事之中，并深深嵌入我们看待成长的方式。

Motivational speakers will push their audience to be complacent to the inevitable end of losing friends.

励志演讲者会鼓动听众接受一个必然的结局——失去朋友。

Spend too much time with friends?

和朋友相处太久？

You're losing time better utilized on your achievements.

你这是在浪费本可用于成就自我的时间。

Invest time in your relationships?

把时间投入到人际关系中？

Invest time in your work instead.

还不如把时间投入工作。

P7

This constant need to succeed has blinded us from seeing through the whirlwind of change that accompanies our everyday lives.

这种对成功的持续追求，使我们对伴随日常生活而来的种种变迁视而不见。

We are expected to tangibly succeed, earn awards, get higher positions, earn better grades.

我们被期望取得实实在在的成功、获得奖项、登上更高的职位、取得更好的成绩。

In order to succeed, we lose friends, and that has sadly become normalized.

为了成功，我们失去了朋友，而令人悲哀的是，这一切已被视为常态。

P8

We cannot be complacent with losing friendships because they are our anchors.

我们不能对失去友谊听之任之，因为朋友是我们的依靠。

Yes, pursue success.

是的，去追求成功吧。

Challenge yourself.

挑战自我。

Push limits.

突破极限。

But contrary to all the self-proclaimed success experts, doing it alone does not make you more successful or any better than someone who achieved it with the help of others.

但与所有自诩为成功专家的人所宣扬的相反，独自奋斗并不能让你更成功，也不能让你比那些在他人帮助下取得成功的人更优秀。

P9

Put effort into fostering healthy relationships, and maintaining them through your busy schedule that seems like it leaves no time for anyone else.

要用心去培育健康的人际关系，即便你的日程安排看似忙得没有时间陪伴任何人，也要努力维系这些关系。

Although we may be living in a society that treasures the independent pursuit of achievement, having people to support and love you on the way is far more important than any end result.

尽管我们所处的社会崇尚独立追求成就，但在这条路上有人支持你、爱你，远比任何最终成果都更加重要。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2455 词 · 24 句

P1

During his acceptance speech for the Nobel Peace Prize, Martin Luther King Jr. said, "I believe that unarmed truth and unconditional love will have the final word in reality."

在诺贝尔和平奖颁奖致辞中，马丁·路德·金说：“我相信，手无寸铁的真理和无条件的爱终将在现实中胜出。”

P2

More than five decades later, our society is still struggling to separate truth from fantasy, fact from fiction.

半个多世纪过去了，我们的社会仍在艰难地分辨真伪、辨别虚实。

From disputes over the climate change to debates about how slavery is taught, America is at a crossroads.

从关于气候变化的争议到关于奴隶制应如何教授的辩论，美国正处在十字路口。

And the battle for equity is at the heart of these vital conversations.

而争取公平的斗争，正是这些至关重要对话的核心所在。

P3

Study after study shows that inequity has had appalling consequences.

一项又一项研究表明，不公平现象已造成触目惊心的后果。

A 2020 report by Citi indicated that "Black students could have increased their lifetime incomes $90 – $113 billion" if equity issues related to higher education had been adequately addressed over the past two decades.

花旗集团2020年的一份报告指出，如果在过去二十年里高等教育领域相关的公平问题得到妥善解决，“黑人学生的终身收入本可增加900亿至1130亿美元”。

P4

This statistic represents a flood of issues we've failed to confront as a nation.

这一统计数据背后，是我们作为一个国家始终未能正视的大量问题。

It also highlights one truth: We're missing out on valuable opportunities to bridge the differences that divide us.

它也揭示了一个事实：我们正在错失弥合分歧、消除隔阂的宝贵机会。

However, by honoring King's legacy, we can get our country back on the path toward true equity.

然而，只要我们铭记金博士的精神遗产，就能让国家重新走上通往真正公平的道路。

P5

Students — and adults for that matter — simply cannot understand the conflicts we have today without understanding what caused them in the first place.

学生——其实成年人也不例外——如果不了解当今冲突的根源，就根本无法理解我们今天面临的矛盾。

That means we have a responsibility to help them make connections between US history and current events.

这意味着我们有责任帮助他们建立美国历史与当下时事之间的联系。

It also means giving them the tools they need to address inequity and bias in their own lives.

这也意味着要赋予他们必要的工具，让他们能够在自己的生活中应对不公平与偏见。

Adherence to these principles, along with a commitment to truthful dialogue, can help every student better appreciate how far we've come and more clearly see the roads we have yet to travel.

坚守这些原则，并秉持真诚对话的承诺，能帮助每一位学生更好地体会我们已经走了多远，也更清晰地看清我们尚需走完的道路。

P6

With more truthful analysis of the need for more qualified, diverse educators, we can improve our recruiting, training, and retention strategies for schools across the country.

只有更诚实地分析我们对更多高素质、多元化教育工作者的需求，我们才能改进全国各地学校的招聘、培训和留任策略。

P7

Unfortunately, our deeply rooted divisions are delaying these advancements.

遗憾的是，根深蒂固的分歧正延缓着这些进步。

A recent survey found that 77 percent of Americans believe the nation is "more divided than ever before".

最近的一项调查发现，77%的美国人认为这个国家"比以往任何时候都更加分裂"。

But reports of increased polarization shouldn't persuade us to look away from one another.

但是，关于极化加剧的种种报道，不应让我们转过头去、彼此回避。

Instead, they should compel us to address our shared issues head-on.

相反，这些报道应促使我们直面共同的问题。

P8

We are not, as some would suggest, living in a "post-racial society".

我们并没有像一些人所声称的那样，生活在一个"后种族社会"之中。

We simply cannot afford to keep our heads buried in the sand when it comes to racial inequity.

面对种族不平等，我们根本承担不起继续把头埋进沙子的代价。

Unless we get honest about intolerance, bias, and how these social diseases impact the ways we treat one another, injustice will continue to haunt us.

除非我们诚实地正视不宽容与偏见，正视这些社会顽疾如何影响我们彼此相待的方式，否则不公将继续纠缠着我们。

But our children and our grandchildren deserve better.

但我们的子孙后代理应得到更好的未来。

P9

On this Martin Luther King Day and beyond, let's commit to working together to disrupt the status quo.

在这个马丁·路德·金纪念日以及今后的日子里，让我们承诺携手合作，打破现状。

Let's continue to build a better, more inclusive nation; and in the words of Martin Luther King Jr., "A society at peace with itself, a society that can live with its conscience."

让我们继续建设一个更美好、更具包容性的国家；用马丁·路德·金的话说，"一个与自身和平共处的社会，一个能与良知相伴而存的社会。"

第 2 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,388 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）302 词 · 15 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) accompanyingB) cognitiveC) domainsD) entertainingE) evokingF) hybridG) impetusH) intuitiveI) onceJ) overseeK) redundantL) stancesM) surpassN) tentativelyO) vanish

We humans love to believe we are special, but science and technology have repeatedly proved this conviction wrong.

我们人类总爱自认为与众不同，但科学和技术已一再证明这种信念是错误的。

People once thought that humans were the only animals to use tools, to form teams or to propagate culture, but science has shown that other animals do each of these things.

人们曾认为只有人类才会使用工具、组建团队或传承文化，但科学已经表明，其他动物同样具备这些能力。

Meanwhile, technology has quashed, one by one, claims that cognitive tasks require a human brain.

与此同时，技术也逐一击破了那些声称认知任务必须由人脑来完成的观点。

The first adding machine was invented in 1623.

第一台加法机发明于1623年。

This past year, a computer-generated work won an art contest.

去年，一幅由计算机生成的作品在一场艺术比赛中获奖。

I believe the moment when computers meet and surpass human intelligence is on the horizon.

我认为，计算机达到并超越人类智能的时刻已为期不远。

How will human intelligence and creativity be valued when machines become smarter and more creative than the brightest people?

当机器变得比最聪明的人更聪明、更有创造力时，人类的智慧和创造力又将如何被衡量和珍视？

There will likely be a spectrum.

届时很可能会出现多种情况并存的局面。

In some domains, people still value humans doing things, even if a computer can do it better.

在某些领域，人们仍然看重由人来做事，即使计算机做得更好也不例外。

It's been a quarter of a century since a computer beat world champion Garry Kasparov, but human chess hasn't gone away.

距离计算机击败世界冠军加里·卡斯帕罗夫已经过去了四分之一个世纪，但人类的国际象棋并没有因此消亡。

In other fields, human skill will seem costly and redundant.

在其他领域，人类的技能将显得代价高昂且多余。

Take illustration for example: most readers don't care whether the graphic accompanying a magazine article was drawn by a person or a computer — they just want it to be relevant, new and perhaps entertaining.

以插画为例：大多数读者并不在意杂志文章配图是人画的还是计算机画的——他们只希望配图切题、新颖，或许还能带来一些趣味。

And, of course, this question isn't black or white.

而且，这个问题当然并非非黑即白。

Many fields will be a hybrid, where some humans find a lucky place, but most of the work is done by computers, like in manufacturing today, where much of the work is accomplished by robots, but some humans must be there to oversee the machines.

许多领域将呈现人机混合的模式——少数人能在其中找到一席之地，但大部分工作由计算机完成，就像今天的制造业一样，大量工作由机器人完成，但仍需一些人在场监督机器运转。

If history is any guide, it's almost certain that advances in AI will cause more jobs to vanish, that creative workers with human-only skills will become richer but fewer in number, and that those who own creative technology will become the new ultra-rich.

如果以史为鉴，几乎可以肯定，人工智能的进步将导致更多工作岗位消失，拥有仅人类才具备技能的创意工作者会变得更富有但人数更少，而掌握创意技术的人将成为新的超级富豪。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1203 词 · 62 句

A

Projects, relationships, and jobs don't always work out as planned — and it's not always clear when to give up.

项目、人际关系和工作并不总会按计划发展——而什么时候该放弃，也往往并不明朗。

We've all repeatedly heard the saying that "quitters never win", but sometimes the best decision is to cut our losses.

我们都反复听过“半途而废者永远赢不了”这句老话，但有时候，最明智的决定恰恰是及时止损。

B

Think about that relationship you held on to even after it had run its course or the job you stayed in despite your boss making you miserable.

想想那段早已走到尽头你却迟迟不肯放手的感情，或者那份让你在上司手下备受煎熬却依然固守的工作。

Many of us try to hold onto the last ray of hope, thinking we can make it work, whatever "it" is.

我们许多人都会死死抓住最后一丝希望不放，觉得自己能让事情成——不管这个“事情”究竟是什么。

And while perseverance is generally an excellent quality, it may also incline you to over-invest in something that is no longer a good idea.

诚然，毅力通常是一种极可贵的品质，但它也可能让你在早已不再明智的事情上投入过头。

C

As an executive coach, I work with numerous passionate and hard-working leaders who don't want to "quit".

作为一名高管教练，我接触过许多充满激情、勤奋努力的领导者，他们都不愿意“认输”。

But the smartest leaders learn to discern the difference between quitting too soon and holding onto something that they shouldn't.

然而，最聪明的领导者懂得分辨何时是过早放弃，何时又是在不该坚持的事情上执迷不悟。

D

We are all susceptible to the sunk-cost delusion, an unconscious bias that leads us to persist in an endeavor we have already invested time, effort, or money into — even when abandoning it would be more beneficial.

我们都会受到沉没成本谬误的影响——这是一种无意识的偏见，驱使我们继续坚持一项已经投入了时间、精力或金钱的事业，哪怕放弃它反而更有利。

We realize that we can't get back the resources we've given, so we persevere — investing in a project that should be halted, staying in a relationship that should be over, or finishing a book we no longer enjoy and should put down, because we're already halfway through it.

我们意识到，已经付出的资源无法收回，于是便选择坚持——继续投入一个本应叫停的项目，守着一段本应结束的感情，或硬着头皮读完一本早已索然无味、本该放下的书，只因为已经读了一半。

If quitting is "not your thing", you may be especially susceptible to this bias.

如果“半途而废”从来不是你的作风，你可能尤其容易受到这种偏见的影响。

E

The sunk-cost delusion causes us to overly worry about what we'll lose if we move on and not think enough about the costs of not moving on.

沉没成本谬误让我们过分忧虑一旦向前走会失去什么，却没有充分考虑不向前走的代价。

To offset this bias, deliberately refocus your thinking on the gains so you can more objectively weigh the alternatives.

要抵消这种偏见，请刻意把思路重新聚焦于收益上，从而更客观地权衡各种选择。

Ask yourself: What might I gain by cutting my losses now?

问问自己：现在及时止损，我可能会得到什么？

Will you be happier or have time for another (better) opportunity?

你会更快乐，还是会有时间去抓住另一个（更好的）机会？

Then ask yourself: What will it cost me to soldier on?

然后再问问自己：继续硬撑下去，我会付出什么代价？

For example, is it possible that you'll be throwing more good money after what you've already lost?

比如，你是否可能在已经损失的基础上，继续把更多的钱投进去？

Or that you won't have the energy to capitalize on other promising possibilities?

又或者，你是否会因为继续坚持，而没有精力去把握其他有前景的机会？

F

The illusion of control can also interfere with our best judgment, leading us to overestimate our ability to control events and attain a positive outcome.

控制错觉也会干扰我们的最佳判断，使我们高估自己控制事件、获得积极结果的能力。

This bias gives us a sense of agency and can promote mental health.

这种偏差给我们一种自主感，有助于促进心理健康。

However, the bias can also result in escalating commitment to a losing course of action.

然而，这种偏差也可能导致我们对失败的行动方针不断加深投入。

Especially when combined with an "I'm not a quitter" mentality, feeling like we have more control over an event or person than we do puts us at risk of doubling down when we should pull out.

尤其是当这种偏差与'我不是半途而废的人'的心态结合在一起时，感觉自己对某件事或某个人的控制力比实际更大，会让我们冒着应该退出时却加倍下注的风险。

G

To counteract this bias, consider your situation and make a simple two-column list of what's in your control and what's not.

要抵消这种偏差，不妨审视一下自己的处境，简单列出两栏：哪些是我能控制的，哪些是我不能控制的。

Think rigorously.

请认真地思考。

While you may be able to influence other people and various circumstances, you can't force them to change or go your way.

虽然你或许能影响他人和各种情况，但你无法强迫他们改变或按你的意愿行事。

H

Getting clear on what you can control and what you can't is essential to making a quality decision about whether to call it quits or persevere.

厘清自己能控制什么、不能控制什么，对于明智地决定是放弃还是坚持，至关重要。

With a written list in hand, you can ensure you focus on what you can control and better assess whether your continued efforts are worth the expense.

有了这份书面清单，你就能确保自己专注于可控之事，从而更好地评估继续付出的努力是否值得。

Expending effort and emotion on things you can't control can be both draining and disempowering.

在无法控制的事情上耗费精力和情感，既会令人疲惫，也会让人感到无能为力。

I

Research indicates that people link their self-identity and social status to their commitments.

研究表明，人们会将自我认同和社会地位与自己所承担的事务联系在一起。

And because we identify with our commitments, withdrawing from one can feel like a threat to our identity or status.

而且，由于我们与自己所承担的事务息息相关，退出其中一项可能会让人觉得是对自身身份或地位的威胁。

J

Take my client Ryan, a leader at a technology company.

以我的客户瑞安为例，她是一家科技公司的领导者。

Ryan had invested two years and a lot of effort in a design project that wasn't delivering the needed outcomes, but the thought of abandoning it after all she had put in was hard to bear.

瑞安已经在一个设计项目上投入了两年时间和大量精力，但该项目始终未能取得预期成果；然而，想到自己付出了这么多却要半途而废，她实在难以接受。

As Ryan and I explored whether she should cancel the initiative she'd been leading or continue to persevere, she stated, "The thing is, I'm not a quitter."

在瑞安与我探讨她究竟是应该取消自己一直主导的这个项目，还是继续坚持下去时，她说："问题是，我不是一个轻言放弃的人。"

This singular, fixed sense of self was clouding her judgment and limiting her choices for action.

这种单一而僵化的自我认知蒙蔽了她的判断力，也限制了她可选择的行动方案。

K

Our self-concept and identity are mental concepts that influence our behavior.

自我概念和身份认同是影响我们行为的心理观念。

If you strongly identify as not being a quitter, you can end up staying stuck in a course of action due to your narrow and rigid self-concept.

如果你强烈地认为自己不是一个轻言放弃的人，那么你可能会因为自我概念的狭隘与僵化，而困在既有的行动路线中无法自拔。

To overcome this kind of internal obstacle, recall your other positive traits and personality characteristics.

要克服这类内在障碍，请回想自己其他的积极特质和性格特点。

Ask yourself: What additional strengths do I have (or aspire to have) that would be helpful to employ here?

问一问自己：我还有哪些额外的优势（或渴望具备的优势）能在这里派上用场？

For example, when I asked Ryan what other positive qualities from her personality she needed to leverage for decision making, she replied, "Discernment and bravery."

例如，当我问瑞安，在她的性格中还有哪些积极品质可以运用到决策中时，她回答说：“明辨与勇气。”

L

Now, examine your situation through the lens of each of these facets of yourself.

现在，通过自身这些不同的侧面来审视你的处境。

What does the discerning part of you say?

你内心那个明辨的部分会怎么说？

The brave part?

那个勇敢的部分呢？

Considering your decision through these different characteristics will enable you to see that you have more choices.

从这些不同特质的角度来思考你的决定，能让你看到自己拥有更多选择。

It's also helpful to seek external sources of information, rather than relying solely on your thinking and instincts.

寻求外部信息源也很有帮助，而不是仅仅依赖自己的思考和直觉。

But it's important to think through whose opinions you solicit.

不过，重要的是要想清楚你该听取哪些人的意见。

M

Ideally, seek out people who are less invested in your decision than you are.

理想情况下，去找那些对你的决定投入程度不如你的人。

If you're considering whether to shut down your entrepreneurial venture, for instance, reach out to other founders as opposed to just your business partner.

比如，如果你正在考虑是否关闭自己的创业项目，就应该向其他创始人求助，而不只是找你的商业伙伴。

Similarly, if you're looking for love advice, ask someone removed from the ups and downs of your relationship instead of relying only on your best friend.

同样，如果你在寻求恋爱方面的建议，可以去问一个与你的感情起伏无关的人，而不是只依赖你最好的朋友。

In all cases, I recommend describing your situation and following up with a question like: "How would you manage this situation?"

在任何情况下，我都建议你先描述自己的处境，然后接着问一个问题，比如：“换作你会如何处理这种情况？”

N

Alternately, you could also seek out hard data that illuminates your real chance of success in whatever endeavor you are contemplating.

或者，你也可以寻找翔实的数据，来说明你在所考虑的任何事业上真正成功的机会有多大。

Sticking with the example of the entrepreneurial venture, numbers are your best friend.

仍以创业为例，数据是你最好的朋友。

Use them to remove yourself from the love you have for this idea and consider your burn rate relative to growth plan, industry growth rates, and other factors that predict start-up success.

借助数据，把你对这个想法的热爱放到一边，冷静审视你的资金消耗速度相对于增长计划、行业增长率以及其他预示创业成功因素的关系。

External sources of information can help you see angles and possibilities you might not have considered.

外部信息来源能帮助你看到自己可能未曾考虑过的角度和可能性。

O

When something that seemed so promising at the start doesn't turn out the way we imagined, we can become overly self-critical.

当一件起初看似大有希望的事情最终没有如我们所料时，我们可能会变得过度自我批评。

Nobody likes to feel that they've made a poor decision, especially if making intelligent decisions is a quality you hold in high esteem.

没有人愿意觉得自己做了一个糟糕的决定，尤其是当你极为看重自己具备明智决策能力的时候。

The most effective strategy here is to give yourself a break.

在这种情况下，最有效的策略是放过自己。

Practicing self-compassion increases your resilience, emotional intelligence, compassion toward others, and can boost your performance as well as help you develop a growth mindset.

践行自我关怀能增强你的韧性、情商和对他人的同理心，还能提升你的表现，并帮助你培养成长型思维。

P

Instead of deliberating and criticizing yourself, focus instead on what you can learn from this situation.

与其反复琢磨、自我苛责，不如把注意力放在你能从这件事中学到什么上。

For example, Ryan recognized she had developed various new skills and leadership capabilities from leading the design project.

例如，瑞安意识到，她在领导设计项目的过程中习得了各种新技能和领导能力。

And despite ultimately deciding to drop it, she was still happy she had tried and grateful for what the experience taught her.

尽管最终决定放弃这个项目，她仍然为自己曾经尝试过而感到欣慰，并对这段经历所教给她的一切心怀感激。

Q

Undeniably, perseverance can help us succeed.

不可否认，坚持不懈能帮助我们取得成功。

But for your well-being, it's important to learn how to distinguish quitting too soon and clinging to a losing course of action.

但为了你的幸福，学会区分过早放弃与死守一条注定失败的路，这一点很重要。

While letting go can be difficult, it will free up your time, energy, and mental space to imagine new possibilities and pursue new opportunities.

放手虽然不易，却能释放出你的时间、精力和心智空间，让你去构想新的可能、追寻新的机会。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1443 词 · 25 句

P1

As interdependent beings we cannot thrive independent of others.

作为相互依存的个体，我们离不开他人而独自茁壮成长。

We like to work with people who are like-minded, with whom we share a common interest, or who also believe in a cause we are serious about.

我们喜欢与志同道合的人合作，与有共同兴趣的人共事，或与同样认同我们所认真追求之事业的人携手。

This is evident in how we have organized ourselves into various collective groups such as nations, regions, communities, organizations, and families.

这一点在我们如何将自身组织成各种集体群体——如国家、地区、社区、组织和家庭——中显而易见。

P2

Does the group you belong to have a strong collective consciousness or shared beliefs?

你所属的群体是否有强烈的集体意识或共同信念？

Is there interdependence among its members?

其成员之间是否存在相互依存的关系？

Do you consider this group a significant part of your existence?

你是否认为这个群体是你存在中的重要组成部分？

If your answers are affirmative, then you are experiencing a micro version of solidarity.

如果你的回答是肯定的，那么你正在体验一种微观层面的团结。

P3

What is solidarity?

什么是团结？

Its simplest definition in the dictionary is: "... a unifying opinion, feeling, purpose or interest among a group of people."

词典中最简单的定义是："……一群人之间一致的意见、情感、目标或利益。

P4

In this increasingly diverse society we live in, solidarity takes on a complex and multifaceted character as it forms bonds of interdependence among unique individuals living in a fast-changing environment and working within a complex division of labor.

在我们所处的这个日益多元的社会中，团结呈现出复杂而多面的特征，因为它在快速变化的环境中、在复杂分工的协作下，把一个个独特的个体联结成相互依存的纽带。

P5

One facet of solidarity fits well with the principles of communitarianism.

团结的一个侧面与社群主义的原则十分契合。

In this setting, individuals give up their own interests to serve the common good.

在这种情境下，个体舍弃自身利益，以服务于共同的福祉。

However, its strict adherence to social cohesiveness and exclusiveness restricts expression and practice of individual differences and preferences.

然而，它对社会凝聚力与排他性的严格坚守，限制了个体差异和个人偏好的表达与实践。

Consequently, individuality and autonomy are sacrificed to maintain the unity of the group.

因此，为了维护群体的统一，个性与自主性被牺牲了。

P6

Solidarity is also viewed as a reaction to the extreme emphasis on individualism and freedom of choice.

团结也被视为是对极端强调个人主义和选择自由的一种反应。

It goes against the liberal idea of unrestricted individuals who see society as a hindrance to their freedom to make choices and to act according to what is good for them regardless of how these will affect others.

它与自由主义理念相违背；在该理念下，个人是不受约束的，他们把社会视为对其自由选择和按自身利益行事的阻碍，而不管这些行为会如何影响他人。

P7

But despite its many facets, social theorists have viewed solidarity as a key factor in bringing about order and progress in society.

然而，尽管团结具有多重面向，社会理论家们仍将其视为推动社会秩序与进步的关键因素。

They believe that for human society to survive as it becomes more diverse, more new bonds need to be formed based on the differences.

他们认为，随着人类社会日益多元化，为了生存，需要在差异的基础上形成更多新的纽带。

In the light of this, they regard a genuine solidarity as more than just shallow relationships based on common interests.

基于此，他们认为真正的团结不仅仅是以共同利益为基础的肤浅关系。

Solidarity is not just a product of strategic calculations of how much it will benefit the self.

团结并非只是经过精打细算、衡量自我能从中获益多少的产物。

It is not an exclusive class founded on the you-and-me-against-the-world principle.

它也不是建立在“你我联合对抗世界”原则之上的排他性圈子。

Rather, it is inclusive and is not forced.

相反，它是包容的，且不是被强迫的。

Acting out of solidarity means supporting and standing up for each other — in recognition that one's fate is in the fate of the other.

出于团结而行动，意味着彼此支持、相互声援——承认一方的命运就存在于另一方的命运之中。

It is a bond characterized by cooperative practices and strengthened by the concern for the well-being of others or the common good.

这是一种以合作实践为特征、并因对他人福祉或公共利益的关切而得到加强的纽带。

It fights for the protection of human dignity and listens to and acts on the cries of the most vulnerable.

它为捍卫人的尊严而奋斗，倾听最弱势群体的呼声并付诸行动。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2440 词 · 18 句

P1

People who repeatedly give unwanted advice can be well-meaning and genuinely want to help.

那些一再给出不请自来的建议的人，可能是出于好意，真心想帮忙。

Others might do this out of a sense of self-worth around the ability to influence others.

另一些人这样做，则是出于一种自我价值感——这种价值感建立在能够影响他人的能力之上。

One study found that people with a high tendency to seek power were more likely to give advice than those with an opposite tendency.

一项研究发现，权力寻求倾向高的人比倾向相反的人更可能给出建议。

P2

Under the guise of unselfishness, people may be driven to give you unwanted advice because it makes them feel powerful or in control.

在无私的幌子下，人们之所以被驱使着向你提出不请自来的建议，是因为这样做让他们感到自己有权力或能掌控局面。

They may not be fully aware of this drive, however.

然而，他们自己可能并未充分意识到这种驱动力。

P3

These people may also display a problematic degree of emotional vulnerability, becoming upset very quickly, and taking a long time to calm down.

这些人还可能表现出程度堪忧的情绪脆弱性：很快就变得心烦意乱，而且需要很长时间才能平复。

It is possible that their emotions were only validated in childhood when they were at their loudest, encouraging them to adopt responses to discomfort that are excessive in most situations.

很可能他们的情绪只有在童年时期声嘶力竭时才得到认可，这促使他们形成了一种应对不适的方式，而在大多数情境下这种反应都显得过度。

P4

When someone is giving advice in order to make themselves feel more powerful, there is underlying anxiety to their behavior that recipients of the advice tend to notice.

当一个人给出建议是为了让自己感觉更有力量时，其行为背后潜藏着焦虑，而接受建议的人往往也能察觉到。

It can be tempting in this situation to react harshly to the advice giver and to accuse them of being manipulative, but this approach might produce an undesired result.

在这种情况下，人们很容易冲动地严厉回应给出建议的人，指责他们居心叵测、操纵他人，但这种做法可能会带来事与愿违的结果。

P5

If the act of giving advice is contributing actively to someone's feelings of self-worth, an outright rejection may be perceived as a threat, activating their fight-or-flight response, possibly causing them to double down on their validation-seeking behavior, or leading to a larger conflict.

如果给出建议这一行为本身就在主动支撑某人的自我价值感，那么断然拒绝可能会被其视为一种威胁，从而触发他们的战斗或逃跑反应，可能促使他们变本加厉地寻求认可，或引发更大的冲突。

P6

The key is to validate without over-identifying.

关键在于给予认可，但不要过度认同。

You can let them know that you have heard them and appreciate where they are coming from without taking on the potentially damaging narrative that you could not have gotten by without their help.

你可以让他们知道，你听到了他们的话，也理解他们为何这样说，但不必接受那种可能有害的说法——即没有他们的帮助你就做不到。

You had better say something like, "Thanks for the idea. I have my own plan for handling this, but I really appreciate your perspective and will take it into consideration. Can I let you know when I need help in the future?"

你最好这样说："谢谢你的建议。这件事我自己有处理方案，但我很感激你的看法，会把它纳入考虑。以后我需要帮助时再向你求助，好吗？"

P7

If you have trouble setting boundaries tactfully, prioritize working on your own ability to self-regulate.

如果你在巧妙地设定界限方面有困难，那就优先提升自我调节的能力。

As uncomfortable as it may be to continuously receive, if you can respond with compassion, the situation will likely defuse much faster.

尽管不断地接受（建议）可能让人感到不适，但如果你能以善意回应，局面往往会更快地缓和下来。

P8

Though it is usually not the intended outcome of giving unsolicited advice, many who receive it often feel stressed, offended, or simply annoyed by unwanted suggestions.

虽然给出未经请求的建议通常并非出于让人不快的目的，但许多收到建议的人往往会因此感到压力、被冒犯，或单纯因不想要的建议而感到恼火。

Setting a boundary in this regard, if you feel you need one, is perfectly reasonable and something that can bring you increased emotional safety.

如果你觉得有必要，在这方面设定一个界限是完全合情合理的，而且这能给你带来更多的情感安全感。

P9

It is best to set boundaries in a way that takes into account the individual's underlying reasons for doing what they are doing to avoid unnecessary conflict and more stress.

设定界限时，最好充分考虑对方采取这些行为的深层原因，以避免不必要的冲突和更大的压力。

第 3 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,421 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）294 词 · 11 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) affixB) correlateC) deviateD) domainsE) influentialF) inherentlyG) laymenH) multipleI) propertiesJ) realityK) reciprocallyL) specimenM) spectrumN) synonymO) unanimous

It's best to think of an accent as a distinct, systematic and rule-governed way of speaking.

最好将口音理解为一种独特、系统且受规则支配的说话方式。

Accent is not a synonym for dialect, but it's related.

口音并不是方言的同义词，但两者之间存在关联。

Dialect is an umbrella term for the way a community pronounces words, creates words, and orders words; accent refers to that first part only.

方言是一个总称，涵盖一个社群的发音方式、造词方式和词序安排；而口音仅指其中的发音部分。

Many laymen presume that there is a single standard way of speaking in each country, with one single standard accent, and that this perceived standard is inherently the best form of speech, but linguists often point out that the concept of a standard accent is better understood as an ideal rather than a reality.

许多外行人认为，每个国家都存在一种单一的标准说话方式，只有一种标准口音，而且这种所谓的标准天生就是最佳的语言形式，但语言学家经常指出，标准口音这一概念更应被理解为一种理想，而非现实。

In other words, no one speaks "standard English"; rather, it is an imagined way of using language that exists only in grammar and style books.

换句话说，没有人真正说"标准英语"；它只是一种存在于语法和文体书中的、人们想象出来的语言使用方式。

One reason linguists agree there is no one true standard is that, through the years, there have been multiple supposed standards, depending on time and place.

语言学家一致认为不存在唯一真正的标准，原因之一在于，多年来，所谓的标准一直有多个，取决于时间和地域。

The standard for English in British newsreels from the 1950s, for example, differs greatly from the standard found on American national news broadcasts today.

例如，20世纪50年代英国新闻影片中的英语标准，与今天美国全国新闻广播中的标准大相径庭。

In fact, there has never been a single standard that's been fully agreed upon — and broadcast outlets across the spectrum have never consistently held to those standards anyway.

事实上，从来没有任何一种标准得到过完全一致的认可——而且各类广播媒体也从未始终如一地遵循过这些标准。

Even so, this idea of a standard accent is influential and many workplace and educational environments perpetuate the idea that accents that deviate from the current standard are less appropriate, or even inappropriate.

尽管如此，标准口音的观念依然影响深远，许多职场和教育环境都在不断强化这样一种看法：偏离当前标准的口音不够恰当，甚至不恰当。

Scholars have found that southern US accent features are more accepted in domains such as government, law and service-oriented workplaces, but less accepted in the technology sector.

学者们发现，美国南部口音的特征在政府、法律和服务型职场等领域更易被接受，但在科技行业中则接受度较低。

The acceptability of nonstandard accents may correlate with differences in class and culture, with newer or higher prestige industries expecting more standard speech in the workplace.

非标准口音的可接受度可能与阶层和文化差异相关，较新的或声望较高的行业往往要求职场中使用更标准的言语。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1223 词 · 87 句

A

Timed traffic lights are set according to schedules that are supposed to coordinate with peak traffic hours.

定时交通信号灯依据预设的时间表运行，这些时间表本应与交通高峰时段相协调。

Cities need to monitor and update signal light time schedules at least every two years to keep these systems effective.

各城市需要至少每两年监控并更新一次信号灯时间表，以确保这些系统保持有效。

In Los Angeles, California, an ambitious project to go a step further and synchronize every red light in Los Angeles cost over 400 million dollars.

在加利福尼亚州洛杉矶，一个更进一步的宏大项目——将洛杉矶所有红灯信号同步——耗资超过4亿美元。

Ask a resident of the city and they are likely to agree that saving time on the road is well worth it.

随便问一位当地居民，他们多半会认为节省路上的时间完全值得这笔花费。

If implemented nationwide, synchronization could shave 10% off traffic congestion and 20% off air pollution.

如果在全国范围内推行，信号同步可以减少10%的交通拥堵和20%的空气污染。

Congestion costs time, money, and lives.

交通拥堵浪费时间、耗费金钱，甚至夺走生命。

Big data can help improve how millions of people safely share the road.

大数据可以帮助改善数百万人安全共享道路的方式。

However, none of this would be possible without the traffic light!

然而，如果没有交通信号灯，这一切都不可能实现！

B

The first traffic light was invented in London in the 1860's, but it was hardly recognizable — or effective.

世界上第一盏交通信号灯于19世纪60年代在伦敦发明，但当时的样子几乎让人认不出来，也谈不上有效。

It had been adapted from the railroad signal system by a railway manager, John Peak Knight, in 1868.

1868年，铁路管理员约翰·皮克·奈特将其从铁路信号系统改造而成。

The railroads used red gas lights at night to signal stops and green gas lights during the daytime.

铁路系统在夜间使用红色煤气灯表示停车，白天则使用绿色煤气灯。

Knight installed a similar gas light, semaphore system near London's Westminster Bridge in December of 1868.

1868年12月，奈特在伦敦威斯敏斯特大桥附近安装了一套类似的煤气灯臂板信号系统。

C

The life of the first traffic light signal was short-lived.

世界上第一座交通信号灯的寿命很短。

A gas leak caused one of the signal lights to explode on its police officer operator within a month of being installed.

安装还不到一个月，一次煤气泄漏就导致其中一盏信号灯在执勤警察面前爆炸。

D

Automating traffic signals stalled after the London incident.

伦敦事故之后，交通信号灯的自动化进程一度陷入停滞。

Forty years passed before cars crowding city streets urged the development of traffic lights in the United States.

又过了四十年，城市街道上日益拥堵的汽车才促使美国发展交通信号灯。

London would not see another traffic light until 1929.

此后伦敦直到1929年才再次出现交通信号灯。

E

The United States is a car culture like no other country.

美国是一个独一无二的汽车文化国家。

Unlike most European cities that started urban planning when horses were high-tech, many cities in the United States were designed around urban sprawl, suburban tracts, and motor cars.

大多数欧洲城市早在马匹还是高科技的时代就开始了城市规划，而美国的许多城市却是围绕城市扩张、郊区大片住宅区和汽车来设计的。

F

As traffic and cities grew, so did the race to solve growing traffic problems.

随着交通流量和城市规模的增长，解决日益严峻的交通问题的竞赛也在不断升级。

The first traffic signals were semaphores.

最早的交通信号装置是臂板信号机。

Semaphores are towers with moving arms signaling for traffic to stop or go.

臂板信号机是带有活动臂杆的塔架，臂杆摆动来示意车辆停下或通行。

They could be manual or automatic.

它们既可以是手动的，也可以是自动的。

There was no consistent design.

其设计并无统一标准。

Various models were invented and put into place in cities as the need arose.

随着需求的出现，各种各样的方案被发明出来，并在城市中投入实施。

G

In 1912, Lester Wire was a police officer exhausted by traffic at Main Street and 200 South.

1912年，警官莱斯特·怀尔在主街与南200街路口被交通问题搞得精疲力竭。

He developed a manually switched stop light.

他研制出一种手动切换的红绿灯。

The four-sided box, raised on a ten-foot pole, looked like a birdhouse and was operated manually by an officer.

这个四方形的箱体架在一根十英尺高的杆子上，看起来像一个鸟舍，由一名警官手动操作。

The officer would flip a switch to change between the dipped red and green lights.

警官只需拨动开关，就能在红色和绿色灯光之间切换。

H

Like the 1910 semaphore, the first electric signal used words.

与1910年的信号臂一样，最早的电气信号也是用文字显示的。

However, the words were no longer written on arms that raised and fell.

只不过，文字不再写在上下起落的信号臂上。

The words "stop" or "move" were on lights.

"停"或"行"字样直接显示在灯光上。

The posts lit up on each of four corner-posts around an intersection.

路口四周的四根角柱上都装有可点亮的柱灯。

It was not automated, though.

不过，这套系统并非自动化。

A booth with an operator was required to flip the switches.

需要设一个岗亭，由操作员负责拨动开关。

I

The system allowed police officers to move from the middle of the street to a corner.

这套系统使警官可以从街道中央转移到路口角落。

From that vantage, an officer could oversee the crowd.

从这一有利位置，执勤警员可以俯瞰人群。

If an emergency vehicle came, the officer could flip a switch and clear the intersection by triggering all red lights.

如有应急车辆驶来，警员只需扳动开关，触发所有红灯，即可清空路口。

The emergency vehicle could now pass effortlessly.

这样一来，应急车辆便可畅通无阻地通过。

Finally, in San Francisco the first red and green electric light system that could be operated automatically (or manually) was introduced in 1917.

最后，1917年，旧金山启用了第一套可自动（或手动）操控的红绿灯系统。

J

In 1920, the first three-color light system was introduced in Detroit — also known as Motor City, USA.

1920年，底特律——又称美国汽车城——启用了第一套三色信号灯系统。

The evolution of traffic light signals could not have a more appropriate context than Motor City.

红绿灯信号的演进，没有比汽车城更为恰当的背景了。

Ford Motor Company was founded in Detroit in 1903 (and other motor companies followed).

福特汽车公司于1903年在底特律创立（随后其他汽车公司也相继成立）。

Ford made cars affordable for more common people, rather than the wealthy, by creating larger scale factory production.

福特通过大规模工厂生产，使汽车不再是富人的专属，而为普通大众所负担得起。

K

Stop and go were insufficient for the new volumes of cars zooming through intersections.

对于穿梭于路口的庞大车流量来说，仅靠红灯停、绿灯行已远远不够。

Streets were full of new numbers of cars in addition to delivery wagons, bustling crowds, bicycles, horse-drawn carriages, and street cars.

街道上除了新增的大量汽车，还有送货马车、熙攘的人群、自行车、马车以及有轨电车，可谓车水马龙。

The introduction of the cautionary yellow light helped increase driver safety, decreasing accidents that come from running red lights.

警示性黄色信号灯的引入有助于提升驾驶安全，减少了因闯红灯而引发的事故。

L

Stop lights were incredibly important in chaotic city streets.

在混乱的城市街道上，红绿灯至关重要。

Crosswalks weren't even introduced until 1951.

人行横道直到1951年才出现。

The first pedestrian fatality in the United States was in 1899.

美国第一起行人死亡事故发生在1899年。

M

The development of the modern traffic light may have started in London, but it grew up in the United States.

现代交通信号灯的发展或许始于伦敦，但它是在美国发展成熟起来的。

In Cleveland, an African-American inventor named Garrett Morgan came up with an affordable, patented traffic signal.

在克利夫兰，一位名叫加勒特·摩根的非裔美国发明家设计出了一款价格低廉、获得专利的交通信号灯。

The yellow light made intersections safer than the old stop and go system.

黄灯的出现使十字路口比旧式的"停/走"信号系统更安全。

The new design was inexpensive enough that it allowed the installation of a lot more lights.

新设计成本足够低，使得大量安装信号灯成为可能。

N

Morgan sold the patent to General Electric — and the spread of the traffic light was green lighted.

摩根把专利卖给了通用电气公司——交通信号灯的推广由此"亮起了绿灯"。

Green means go.

绿灯意味着通行。

It is that simple, but it's still best to check both ways for oncoming traffic that may not be paying attention or is running the light.

就这么简单，但最好还是朝两边看看有没有驶来的车辆——它们可能注意力不集中，或者正在闯红灯。

Yellow means slow down and prepare to stop.

黄灯意味着减速并准备停车。

It does not mean full-throttle ahead.

这并不意味着全速前进。

While you may get away with gunning it into the intersection, running a yellow can lead to tickets and dangerous accidents.

虽然你或许能侥幸一脚油门冲过路口，但闯黄灯可能会招来罚单，甚至引发危险的交通事故。

Red lights are a clear and consistent warning to stop.

红灯是明确且一致的停车信号。

Even where right turns are allowed on red lights, you must come to a complete stop before turning right.

即使在允许红灯右转的路口，你也必须在右转前完全停车。

You must also yield to any through traffic, which has the right of way.

你还必须让行享有通行权的直行车辆。

In most states and cities, you can take a right on red unless otherwise indicated.

在大多数州和城市，除非另有指示，你可以在红灯时右转。

New York City does not allow a right on red.

纽约市不允许红灯右转。

O

Red arrows mean you cannot turn right on a red in most states.

在大多数州，红色箭头表示不能在红灯时右转。

The green arrow, in these cases, is the only time you can make the turn.

在这种情况下，只有绿色箭头亮起时才能转弯。

However, you can take a right on a red arrow after a full stop in the states of Illinois, Kansas, Louisiana, Maryland, Michigan, New Hampshire, North Dakota, Oregon, South Carolina, Utah, Washington State and Wyoming.

不过，在伊利诺伊、堪萨斯、路易斯安那、马里兰、密歇根、新罕布什尔、北达科他、俄勒冈、南卡罗来纳、犹他、华盛顿州和怀俄明这些州，完全停车后可以按红色箭头指示右转。

P

Flashing Red is the same as a stop sign, generally used at four-way intersections where there is minimal traffic.

闪烁红灯等同于停车标志，通常设在车流量很少的四向交叉路口。

When arriving at a flashing red light, make a full and complete stop.

遇到闪烁红灯时，必须完全停车。

Flashing Red Arrows similarly mean to stop and then proceed to take the turn.

闪烁的红色箭头同样表示先停车，然后再转弯。

Flashing Yellow lights mean yield or signal caution, like outside of a fire station.

闪烁的黄灯表示让行或警示，例如设在消防站附近。

There are no all-direction flashing yellow lights, since it is prohibited in the United States.

由于美国禁止使用全方向闪烁黄灯，因此不存在这种信号灯。

If you have a flashing yellow light, you are to yield to oncoming traffic.

如果遇到闪烁的黄灯，你应当让行对向来车。

Q

Flashing Yellow Arrows are part of a four-signal traffic light.

闪烁黄箭头是四灯式信号灯的一部分。

These are used in some states after a protected green turn signal, or arrow, but before a steady yellow arrow.

在美国一些州，这种信号用于受保护的绿色转弯信号（即绿色箭头）之后、恒亮黄箭头之前。

When a flashing yellow arrow is shown, you can take the turn after yielding to pedestrians and traffic.

当出现闪烁黄箭头时，可在让行行人和车辆后进行转弯。

Steady Yellow Arrows maintain the normal yellow light caution in three-light signals, meaning to stop or prepare to stop.

恒亮黄箭头与三灯式信号灯中的普通黄灯含义相同，表示停车或准备停车。

In a four-signal system that has a flashing yellow arrow, the steady yellow arrow is a signal that the yield period is ending.

在设有闪烁黄箭头的四灯式信号系统中，恒亮黄箭头表示让行时段即将结束。

The red arrow is about to start.

红色箭头即将亮起。

Vehicles should stop if safely possible.

如能安全停车，车辆应当停车。

Traffic tickets are possible for running a steady yellow arrow.

闯恒亮黄箭头可能会被开具交通罚单。

R

Traffic light meaning is fairly consistent across the board with various deviations.

各地交通信号灯的含义总体相当一致，但也存在各种差异。

Green means it's safe to proceed (though you should still exercise caution).

绿灯表示可以安全通行（但仍应保持谨慎）。

Yellow means caution and yield.

黄色代表警示与让行。

Red means danger and stop.

红色代表危险与停止。

Always drive defensively and obey traffic signals.

行车时应始终保持防御性驾驶，并遵守交通信号。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1453 词 · 19 句

P1

Mindfulness has been shown to have a number of meaningful health benefits — it can help reduce anxiety and promote healing in those suffering from chronic illness.

研究表明，正念对健康有诸多益处——它有助于减轻焦虑，并促进慢性病患者的康复。

P2

The practice is based on an insight first described in ancient Buddhist texts that human beings have the capacity to observe experience without being caught up in it.

这种练习基于古代佛教典籍中最早提出的一种洞见：人类有能力观察自己的体验而不深陷其中。

This means simply that it is possible to observe ourselves having a craving, or a happy thought, or even a scary emotion, without reacting in the moment in a way that amplifies the feeling or sends the mind spiraling off into thinking about old memories or anticipating events.

这简单地说，就是我们可以观察到自己正在产生某种渴望、某个快乐的念头，甚至一种可怕的情绪，而不会在当下作出反应，放大这种感受，或让思绪失控地陷入对往事的回忆、对未来的预想。

P3

This practice of mindfulness can help calm the mind and the body as we learn not to react to experience with likes and dislikes or judgments of good and bad.

当我们学会不再以好恶或是非评判来回应体验时，正念练习有助于使身心平静下来。

It does not make us cold or indifferent but more fully present.

它不会让我们变得冷漠或麻木，而是让我们更加全然地活在当下。

P4

One of the challenges of practicing mindfulness in our contemporary world is that there has been a profound transformation in human attention.

在当代社会践行正念面临的挑战之一，是人类的注意力已经发生了深刻的转变。

The artist Jenny Odell argues that in our "attention economy" human attention has been transformed into a commodity that big corporations buy and sell.

艺术家珍妮·奥德尔指出，在我们的“注意力经济”中，人类的注意力已被转化为一种由大公司买卖的商品。

This economy rests on a technological revolution of mobile phones and social media that makes it possible for corporations to target our attention with their content at every moment.

这种经济建立在移动电话和社交媒体的技术革命之上，使企业得以随时随地用他们的内容争夺我们的注意力。

P5

The devices most people use are a perpetual diversion from the present moment.

大多数人使用的设备，让人一刻不停地偏离当下。

The result is that it can feel as though our ability to focus, and be fully present, has been stolen.

结果便是，我们似乎觉得自己的专注能力和全然临在的能力已被窃取。

But mindfulness can help us resist the attention economy and relish the things that make life special, like being together with those we love.

但正念可以帮助我们抵御注意力经济的侵蚀，尽情品味那些让生活变得美好的事物，比如与所爱之人相伴。

P6

Most mindfulness research focuses on the individual benefits of the practice, but some scholars assert that we can not only practice mindfulness for ourselves but also practice it for others.

大多数关于正念的研究都聚焦于练习对个人的益处，但有些学者主张，我们不仅可以为自己修炼正念，也可以为他人修炼正念。

It can help us build stronger, healthier relationships.

它可以帮助我们建立更牢固、更健康的亲密关系。

P7

The sad truth is that living in the attention economy, most of us have become bad listeners.

可悲的事实是，在注意力经济中生活，我们大多数人都变成了糟糕的倾听者。

However, just as it is possible to watch ourselves having an experience without reacting, it's possible to watch another person have an experience without getting tied up in reactivity and judgment.

然而，正如我们可以在不做出反应的情况下观察自己的经历一样，我们也可以在不被情绪反应和评判牵绊的情况下去观察另一个人的经历。

P8

The gift of mindfulness is a practice of listening with compassion to others describe their experiences.

正念的馈赠，是一种带着慈悲心倾听他人讲述自身经历的修行。

To give this gift means putting away your phone, turning off social media, and setting aside other common distractions.

要送出这份礼物，意味着收起手机、关闭社交媒体，并将其他常见的干扰源暂时搁置一旁。

It means practicing being fully present in another person's presence and listening to them with complete attention without judgment, while resisting the urge to make the interaction about you.

这意味着练习在另一个人面前全然在场，带着完全的专注去倾听，不加评判，同时克制住把互动变成以自己为中心的冲动。

P9

If we judge the value of gifts based on their price, this gift may seem worthless, but in a distracted world, it is a precious one.

如果我们以价格来衡量礼物的价值，这份礼物或许看似一文不值；但在一个充满分心的世界里，它却无比珍贵。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2451 词 · 27 句

P1

The other day I had to log into a service I hadn't used before.

前几天，我需要登录一个从未使用过的服务。

Since I was a new user, the website decided that it needed to check I wasn't a robot and so set me a Captcha test to determine whether the user is a person or a machine.

由于我是新用户，网站认为有必要确认我不是机器人，于是给我出了一道验证码测试，以判断用户是真人还是机器。

P2

I was presented with an image of a roadside scene over which was laid a grid.

我面前出现了一张路边场景的图片，上面覆盖着一层网格。

My "challenge" was to click on each cell in the grid that contained a traffic sign.

我的“挑战”是点击网格中每一个含有交通标志的方格。

I did so, a bit irritated.

我照做了，心里有些恼火。

Then I was presented with another image and another grid — also with a request to identify road signs.

接着又出现了一张新图片和一层新的网格——同样是要求识别道路标志。

Like a lamb, I complied, after which the website accepted my input.

我像个羔羊一样乖乖照做，之后网站才接受了我的输入。

P3

And then I realised what I had been doing was adding to a dataset for training the machine-learning software that guides self-driving cars.

随后我才意识到，我所做的其实是在为训练指导自动驾驶汽车的机器学习软件提供数据集。

So, to gain access to an automated service that will benefit financially from my input, I first have to do some unpaid labor to help improve the performance of autonomous vehicles.

也就是说，为了使用一项能从我的输入中获利的自动化服务，我首先得无偿劳动，以帮助提升自动驾驶车辆的性能。

P4

Neat, eh?

够精明的吧？

But note also the delicious additional irony that the Captcha is described as an "automated Turing test".

但请注意其中另一层绝妙的讽刺：这道验证码被称为“自动图灵测试”。

The Turing test was conceived, you may recall, as a way of enabling humans to determine whether a machine could respond in such a way that one couldn't tell whether it was a human or a robot.

你或许还记得，图灵测试的构想是让人能够判断机器的应答方式是否让人无法分辨它是人还是机器人。

So we have wandered into a world in which machines make us do tricks to prove that we are humans!

于是我们误入了一个怪圈：机器反倒要我们耍把式、做题，来证明自己是人！

P5

The strangest aspect of this unparalleled shift is how under-discussed it has been.

这场前所未有的变革最奇怪的一点，是人们对此讨论之少。

The metaphor of the boiling frog comes to mind.

这让人想起温水煮青蛙的比喻。

Have we become so subtly conditioned by digital technology that we don't see what's been happening to us?

我们是否已被数字技术潜移默化地驯化，以至于对发生在自己身上的事情浑然不觉？

Have we been conditioned to accept a world governed by "smart" tech, trading convenience to the point where we become a bit like machines ourselves?

我们是否已被驯化到接受一个由“智能”科技主导的世界，为了换取便利而不断让步，最终自己也变得有点像机器？

P6

In a recent startling and thoughtful book, two scholars — Brett Frischmann, a law professor, and Evan Selinger, a philosopher — argue that the answer to that question is "yes".

最近一本发人深省的著作中，两位学者——法学教授布雷特·弗里希曼和哲学家埃文·塞林格——认为，对这个问题的回答是“是的”。

P7

And they're right.

他们说得没错。

There's nothing technophobic about that.

这绝非什么技术恐惧症。

We shape our tools and then our tools shape us.

我们塑造工具，工具继而塑造我们。

Technology is supposed to provide tools that serve human ends.

技术本应提供服务于人类目的的工具。

But, as the machine-learning Captcha demonstrates, a significant proportion of digital tech now sees (and uses) humans as means to ends that are not ours.

然而，正如机器学习版验证码所表明的，如今相当一部分数字技术把人视为（并当作）实现非人类目的的手段。

In the process, they reduce us to the status of cheery rats running on treadmills designed by people who do not have our interests at heart.

在这个过程中，我们被降格成了兴高采烈地在跑步机上奔跑的老鼠，而设计跑步机的人并不把我们的利益放在心上。

So back to the frog metaphor.

那么，再回到青蛙的比喻。

Are we smart enough to jump out before it's too late?

我们是否足够聪明，能在为时已晚之前跳出来？

You don't even have to Google it to know the answer.

你甚至不需要上网搜索就能知道答案。
