Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）291 词 · 14 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) anywhereB) assertsC) constituentD) correlatedE) determiningF) devastatingG) enactedH) entailsI) exemptionJ) imposeK) notifiedL) notwithstandingM) presumeN) rehabilitationO) reproaching

Two proposals are before the state legislature that look to put some limits on ever-increasing rent prices, which some lawmakers say are contributing to rising homelessness rates.

州议会目前有两项提案，旨在对不断上涨的租金价格加以限制，一些议员认为，租金上涨正在加剧无家可归率的上升。

The proposals impose caps on rent increases, preventing landlords in the state from raising rent more than the rate of inflation in most cases.

这两项提案对租金涨幅设定上限，在大多数情况下，禁止该州房东将租金提高到超过通货膨胀率的水平。

There are exceptions for buildings less than 10 years old, public housing, improvements beyond maintenance, and a hardship exemption.

豁免情形包括楼龄不足10年的建筑、公共住房、超出日常维护范围的改善工程以及困难减免。

In a 2020 study, researchers at the U.S. Government Accountability Office estimated that every $100 in rent increase is correlated to a 9% rise in homelessness.

在2020年的一项研究中，美国政府问责局的研究人员估算，租金每上涨100美元，无家可归人数就会相应增加9%。

Rent increases have an even more detrimental effect on some demographic groups, such as racial minorities.

租金上涨对某些特定人群，例如少数族裔，产生的不利影响更为严重。

Representative Alex Ramel explained why he thought that this kind of legislation was so important, telling the story of a constituent struggling to live in the community.

众议员亚历克斯·拉梅尔阐述了他认为这类立法为何如此重要，讲述了一位选民在社区艰难生活的经历。

Ramel pointed out the devastating effect of double-digit rent increases, saying that he made the decision to introduce the legislation while talking to a young man in Bellingham.

拉梅尔指出租金两位数上涨所造成的毁灭性影响，并表示自己正是在与一名年轻人交谈时决定提出这项立法的，那是在贝灵厄姆。

"He's a recent graduate from Western Washington University and just got a new job," Ramel said.

拉梅尔说："他刚从西华盛顿大学毕业，找到了一份新工作。"

"He was leaving our community because his landlord notified him that his rent was going to go up by $600 a month."

"他正在离开我们的社区，因为房东通知他每月租金要上涨600美元。"

The Rental Housing Association of Washington opposes the legislation.

华盛顿州租赁住房协会反对这项立法。

The association asserts that the policy won't actually solve the problem of rental prices.

该协会声称，这项政策实际上无法解决租金价格问题。

Instead, it will punish small landlords and allow wealthy renters easy access to cheap housing.

相反，它会惩罚小房东，并让富裕租户轻松获得廉价住房。

"The rent control policy has failed everywhere it has been enacted in this county. It has not fixed one housing problem anywhere. It also does not distinguish between rich and poor when determining who gets the benefits of rent control,"

“租金管制政策在该县所有实施过的地方都无一例外地失败了。它在任何地方都没能解决哪怕一个住房问题。而且，在确定谁能享受租金管制带来的好处时，它也不分贫富，”

said Chester Baldwin with the Rental Housing Coalition.

租赁住房联盟的切斯特·鲍德温说。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1194 词 · 44 句

A

For those "citizens of the world", the office can be anything from a beach hut in Brisbane to a Starbucks in Seattle, thanks to the growing prevalence of remote-based work.

对于这些“世界公民”来说，由于远程办公日益普及，办公室可以是布里斯班的一间海边小屋，也可以是西雅图的一家星巴克。

But roaming the globe from café to café is not without its challenges.

然而，穿梭于全球各地、从一家咖啡馆辗转到另一家，也并非没有挑战。

B

When the average traveller lands in a city like Bangkok, Berlin, or Buenos Aires, their first step might be to drop their bags at their hotel and to log on to TripAdvisor to find out the hours of a popular tourist attraction or the best rooftop bar for happy hour.

普通旅客抵达曼谷、柏林或布宜诺斯艾利斯这样的城市时，第一步或许是先把行李放到酒店，然后登录猫途鹰，查询某个热门景点的开放时间，或是寻找适合欢乐时光时段的最佳露台酒吧。

C

But people like Pieter Levels are not your average traveller.

但皮特·莱弗斯这样的人并非普通旅客。

A Dutch website developer, Levels has been living and working nomadically in places like Singapore, Hong Kong, Tokyo and Manila for two years.

莱弗斯是一名荷兰网站开发者，两年来他一直以游牧的方式在新加坡、香港、东京和马尼拉等地生活和工作。

With just a carry-on backpack, he chooses his location at will, based on a variety of factors—Wi-Fi speed, average temperature, exotic food and cost of living—that have little bearing on the work he actually does.

仅凭一个随身背包，他便随心所欲地选择落脚之地，依据的是各种与他实际从事的工作并无多大关系的因素——网速、平均气温、异域美食和生活成本。

D

Somewhere in his nomadic journey, Levels noticed that there was a growing need to create an online infrastructure for people like himself.

在游牧般的旅途中，莱弗斯注意到，像他这样的人越来越需要一套在线基础设施。

That's when he created #nomads, a membership-based chat system which runs on the team communication platform known as Slack.

于是他创建了 #nomads，一个基于会员制的聊天系统，运行在名为 Slack 的团队沟通平台上。

The channel allows users to ask people in their current city the types of practical questions that a tourist would unlikely need to ask.

该频道允许用户向身处当前城市的人提出各类实际问题——这些问题是普通游客几乎不会去问的。

E

Members of the geographically dispersed subculture that Levels is a part of call themselves "digital nomads".

莱弗斯所属的这一地理分布广泛的亚文化群体自称“数字游民”。

They are borderless, mostly self-employed remote workers who interact with cities in a way that's entirely different from a tourist, but still doesn't quite reach the status of resident.

他们是没有国界、大多为自雇人士的远程工作者，他们与城市互动的方式与游客截然不同，但又尚未达到居民的地位。

A relatively new phenomenon, there are no hard statistics on the prevalence of digital nomads or location independent workers.

作为一种相对较新的现象，目前尚无确切统计数据说明数字游民或不受地域限制的工作者的普遍程度。

But thanks to the growing prevalence of freelance livelihoods, a global sharing economy and numerous digital tools, the number of people who are able to live this life is growing quickly.

不过，随着自由职业日益普遍、全球共享经济兴起以及各种数字工具的涌现，能够过上这种生活的人正迅速增多。

And in a rapidly urbanising world, where a major city in Asia increasingly offers the same comforts as one in Europe, many people are reaching the realisation they don't need to stay in one place.

而在这个快速城市化的世界里，亚洲的大城市所能提供的舒适条件日益与欧洲大城市不相上下，许多人渐渐意识到，自己不必固守一地。

F

So how does a nomad differ from a tourist or resident?

那么，游牧者与游客或常住居民究竟有何不同？

In many ways, Levels says, you can think of a digital nomad like a "short-term independent expat".

莱韦尔斯说，从许多方面来看，可以把数字游民看作是“短期的独立外籍人士”。

Unlike a resident expat, they don't work for a large multinational company in an air conditioned office building and they generally enter a country on a tourist visa, simply renewing or moving on when it expires.

与常驻的外籍人士不同，他们不在装有空调的写字楼里为大型跨国公司工作；他们通常持旅游签证入境，签证到期时便续签或转往他处。

But unlike a tourist, they don't arrive in a foreign city without any responsibilities.

但与游客不同，他们来到一座异国城市时并非毫无责任。

Travel isn't necessarily required for them to do their jobs, but neither is staying in one place.

他们工作未必需要四处旅行，但也未必非得待在一个地方。

G

Janet Brent is an independent ebook designer.

珍妮特·布伦特是一名独立电子书设计师。

She says many people she knows assume she travels for work, but in reality her international destinations are largely up to her, and driven by cost.

她说，她认识的许多人都以为她是因工作而出行，但实际上她去哪些国家主要由自己决定，并由成本驱动。

"Contrary to what some people might think, I don't get paid to travel—I simply choose the lifestyle. Rather than rent in one given city, I choose to travel and do short-term, temporary stays around the world, and my travel decisions are literally based on what makes the most sense financially for me,"

“与有些人想的相反，我并不是靠旅行挣钱——我只是选择了这种生活方式。我不在某一座城市租房，而是选择旅行，在世界各地作短期、临时的停留；我决定去哪里，完全取决于怎样在经济上对我最划算，”

Brent said.

布伦特说。

"It's completely different from vacationing—you really sink into the city life and get your feet wet in the communities."

“这和度假完全是两回事——你会真正沉浸于城市生活，深入当地社区之中。”

H

Bryce Adams is another such nomad.

布莱斯·亚当斯就是另一位这样的游牧者。

He works as a developer for a South African company WooThemes, which has a workforce that's more than 80% remote-based.

他在一家南非公司WooThemes担任开发人员，该公司超过80%的员工都在远程办公。

Though Adams frequently posts pictures on Instagram of his enviable cooking adventures from Seoul to Bangkok, he says nomad life in foreign cities has its downsides, including finding adequate Wi-Fi for the bandwidth-hungry work his livelihood depends on.

尽管亚当斯经常在Instagram上发布他在首尔到曼谷之间令人艳羡的烹饪之旅的照片，但他说，在异国城市过游牧生活也有其弊端，其中就包括为他赖以为生的、需要高带宽的工作寻找足够快的Wi-Fi。

Contrary to the perception that he works under a mango tree in a tropical haven, Adams says the reality of this life means that he often ends up staying in more western style places in globalised cities—ones that are sure to have air conditioning and a decent connection—rather than exotic beachfront cafés.

与人们想象的他在热带天堂的芒果树下工作的场景相反，亚当斯说，这种生活的现实意味着他往往最终会待在全球化城市中更西式的地方——这些地方肯定配有空调和稳定的网络——而非异国情调的海滨咖啡馆。

I

"A fellow nomad posted quite a funny but honest message on Twitter a few days ago that stuck with me: 'It's funny that digital nomads spend 16 hours to fly to the other side of the world to sit in Starbucks,' and it's completely true," Adams said.

亚当斯说：“几天前，一位数字游民伙伴在Twitter上发了一条既好笑又诚实的留言，令我印象深刻：‘有趣的是，数字游民花16个小时飞到地球的另一端，却只是为了坐在星巴克里。’这话完全属实。”

"When you're a digital nomad, you don't actually have as much freedom as most people think you do. We have deadlines, we all have people relying on us and a mouth (or two) that needs to be fed. It's nice to be able to work anywhere in the world but it's not without its challenges."

“当你是一名数字游民时，你其实并没有大多数人以为的那么自由。我们有截止日期，有需要依靠我们的人，还有一张（或两张）等着喂的嘴。能够在全球任何地方工作固然很好，但也并非没有挑战。”

J

But these challenges can often provide an opportunity for nomads to see a side of a city they may not have if they were staying in a luxury hotel for a long weekend.

然而，这些挑战往往能让游民看到一座城市的另一面——如果他们只是在一座城市度过一个豪华的长周末，可能根本无缘得见。

However inconvenient, a trip to a local doctor, immigration office or laundry—or a conflict with a local Airbnb host (the accommodation style of choice for most nomads)—can be far more culturally instructive than a trip to the national museum.

去看当地医生、去移民局、去洗衣店——或者与当地的Airbnb房东（大多数游民偏爱的住宿方式）发生摩擦——无论多么不便，都远比去一趟国家博物馆更具文化启发性。

And because nomads often have to stay where the strongest internet connection is, they tend to spend more time in developing cities compared to the average tourist, who often flees to more touristy destinations soon after landing at the airport.

而且，由于游民往往不得不待在网络信号最强的地方，与普通游客相比，他们在一个城市停留的时间更长，而普通游客通常一下飞机就奔向更热门的旅游目的地。

K

The nomad life is, by all accounts, one built out of privilege.

据所有人说，游牧生活是一种建立在特权之上的生活。

You not only need a relatively powerful passport that makes tourist visas easy to attain, but also a knowledge economy job and access to the technology and devices that make remote working possible.

你不仅需要一本实力相对较强的护照，使你能够轻松获得旅游签证，还需要一份知识经济类的工作，以及接触使远程办公成为可能的技术和设备的条件。

But nomads like Adams say that while they are aware of this privilege, they believe living this way elevates what they're able to offer, by giving them a broader perspective on what they're building, writing, or designing and expanding their world view.

但亚当斯这样的游民表示，尽管他们意识到了这种特权，他们相信这种生活方式提升了他们的能力，使他们能够以更广阔的视角看待自己正在构建、撰写或设计的内容，并拓展了他们的世界观。

L

"Being able to travel and get around makes it easy to stay inspired," he added.

"能够四处旅行、走南闯北，让人很容易保持灵感，"他补充道。

"I truly believe that it makes me a more sensible human being, as I tend to see and appreciate the creative solutions people come up with in other parts of the world, even though many tend to have far more constraints than we have at home."

"我真心相信，这让我成为一个更通情达理的人，因为我往往会看到并欣赏世界各地的人们想出的创造性解决方案，尽管他们面临的限制往往远比我们在国内面临的多。"

M

So when you're constantly on the road and don't own any property or have a fixed address, which city do you call home?

那么，当你常年在路上，没有房产、没有固定住址时，你会把哪座城市称为家呢？

It's no surprise that many nomads are dual citizens or "third culture kids," who have grown up with a notion of identity that's not tied to nationalism or staying in one place.

难怪许多游牧者都是双重国籍人士或"第三文化孩子"，他们的成长伴随着一种不与民族主义或定居一地挂钩的身份认同观念。

Levels says that the nomad life has not only changed his notions of citizenship, but the people he chooses to spend time with as well.

Levels说，游牧生活不仅改变了他对公民身份的看法，也改变了他选择与之相处的人。

"Saying you're a 'citizen of the world' is a super cliché, right? But it's becoming quite true,"

"说自己是'世界公民'，这话超级俗套，对吧？但它正变得相当真实，"

Levels said.

Levels说道。

"This global awareness has been the reason I started Nomad List and it's affected who my friends are. It's normal to have dinner now and sit with a Korean girl, a British-Singaporean guy, a Mexican girl and a Nigerian guy. As much as that sounds like a politically correct TV advertisement, it's great that that's the time we're in now."

"这种全球意识正是我创办Nomad List的原因，它也影响了我的交友圈。现在和一位韩国姑娘、一位英裔新加坡小伙、一位墨西哥姑娘和一位尼日利亚小伙同桌吃饭，已是家常便饭。尽管这听起来像是一则政治正确的电视广告，但能生活在这样一个时代，真的很棒。"

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1447 词 · 21 句

P1

Children's use of social media is a problem.

儿童使用社交媒体是个问题。

That doesn't mean it should be stopped.

但这并不意味着就该禁止他们使用。

While younger children should not be on platforms where 13 is the minimum age, it is neither practical nor desirable to imagine a world in which teenagers are prevented from accessing the platforms and messaging systems that the adults around them use to organise their lives.

虽然低龄儿童不应使用那些规定最低年龄为13岁的平台，但要设想一个让青少年无法接触周围成年人用来安排生活的平台和通讯系统的世界，既不现实也不可取。

P2

But the pleasure and stimulation that people of all ages find on their screens and smartphones must not blind us to the harm that spending time in this way can cause.

然而，各年龄段的人在屏幕和智能手机上获得的愉悦和刺激，绝不能蒙蔽我们，让我们忽视以这种方式消磨时间所带来的危害。

The finding that the alarmingly high rate of depression in British teenage girls is closely correlated to time spent on social media is extremely concerning.

有研究发现，英国少女的抑郁率高得惊人，且与使用社交媒体的时间密切相关，这一发现令人极为担忧。

Research found that two in five girls are on social media for at least three hours a day—with half as many boys engaged to the same extent.

研究发现，五分之二的女孩每天使用社交媒体至少三小时——达到这一程度的男孩人数只有女孩的一半。

More than a third of depressed girls have experienced online harassment.

超过三分之一的抑郁女孩曾遭受网络骚扰。

About half of affected girls suffered disrupted sleep, compared with 20% of depressed boys.

约半数受抑郁影响的女孩睡眠受到影响，而抑郁男孩中这一比例为20%。

P3

Although the research does not prove that social media causes depression, the importance of the finding that there is a connection should not be underestimated.

尽管该研究并未证明社交媒体会导致抑郁，但两者存在关联这一发现的重要性不容低估。

Schools have done important work in communicating key messages about the risks of online abuse.

学校在传播有关网络侵害风险的关键信息方面做了重要工作。

But when it comes to broader questions about "screen time", many professionals as well as parents are confused and uncertain.

但涉及到有关“屏幕时间”的更广泛问题时，许多专业人士和家长都感到困惑和不确定。

P4

The health secretary, Matt Hancock, wants official guidelines on time and age limits.

卫生大臣马特·汉考克希望出台有关使用时长和年龄限制的官方指南。

This idea has the virtue of simplicity, though some experts question its effects and call for a greater degree of differentiation, arguing that time online by itself is not a bad thing and it all depends on what you are doing.

这一理念的优点在于简单明了，尽管一些专家质疑其效果，主张应有更大的区分度，并指出上网时间本身并非坏事，关键在于你在网上做什么。

Also due this year is information commissioner Elizabeth Denham's Age Appropriate Design Code, covering the use of children's personal data.

今年还将实施的是信息专员伊丽莎白·登厄姆提出的《适龄设计规范》，涉及儿童个人数据的使用。

Both initiatives are welcome.

这两项举措都值得欢迎。

But the government is playing catch-up.

但政府一直在亡羊补牢。

Statutory regulation of the digital media giants is long overdue.

对数字媒体巨头实施法定监管早已迫在眉睫。

The failure to protect children from data harvesting and targeted advertising thus far is as shocking as the lack of concern shown by the companies themselves with regard to children's psychological health.

迄今为止未能保护儿童免受数据收割和定向广告的侵害，其令人震惊的程度不亚于这些公司自身对儿童心理健康问题所表现出的漠不关心。

P5

Of course, social media companies are not the source of all our ills, and can offer a too-easy target for politicians keen to divert attention from inadequately resourced schools and health services.

当然，社交媒体公司并非所有弊病的根源，它们也容易成为政客们的靶子——这些人急于将公众注意力从资金不足的学校和医疗服务上转移开。

There are other reasons for the rising incidence of depression in young people, with increasing pressure to succeed in education and in life more broadly—to be famous, beautiful, rich—often cited as a source of unhappiness or anxiety which can, if aggravated by other factors and not managed, tip into ill health.

年轻人抑郁率上升还有其他原因，比如在教育乃至更广泛的人生领域中日益加剧的成功压力——成名、貌美、富有——常被视为不快乐或焦虑的来源，而这些情绪若因其他因素加剧且得不到疏导，就可能恶化为疾病。

Safeguarding means more than the avoidance of physical threat.

保护儿童不仅仅是避免身体受到威胁。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2443 词 · 20 句

P1

About a decade ago, the G-20, a forum of the world's biggest economies, agreed to crack down on multinational corporations' abusive use of tax havens.

大约十年前，二十国集团（G-20）——由全球最大的经济体组成的论坛——同意严厉打击跨国公司滥用避税天堂的行为。

This resulted in a plan that aimed to curb practices that shielded a large chunk of corporate profits from tax authorities.

为此出台了一项计划，旨在遏制那些使大量企业利润得以逃税务部门监管的做法。

P2

But, instead of reining in the use of tax havens—countries such as the Bahamas and Cayman Islands with very low or no effective tax rates—the problem has only gotten worse.

然而，非但没有遏制避税天堂的使用——即巴哈马、开曼群岛这类实际税率极低甚至为零的国家和地区——问题反而愈演愈烈。

P3

In a new study, we measured the excessive profits reported in tax havens that cannot be explained by ordinary economic activity such as employees, factories and research in that country.

在一项新研究中，我们测算出避税天堂所报告的、无法用该国员工、工厂和研发等正常经济活动来解释的超额利润。

Our findings show a striking pattern of artificial shifting of paper profits to tax havens by corporations, which has been relentless since the 1980s.

我们的研究结果揭示了一个触目惊心的模式：企业将账面利润人为转移至避税天堂，且自20世纪80年代以来这种做法从未停歇。

P4

We found that the world's biggest multinational businesses shifted 37% of the profits—$969 billion—they earned in other countries to tax havens in 2019.

我们发现，2019年，全球最大的跨国企业将其在其他国家赚取的利润中的37%（9690亿美元）转移到了避税天堂。

The figure was less than 2% in the 1970s.

而在20世纪70年代，这一比例还不到2%。

The main reasons for the large increase were the growth of the tax avoidance industry in the 1980s and U.S. policies that made it easier to shift profits from high-tax countries to tax havens.

大幅增长的主要原因，是20世纪80年代避税产业的兴起，以及美国的政策使企业更容易将利润从高税率国家转移到避税天堂。

P5

We estimate that the amount of corporate taxes lost as a result reached 10% of total corporate revenue in 2019, up from less than 0.1% in the 1970s.

我们估计，由此导致的企业税收损失在2019年达到了企业总收入的10%，而在20世纪70年代还不到0.1%。

In 2019, the total government tax loss globally was $250 billion.

2019年，全球政府税收损失总额达2500亿美元。

U.S. multinational corporations alone accounted for about half of that, followed by the U.K. and Germany.

仅美国跨国公司就占了其中约一半，其次是英国和德国。

P6

How do policymakers fix this?

政策制定者该如何解决这一问题呢？

So far, the world as a whole has been trying to solve this problem by cutting or scrapping corporate taxes in a very gradual way.

迄今为止，世界各国一直试图通过渐进式削减或废除企业所得税来解决这一问题。

In the past 40 years, the global effective corporate tax rate has fallen from 23% to 17%.

在过去40年间，全球实际企业所得税率已从23%降至17%。

At the same time, governments have relied more heavily on consumption taxes.

与此同时，各国政府更加依赖消费税。

P7

But the root cause of profit-shifting is the incentives involved, such as generous or lenient corporate tax rates in other countries.

但利润转移的根本原因在于其中的利益驱动，例如其他国家实行的优厚或宽松的企业税率。

If countries could agree on a global minimum corporate tax rate of, say, 20%, the problem of profit-shifting would largely disappear, as tax havens would simply cease to exist.

如果各国能就全球最低企业所得税率达成一致，比如20%，利润转移问题就将基本消失，因为避税天堂将不复存在。

P8

This type of mechanism is exactly what more than 130 countries signed onto in 2021, with implementation of a 15% minimum tax set to begin soon in the EU, U.K., Japan, Indonesia and many other countries.

这正是2021年130多个国家签署认可的机制类型，欧盟、英国、日本、印度尼西亚及许多其他国家即将开始实施15%的最低税率。

The U.S. has notably not been able to pass this legislation.

美国显然未能通过这项立法。

P9

Our research suggests implementing this type of tax reform is necessary in reversing the shift of ever-greater amounts of corporate profits to tax havens—instead of being taxed by the governments where they operate and create value.

我们的研究表明，实施此类税制改革对于扭转越来越多的企业利润流向避税天堂的趋势是必要的——这些利润本应由其开展经营并创造价值所在国的政府征税。

第 2 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,372 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）279 词 · 13 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) behaviouralB) eliminateC) fragileD) globalE) implicationsF) maximiseG) overlappingH) personallyI) presumeJ) proneK) prototypesL) reassureM) skepticallyN) traitsO) underlying

Most financial crises have plenty in common: they tend to start in the banking sector and involve excessive borrowing, together with an asset bubble.

大多数金融危机都有许多共同之处：它们往往始于银行业，涉及过度借贷，并伴随资产泡沫。

The global crisis of 2008 was no different, with the asset bubble focused on US real estate.

2008年的全球危机也不例外，当时的资产泡沫集中在美国房地产市场。

But my research suggests this crisis had another underlying cause—that some people in the banking sector were playing or "gaming" the system for their own financial gain.

但我的研究表明，这场危机还有另一个深层原因——银行业内部有些人为了个人经济利益而在钻制度的空子，或者说“玩弄”体制。

The game being played had several important features.

这场“游戏”有几个重要特征。

The first was the deliberate complexity of the financial products at its core.

第一个特征是其核心金融产品的刻意复杂化。

Then there are the behavioural biases that permeate decision-making at all levels of the banking industry.

其次是渗透于银行业各层级决策过程的行为偏差。

Banking often attracts people with certain personality traits, including those who are overconfident and take excessive risks.

银行业往往吸引具有某些性格特质的人，包括过度自信、甘冒过大风险的人。

Such people tend to like complexity for its own sake, but they often do not fully understand the implications of that complexity for the stability of the financial system as a whole.

这类人往往单纯因为复杂而喜欢复杂，但他们常常不能充分理解这种复杂性对整个金融体系稳定的影响。

Often they do not care—they are primarily interested in gaming the system to maximise their bonuses.

而且他们往往并不在意——他们主要关心的是钻制度的空子，把奖金最大化。

The next element is risk.

下一个要素是风险。

There are parts of the banking sector that will always be prone to risk, but many bankers have come to feel immune to its potential impact, because they presume that, however recklessly banks behave, governments will always be there to bail them out.

银行业中总有一些领域容易与风险相伴，但许多银行家已觉得自己可以免受风险潜在冲击的影响，因为他们认为，无论银行行事多么鲁莽，政府总会出手救助。

Gaming in the banking sector seems virtually impossible to eliminate.

银行业中的这种钻空子行为似乎几乎不可能根除。

The only effective measure to end it would be to make bankers personally liable for losses, but no government has ever passed such a law, and no single government could do so on its own, since this would immediately cause their entire national banking sector to move wholesale to another jurisdiction.

唯一有效的终结办法是让银行家对损失承担个人责任，但没有任何一个政府曾通过这样的法律，也没有哪一个政府能够单方面这样做，因为这会立即使其整个国内银行业整体迁往其他司法管辖区。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1201 词 · 52 句

A

Five hundred years ago, forests covered much of the Iberian peninsula.

五百年前，森林覆盖了伊比利亚半岛的大部分地区。

But that soon changed.

但这种情况很快发生了改变。

Centuries of wars and invasions, agricultural expansion and woodcutting for firewood and shipping wiped out most of the woods and transformed places like Matamorisca, a small village in northern Spain, into degraded landscapes.

数百年的战争与入侵、农业扩张，以及为获取燃料和造船用材而进行的砍伐，摧毁了大部分林地，并将马塔莫里斯卡这样的地方——西班牙北部的一个小村庄——变成了退化的荒地。

B

The region's dry climate and depleted soils would be a recipe for disaster in your average program to replant trees, but for the Amsterdam-based Land Life Company it's an ideal place.

该地区干燥的气候和贫瘠的土壤，对常规的植树项目而言无异于灾难的根源，但对总部设在阿姆斯特丹的生命树公司（Land Life Company）来说，这里却是理想的场所。

"We typically operate where nature does not come back by itself," says Jurrian Ruys, its CEO.

其首席执行官尤里安·鲁伊斯（Jurrian Ruys）表示："我们通常在自然无法自行恢复的地方开展工作。"

"We go where there are rougher conditions in terms of weather, with rough or very hot summers."

"我们去往天气条件更为恶劣的地区，那里夏季酷热难耐或极为粗糙。"

In Matamorisca, they intervened in 17 barren hectares owned by the regional government and peppered them with their signature device: a biodegradable cardboard container they call the cocoon which can hold 25 litres of water underground to aid a seedling's first year.

在马塔莫里斯卡，他们在地方政府所有的17公顷荒地上进行了干预，并在其间布设了公司的标志性设备：一种可降解的硬纸板容器，他们称之为"茧"（cocoon），能够在地下储存25升水，帮助树苗度过第一年。

Around 16,000 trees, including oaks, ashes, walnuts, rowans, and whitebeams, were planted in May 2018, and the company reports that 96% of them survived that year's burning hot summer without extra irrigation, a critical milestone for a young tree.

2018年5月，约16000棵树被种下，包括橡树、白蜡树、核桃树、花楸树和欧洲白蜡树。公司报告称，其中96%在当年酷热的夏季无需额外灌溉便存活了下来，这对幼树而言是一个关键的里程碑。

C

"Does nature return by itself?"

"大自然会自行恢复吗？"

asks Arnout Asjes, Land Life Company's chief technology officer, who oversees a mix of drone and satellite imagery, big data analysis, soil enhancement, QR tagging and site-specific tree configuration designs.

生命树公司的首席技术官阿尔诺特·阿杰斯（Arnout Asjes）问道。他负责统筹无人机与卫星影像、大数据分析、土壤改良、二维码标签以及因地制宜的树木配置设计等多项工作。

"Probably, but it can take decades or hundreds of years, so we are speeding things up."

"可能会，但需要几十年甚至几百年的时间，所以我们正在加快这一进程。"

D

His company belongs to a global movement of organisations trying to save degraded or deforested areas, ranging from fertile tropical lowlands to dry hills in temperate regions.

他所在的公司属于一个全球性的组织网络，致力于拯救退化或森林砍伐的地区，范围从肥沃的热带低地一直到温带地区的干旱丘陵。

Spurred by global biodiversity loss and climate change, these groups are pushing the boundary of how to revive forest cover.

在全球生物多样性丧失和气候变化的推动下，这些团体正在不断突破恢复森林覆盖的边界。

"It's not a theoretical proposition," says Walter Vergara, a forest and climate specialist at the World Resources Institute(WRI).

世界资源研究所（WRI）森林与气候专家沃尔特·韦尔加拉（Walter Vergara）说：“这并非纸上谈兵。”

"It requires the right incentives, the right stakeholders, the right analysis and sufficient capital, but it can happen."

“它需要恰当的激励机制、合适的利益相关方、科学的分析以及充足的资本，但这是可以实现的。”

E

How these factors come together for a particular project—and whether saving destroyed woods is even possible—depends on what kind of ecosystem you have in mind.

这些因素能否在某个具体项目中协同发挥作用——以及拯救已被毁坏的森林是否真的可行——取决于你所设想的是哪种生态系统。

Secondary forests in the Amazon are different from Texas pines recovering after wildfires or the Arctic timber woods that cover much of Sweden.

亚马逊的次生林不同于得克萨斯州野火后正在恢复的松林，也不同于覆盖瑞典大片国土的北极林木。

Each provides different reasons for reforestation programs and each has particular needs.

不同的森林为造林项目提供了不同的理由，也各有其特殊需求。

In the dry conditions around Matamorisca and similar areas in Spain, Land Life Company worries about rapid desertification.

在马塔莫里卡及西班牙类似地区的干旱环境中，生命土地公司（Land Life Company）担心的是快速荒漠化问题。

As its focus is on restoring an ecosystem, it works with organisations that don't expect their money back.

由于公司的重点在于恢复生态系统，它与那些不指望收回资金的组织合作。

F

With around 600 hectares replanted around the world since 2015 and another 1,100 planned for this year, the company's drive fits into the Bonn Challenge, a global effort to restore 150 million hectares of the world's deforested and degraded land by 2020.

自2015年以来，该公司已在全球重新种植了约600公顷，今年还计划再种植1100公顷；该公司的努力与“波恩挑战”相契合——这是一项全球性行动，旨在到2020年恢复全球1.5亿公顷的毁林和退化土地。

That's an area roughly the size of Iran or Mongolia.

这一面积大致相当于伊朗或蒙古的国土面积。

By 2030, the goal is to reach 350 million hectares—20% more land than India.

到2030年，目标是达到3.5亿公顷——比印度的国土面积还多20%。

These targets include both the rehabilitation of forested areas that have lost density or look a bit weak (a process known as 'restoration' in forestry jargon) and the recovery of forest cover in areas where it was completely cleared (what's known as 'reforestation').

这些目标既包括对林木密度下降或长势疲弱的林区进行修复（林业术语称之为“生态恢复”），也包括在森林被彻底砍伐的地区重新造林（即所谓“造林”）。

G

That global target is broken into smaller pieces and takes shape in Latin America as the 20x20 Initiative, an effort to contribute 20 million hectares to the overall goal by initiating small to medium projects with the political backing of governments.

这一全球目标被分解为若干子目标，在拉丁美洲落地为“20x20倡议”——该项目旨在以中小规模项目为依托，在各国政府的政治支持下，为整体目标贡献2000万公顷林地。

Unlike Land Life Company, this region-wide project makes the economic and business case for reforestation, even though they are after biodiversity conservation.

与陆地生命公司不同，这个覆盖整个区域的项目从经济和商业角度论证了造林的可行性，尽管其最终目的同样是保护生物多样性。

"You need to bring in private sector money," says WRI's Vergara, who leads the initiative, "and that capital needs to see a return on their investment."

世界资源研究所的维尔加拉是该倡议的负责人，他说：“你需要引入私营部门的资金，而这些资本需要看到投资回报。”

A study he led predicts Latin America would see an estimated net present value of about $23bn over a 50-year period if it reaches its goal.

由他主导的一项研究预测，如果拉丁美洲实现其目标，未来50年内该地区将获得约230亿美元的净现值。

H

Money can come from timber sales in sustainably managed forests or from harvesting 'non-wood products' like nuts, oils and fruits from the trees.

资金可以来自可持续经营林木的木材销售，也可以来自从树木中采集坚果、油脂和果实等“非木质产品”。

You can take note of how much carbon dioxide your forest is capturing and sell carbon credits to companies keen to offset their emissions.

你可以记录森林吸收了多少二氧化碳，然后将碳信用额出售给希望抵消自身排放的企业。

Or you can even grow the forest hoping that biodiversity will attract ecotourists who'd pay for lodging, birdwatching tours and meals.

或者，你甚至可以培育森林，寄望于生物多样性能够吸引生态旅游者，从而带来住宿、观鸟之旅和餐饮方面的收入。

I

Still, these backers are not your high-street bank.

不过，这些投资方并非你平时光顾的银行。

Money for the 20x20 Initiative comes mostly from financial organisations with triple goal—a modest return on their investment, environmental benefits and social gains—known as impact investors.

“20x20倡议”的资金主要来自具有三重目标的金融机构——在投资上获得适度回报、产生环境效益并取得社会效益——这类机构被称为影响力投资者。

Take the German fund 12Tree for instance, one of the 20x20 partners.

以德国基金12Tree为例，它是“20x20倡议”的合作方之一。

It put $9.5 million into Cuango, a 1,455-hectare property on the Caribbean coast of Panama that combines a commercial cocoa tree plantation with timber extraction from a sustainably managed secondary forest.

该基金向位于巴拿马加勒比海沿岸、占地1455公顷的库安戈项目投入了950万美元，该项目将商业可可种植园与可持续经营的次生林木材采伐结合起来。

With their money, they reforested a former cattle ranch, provided high-quality jobs for the surrounding communities and made a return on their investment.

借助这笔资金，他们重新造林了原为牧场的土地，为周边社区提供了高质量的就业机会，同时获得了投资回报。

J

Even on land cleared decades ago and currently used by farmers, some crops can coexist with the forest, if you find the right balance.

即便是在几十年前就已开垦、目前由农民耕种的土地上，只要找到恰当的平衡，一些作物也能与森林共存。

Although not technically reforestation, agroforestry (a system of land use in which harvestable trees or shrubs are grown among or around crops or on pasture land, as a means of preserving or enhancing the productivity of the land) provides an opportunity for small-holder farmers to sustain their livelihoods while adding some forest cover to their farms.

农林复合经营虽然严格来说不算重新造林，但它（一种将可收获的树木或灌木种植在作物或牧场周围或之间的土地利用方式，旨在保护或提升土地生产力）为小农户提供了一个机会，让他们在为农场增添一些森林覆盖的同时维持生计。

K

A global project called Breedcafs is studying how trees behave in coffee farms, hoping to find crop varieties that manage to grow under the shade of trees.

一个名为Breedcafs的全球项目正在研究树木在咖啡园中的生长状况，希望找到能在树荫下成功生长的作物品种。

Coffee grows naturally in such forests, so copying that in farms is taking the crop back to its roots.

咖啡原本就生长在这类森林中，因此在农场中复制这种模式，就是让作物回归其本源。

"By reintroducing trees in the landscape, we impact positively humidity, rain capture, soil conservation and biodiversity preservation," says coffee expert Benoit Bertrand, who leads the project from the French Agricultural Research Centre for International Development.

法国农业发展国际研究中心（CIRAD）的咖啡专家贝努瓦·贝特朗（Benoit Bertrand）领导该项目，他说："通过在景观中重新引入树木，我们对湿度、雨水截留、土壤保护和生物多样性保护产生了积极影响。"

Bertrand is analysing which of dozens of coffee varieties fits better for this system.

贝特朗正在分析数十种咖啡品种中哪一种最适合这种种植体系。

A similar approach can be applied to land with cocoa, vanilla and fruit trees.

类似的方法也可用于种植可可、香草和果树的土地。

L

Not every piece of land is fit for reforestation.

并非所有土地都适合重新造林。

Vergara's partners look for safe investments, and even Land Life Company only runs major projects in what they consider "low-risk" countries.

韦尔加拉（Vergara）的合作伙伴注重安全投资，即便是生命土地公司（Land Life Company）也只在他们认为"低风险"的国家开展大型项目。

"We tend to avoid large-scale operations in countries in some parts of the Middle East or Africa where the permanence is not secured," says Ruys.

Ruys说："我们倾向于避免在中东或非洲部分地区的国家开展大规模项目，因为那里的长期稳定性无法得到保障。"

M

But in the right location, perhaps all you need is time.

但在合适的地方，或许你所需要的只是时间。

In the Central Pacific of Costa Rica, the 330-hectare National Wildlife Refuge Baru looks nothing like the cattle ranch it was before 1987, when Jack Ewing decided to turn this large estate into an ecotourism destination.

在哥斯达黎加中部太平洋沿岸，占地330公顷的巴鲁国家野生动物保护区看起来与1987年之前的养牛场截然不同——那一年，杰克·尤因（Jack Ewing）决定将这片大型庄园改造成生态旅游目的地。

Instead of intervening, a friend told him to let nature have its way.

一位朋友没有选择插手，而是劝他顺其自然。

Baru's former grazing lands are now thriving woods and the property boasts over 150 hectares of secondary forests with no intervention from humans.

巴鲁昔日的牧场如今已变成茂密的林地，这片庄园拥有150多公顷的次生林，完全依靠自然生长，未加人工干预。

In the past 10 years, animals such as howling monkeys, scarlet macaws and even migratory cougars have returned to the refuge's land, boosting tourism and revitalising the ecosystem.

过去十年间，吼猴、红金刚鹦鹉甚至迁徙的美洲狮等动物纷纷回到这片保护区，带动了旅游业的发展，也让生态系统焕发了生机。

Ewing, now 75, explains this success using the words his friend chose three decades ago: "In Costa Rica, when you stop managing the shrubs, the jungle comes back for its revenge."

现年75岁的尤因用三十年前那位朋友的话来解释这一成功：'在哥斯达黎加，当你不再打理灌木丛时，丛林就会卷土重来，展开报复。'

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1448 词 · 22 句

P1

What does self-discipline look like at work?

自律在工作中是什么样子的？

Sometimes, it's the ability to resist temptation, such as distractions to avoid a particular task.

有时候，自律是一种抵制诱惑的能力，比如抵御让人逃避某项任务的干扰。

Other times it's the ability to persist with a task when your energy and motivation seem low.

另一些时候，自律是在精力和动力似乎都很低落时仍能坚持完成任务的能力。

P2

What do you believe about the nature of your own self-discipline?

你对自己的自律本质上持何种看法？

Is it something that runs on internal energy that is spent with use?

它是一种消耗内在能量、越用越少的东西吗？

Or is it something that is bolstered as you apply it and persist?

还是一种越运用、越坚持就越能被强化的东西？

P3

Your answer matters, as it determines how much self-discipline you experience.

你的答案很重要，因为它决定了你能体验到多少自律。

For years, research in psychology pointed to self-control as a resource that is depleted with use and needs to be restored through breaks, rest, or sleep.

多年来，心理学研究一直认为自我控制是一种会被消耗的资源，需要通过休息、小憩或睡眠来恢复。

More recently, however, research points to the importance of mindset.

然而，近年来的研究则强调心态的重要性。

If you view self-discipline as an expendable resource, your willpower tends to fade with use.

如果你把自律视为一种可消耗的资源，你的意志力往往就会越用越弱。

If you believe that exercising your self-control strengthens and fuels it, that is more likely to be your experience as you resist temptation or push yourself to get something done that is not appealing.

如果你相信运用自我控制会使其更强、为其补充能量，那么当你抵制诱惑或逼迫自己完成一件毫无吸引力的事情时，你更可能获得这样的体验。

P4

If you don't already view self-discipline as a force that feeds itself, what might you do?

如果你还没有把自律看作一种能自我滋养的力量，你可以怎么做呢？

Reflect on times that you had initial difficulty resisting temptation, getting started on something, or sticking with it, but pushed ahead and accomplished the task or met the goal.

回想那些起初难以抵制诱惑、难以开始行动或难以坚持下去，但最终仍然奋力前行并完成任务或实现目标的经历。

That's the kind of experience we're talking about with this particular belief about self-discipline, and you've already had such an experience, so you've shown that it's possible.

我们所说的关于自律的这种特定信念，指的就是这类经历，而且你已经有过这样的经历，所以你已经证明了这是有可能的。

When you feel your willpower and motivation fading, remind yourself of those previous experiences and imagine the same happening now.

当你的意志力和动力逐渐消退时，提醒自己回忆那些以往的经历，并想象此刻同样的事情正在发生。

P5

What if your experiences have always seemed to indicate that your self-control diminishes with use, and you need breaks to recharge?

如果你的过往经历似乎一直都表明，你的自控力会随着使用而不断消耗，你需要休息来恢复精力，那该怎么办？

There's nothing wrong with that as long as you can afford to take those breaks.

只要你承受得起这些休息，这也没什么不好。

If not, remind yourself that not everyone experiences self-discipline as a depletable resource and that your experience might be different if you focus on gathering more energy at this point rather than focusing on how difficult it is to continue or the need for a break.

但如果承受不起，就提醒自己，并非所有人都把自律视为一种会耗尽的资源，而且如果你此刻专注于积蓄更多能量，而不是专注于继续下去有多艰难或者需要休息，你的经历可能会有所不同。

P6

Also, if you know someone who is a role model of self-discipline, picture that person in your situation, persisting in the task.

此外，如果你认识某个自律方面堪称榜样的人，就设想那个人处于你目前的境况中，坚持完成这项任务。

Regardless of how you do it, as you push forward to continue to accomplish progress, be sure to mentally reinforce yourself by taking pride in your ability to exert more willpower as you persist.

无论你采用哪种方式，在你继续推进、不断取得进展的过程中，一定要在心理上肯定自己，为坚持的过程中能够调动更多意志力而感到自豪。

P7

The benefits of exerting willpower are cumulative.

意志力的运用带来的益处是累积的。

Your self-discipline is akin to a muscle, and each time you resist temptation or persist in a task despite a lack of motivation, you strengthen that muscle, leading to less effort and resistance in similar instances in the future.

你的自律就像一块肌肉，每一次你抵制诱惑或在缺乏动力的情况下坚持完成一项任务，你都在强化这块肌肉，从而在未来遇到类似情况时付出更少的努力、遇到更小的阻力。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2444 词 · 22 句

P1

Smartphones have always posed challenges for parents of teens.

智能手机始终给青少年的父母带来种种挑战。

From social media apps to excessive screen time, the digital world is as threatening as the physical.

从社交媒体应用到屏幕使用时间过长，数字世界和现实世界一样令人担忧。

P2

A new study shows that about half of parents of teens are worried about their teens being exposed to explicit content online.

一项新研究显示，约有一半的青少年父母担心自己的孩子在网上接触到露骨内容。

This is a valid concern, of course.

这种担忧当然是合情合理的。

But there are ways to alleviate the spread of explicit content, from changing the settings in their kids' phone to monitoring such content with apps like Bark.

但缓解露骨内容传播的方法是有的，比如更改孩子手机的设置，或使用Bark等应用监控此类内容。

P3

Just as alarming, if not more so, is that the amount of time teens spend on social media can lead to anxiety and depression.

同样令人担忧、甚至有过之而无不及的是，青少年花在社交媒体上的时间会引发焦虑和抑郁。

P4

Excessive screen time has detrimental effects, for a lot of obvious reasons.

出于许多显而易见的原因，屏幕使用时间过长会带来有害影响。

Constant media consumption inhibits healthy activities associated with childhood, like spending time outside, exercising, talking to friends and family.

持续不断地浏览媒体会妨碍那些与童年息息相关的健康活动，比如到户外活动、锻炼身体、与朋友和家人交流。

If it interferes with sleep, that's even worse.

如果它还干扰睡眠，那就更糟糕了。

P5

Ample research shows too much smartphone use produces significant anxiety, depression, and feelings of isolation in teens.

大量研究显示，过度使用智能手机会引发青少年严重的焦虑、抑郁和孤独感。

Teens log on to social media or spend time online trying to make connections, but end up feeling more alone and sad than ever.

青少年登录社交媒体或上网，本想与人建立联系，结果却比以往任何时候都更加孤独和悲伤。

Faceless communication not only produces feelings of isolation and loneliness but increases instances of online bullying and susceptibility to criminal conducts.

不见面的交流不仅会带来孤立感和孤独感，还会增加网络欺凌的发生率，并使青少年更容易遭受犯罪行为的侵害。

P6

Though I've banned most social media, I've still battled smartphone time with my own kids.

虽然我已经禁止孩子们使用大多数社交媒体，但和自己的孩子在手机使用时间上的博弈依然让我头疼不已。

A lot of the time I wish I could throw my kids' smartphones into the nearest lake.

很多时候，我恨不得把孩子们的手机扔进最近的湖里。

The convenience of communication—when they need a ride home from school after practice, for instance—often feels like it isn't worth the battle to keep their screen time low and productive, and continue to encourage "real-life" activities outside and with friends and family.

沟通的便利——比如孩子们放学后练完体育需要搭车回家时——往往让人觉得，为了让他们少看屏幕、把时间用在有意义的事情上，同时继续鼓励他们参加户外活动以及与朋友和家人的面对面相处而不断抗争，实在得不偿失。

I understand the addiction and often tell them I'm not sure how I would have handled having a smartphone as a teen.

我理解这种成瘾性，也常对他们说，如果我十几岁时就有智能手机，不知道自己会怎样应对。

As an adult, I too, struggle with overuse.

作为成年人，我自己也在和过度使用手机作斗争。

P7

In a recent TV interview to promote a new film about a mother and daughter navigating social media use, actress Kate Winslet expressed her view on teens and social media—and her concern mirrors the trends.

在最近一次为一部关于母女二人共同应对社交媒体使用问题的新片做宣传的电视采访中，女演员凯特·温斯莱特表达了她对青少年与社交媒体的看法——她的担忧与当下的趋势不谋而合。

"I do definitely feel that the world of social media is frightening to parents because we don't really know what's there," she said.

她说：'我确实觉得社交媒体的世界令父母感到恐惧，因为我们根本不知道那里究竟有什么。'

"We don't know how our children are using it. We don't know the effects that it's really having on them."

'我们不知道孩子们是怎么使用它的。我们不知道它究竟对他们产生了什么影响。'

P8

I don't think the U.S. government should regulate social media and teen smartphone use, but it is a vital topic for parents to understand and take seriously.

我认为美国政府不应监管社交媒体和青少年的手机使用，但这是父母必须了解并认真对待的一个至关重要的话题。

Unfortunately, now, parents don't just have to worry about real-world dangers, but all the ones sneaking online and the effects they have on teenagers and their mental health.

遗憾的是，如今父母不仅要提防现实世界中的种种危险，还要警惕网络上潜藏的所有隐患，以及它们对青少年及其心理健康造成的影响。

第 3 套 · 4 篇 · 共 2,407 词

Section A · 选词填空（填空已还原）292 词 · 16 句

词库（绿 = 本题所选）：A) ascertainB) bufferC) correlatedD) craveE) demographicsF) entailG) legitimatelyH) mandatedI) potentialJ) proximityK) rigidL) statisticsM) substantiallyN) traitO) underlie

Imagine you and a stranger are sitting on a couch.

想象一下，你和一个陌生人坐在一张沙发上。

How close would you sit to them?

你会坐得离对方多近？

It turns out that how much of a buffer zone you prefer may reveal a lot about your personality.

事实证明，你偏好的缓冲区大小可能会在很大程度上揭示你的性格。

In one study, researchers sought to ascertain how our personality may be linked to our preferred amount of personal space.

在一项研究中，研究人员试图探究我们的性格如何与我们偏好的个人空间大小相关联。

The researchers found that people who scored higher in the trait of agreeableness tended to sit closer to others, as did people who were more open and outgoing.

研究人员发现，宜人性这一特质得分较高的人往往坐得离别人更近，更开放、更外向的人也是如此。

Other individual differences, such as gender and age, can also affect how much personal space we crave.

其他个体差异，如性别和年龄，也会影响我们渴望多少个人空间。

Women tend to prefer more physical distance from strangers or acquaintances and older adults also generally tend to prefer to keep more distance between themselves and others.

女性往往偏好与陌生人或熟人保持更大的身体距离，而年长者通常也倾向于与他人保持更多距离。

Beyond reflecting our personality and demographics, the amount of personal space we prefer is also linked to our cultural backgrounds.

我们偏好的个人空间大小，除了反映我们的性格和人口统计特征外，还与我们的文化背景有关。

In a study involving participants from 42 countries, researchers found that around the world people's desired amount of personal space varied substantially.

在一项涵盖42个国家参与者的跨文化研究中，研究人员发现，世界各地人们所期望的个人空间大小存在显著差异。

For example, in Romania, the average amount of personal space people said they would prefer from a stranger was more than 4 feet, whereas in Argentina it was roughly half that.

例如，在罗马尼亚，人们表示与陌生人之间偏好的平均个人空间距离超过4英尺，而在阿根廷，这一距离大约只有前者的一半。

What might underlie this variation?

这种差异的背后可能是什么原因？

The researchers looked at a number of potential factors, but the only one that was significantly correlated with preferred interpersonal distance was average temperature.

研究人员考察了多种潜在因素，但唯一与偏好的人际距离显著相关的因素是平均气温。

The warmer the place, it seems, the "warmer" the people and the less space they needed between them and others.

地方越温暖，人们似乎也越“热情”，彼此之间的距离需求也越小。

Personal space may also reflect interpersonal attraction.

个人空间也可能反映出人际吸引。

Researchers have found that our chosen physical proximity also appears to reflect how much we like others.

研究者发现，我们所选择的身体上的亲疏程度，似乎也能反映出我们对他人的好恶。

The more people would like to be friends with the other person, the closer they sat to them.

人们越想与对方成为朋友，坐得离对方就越近。

Section B · 长篇阅读（信息匹配）1223 词 · 48 句

A

The cost of a mortgage or rents, meeting monthly utility bills, paying for a new car—all headaches most people get to worry about from their 20s.

房贷或房租、每月缴纳水电燃气费、买一辆新车——这些麻烦事大多从二十多岁起就成了人们的心头之忧。

But while some may struggle to pay their bills after a few too many nights out at the start of the month, others will have a tightly followed plan for their repayments.

不过，有些人月初连着潇洒了几个晚上后就可能捉襟见肘、难以付清账单，另一些人却会严格按照计划还款。

So what is the difference between the shrewd saver and the more reckless spender?

那么，精明的储蓄者和大手大脚的消费者之间究竟有何差别？

B

Experts believe it is all about the age you learn how to spend responsibly.

专家认为，关键在于你是在多大年龄学会理性消费的。

Adult money habits are typically set by the age of seven, according to a large-scale study by the government's Money Advice Service and behaviour experts at Cambridge University.

根据政府的货币咨询服务（Money Advice Service）与剑桥大学行为专家开展的一项大规模研究，成年后的金钱习惯通常在七岁前就已定型。

By then, most children have grasped how to recognise the value of money and are capable of complex functions such as planning ahead, delaying a decision until later and understanding that some choices are irreversible.

到那时，大多数孩子已经懂得认识金钱的价值，并能完成一些复杂的行为，比如提前规划、把决定推迟到以后再做，以及明白有些选择是不可逆的。

C

Tomorrow marks the start of Global Money Week, a worldwide initiative to help children make smart financial decisions and learn that "money matters matter".

明天是全球金钱周（Global Money Week）的开幕日，这是一项全球性活动，旨在帮助儿童做出明智的财务决策，并让他们认识到“理财很重要”。

But for many young people in the UK, it's a matter of too little, too late.

但对英国的许多年轻人来说，这事来得太少、太晚了。

"The habits of mind which influence the ways children approach complex problems and decisions, including financial ones, are largely determined in the first few years of life," says Dr David Whitebread, the co-author of the study.

该研究的合著者戴维·怀特布雷德博士说：“影响孩子处理复杂问题和决策——包括财务决策——方式的思维习惯，主要是在人生最初几年形成的。”

And this core behaviour is very likely to influence their financial decisions when they are adults, he says.

他说，这种核心行为极有可能影响他们成年后的财务决策。

D

The study also urges parents not to underestimate their own good and bad money habits.

研究还呼吁父母不要低估自身金钱习惯的好坏所带来的影响。

"Since young children have few monetary resources they control independently, it is the basic approaches and skills which are modelled, discussed and demonstrated by parents that are likely to be influential levers, breeding efficient habits and practices," it says.

报告写道：“由于年幼的孩子很少有自己能独立支配的金钱资源，父母所示范、讨论和展示的基本方法与技能，才是可能产生影响的关键杠杆，有助于培养高效的习惯与做法。”

E

Parents who choose to help their children learn how to plan ahead, reflect on the past and self-regulate can make a huge difference in promoting good financial behaviour in the future, explains Whitebread.

怀特布雷德指出，选择帮助孩子学会未雨绸缪、反思过去以及自我调节的父母，对孩子未来养成良好的理财行为有着巨大的促进作用。

According to a survey by M&G Investments, the vast majority (83%) of parents recognise the value of teaching their children about money.

据M&G Investments的一项调查显示，绝大多数（83%）的父母都认识到教孩子认识金钱的价值。

However, this concern does not always carry through to practice—one in six do not feel confident enough to do it.

然而，这种意识并不总能落实到行动上——六分之一的父母对此没有足够的信心去实践。

More than one in every four parents think children should take responsibility for understanding money themselves.

超过四分之一的父母认为，孩子应当自己负起了解金钱的责任。

F

"There's joy in the fact that young children are spontaneous and live life in the moment, unrestrained by adult problems. But teaching children about money when they are young is a good way for parents to help them learn to delay satisfaction, so that the child thinks: 'I'd like to do this but I'm not going to'. Hard evidence suggests, if you want your child to do well in life, the earlier you can start trying to teach them inhibition and self-discipline, the better,"

“小孩子天真随性、活在当下、不受成人烦恼的束缚，这本身就令人欣喜。但趁孩子年幼时教他们认识金钱，是帮助他们学会延迟满足的好办法，让孩子能够想到：‘我很想做这件事，但我还是忍住不做。’确凿的证据表明，如果你想让孩子在人生中有所成就，越早开始培养他们的克制力和自律能力，效果就越好。”

says Whitebread, pointing to research that has shown people in prison frequently have poor impulse control, a personality trait that can affect relationships and long-term health.

怀特布雷德说道，他援引的研究表明，监狱中的服刑人员往往自控力较差，而这种性格特征会影响人际关系和长期健康。

G

But how should a parent try to teach their child to spend responsibly?

但是，父母应该如何教孩子理性消费呢？

One simple way is to give them pocket money and then help them to analyse and learn from any foolish spending decisions.

一种简单的方法是给孩子零花钱，然后帮助他们分析愚蠢的消费决定并从中吸取教训。

H

Another is through board games which can help teach financial responsibility.

另一种方法是借助有助于培养理财责任感的桌面游戏。

Pop to the Shops, a shopping board game for five-to-nine-year-olds, has been one of Orchard Toys' top 10 bestselling board games every year for the past 15 years.

《Pop to the Shops》是一款面向五至九岁儿童的购物主题桌面游戏，过去15年来每年都是Orchard Toys十大畅销桌游之一。

To win, players must use fake coins to purchase everyday grocery items from various shops around the board, and give the other players who visit their shop the correct change.

为了获胜，玩家必须使用游戏币在棋盘上的各家商店购买日常杂货商品，并在其他玩家光顾其店铺时找零。

If they don't have enough money, they must purchase a cheaper item.

如果钱不够，他们就必须购买更便宜的商品。

"It acts as a conversation starter about money—parents can fill in the gaps," says Orchard Toys product manager Rachael Sutcliffe.

“它起到了开启金钱话题的作用——家长可以补充讲解，”Orchard Toys产品经理雷切尔·萨特克利夫说道。

I

There are also some pocket-money-tracking and payment apps, such as RoosterMoney and goHenry, which are designed to be used by under-sevens, and which boast that they can help children develop good money habits.

此外还有一些零花钱记录与支付类应用，例如RoosterMoney和goHenry，专为七岁以下儿童设计，并宣称能帮助孩子养成良好的金钱习惯。

This is dependent on the children receiving enough pocket money to need an app to track their spending, which research suggests is not always the case.

但这取决于孩子拿到的零花钱是否多到需要用应用来记录支出，而研究表明事实并非总是如此。

J

Surprisingly, given the research about habit-forming in young children, there are very few gaming apps that can help under-sevens—Pigby's Fair, developed by NatWest, being a notable exception.

令人意外的是，尽管已有大量关于幼儿习惯养成的研究，专门帮助七岁以下儿童的益智类游戏应用却少之又少——由NatWest开发的Pigby's Fair是一个显著的例外。

In this, children who solve basic puzzles will earn virtual money selling what they have made at different stalls in a fair, then spend or save it.

在游戏中，孩子们通过解开基础谜题赚取虚拟钱币，在集市的不同摊位上出售自己的作品，然后选择花掉或存起来。

But the need to shift virtual money around, and take into account interest payments and pocket money bonuses, makes it a complex financial education tool.

然而，虚拟钱币的流转操作、利息收益以及零花钱奖励等因素，使它成为一个相当复杂的金融教育工具。

K

There are plenty of online games which focus on coin recognition and counting with money, such as the Igloo Shopping game or Top Marks' Coins Game.

网上有许多侧重于钱币辨认和用钱计数的游戏，例如Igloo Shopping游戏和Top Marks的Coins Game。

So what if children find such games boring?

那么，如果孩子觉得这类游戏很无聊怎么办？

An exciting (and free) real-world option for parents to use is a visit to Metro Bank.

家长不妨带孩子去Metro Bank走一走，这是一个既令人兴奋又免费的现实选择。

Although, like most banks, it does not allow children to open an account until they turn 11, it specifically tries to offer young children a chance to learn about money inside its branches by inviting kids to use the "magic money machines" to count their coins.

虽然和大多数银行一样，它不允许11岁以下儿童开户，但它专门在网点内为年幼的孩子提供学习金钱知识的机会，邀请他们使用“魔法数币机”来清点硬币。

Children who accurately guess how much was in their piggy bank get a prize, and free candies are handed out when the coins are exchanged for banknotes.

准确猜出储蓄罐里有多少钱的孩子会得到奖品，硬币兑换纸币时还会免费分发糖果。

The bank does not take a commission from the transaction.

银行不从这类交易中收取手续费。

L

At six years old, my daughter Flora is getting to grips with her finances early.

我六岁的女儿弗洛拉很早就在学习如何管理自己的财务了。

She tested out a number of games aimed at teaching children her age the value of money.

她试玩了几款旨在教导她这个年龄段的孩子认识金钱价值的游戏。

She enjoyed them all, but best, in her view, is Money Match Cafe, a board game where she must work out the value of different denominations of coin in her imaginary restaurant, her "customers" being her stuffed toys.

这些游戏她都喜欢，但在她看来最好的一款是《金钱匹配咖啡馆》（Money Match Cafe），这是一款棋盘游戏，她必须在自己的模拟餐厅里算出不同面值硬币的价值，她的“顾客”就是她的毛绒玩具。

"It was really fun because you get to serve food to your own toys on a little tray with a napkin on it, and then you can hug them," she says.

她说：“真的很好玩，因为你可以用铺着餐巾的小托盘给自己的玩具上菜，然后还可以抱抱它们。”

M

"If you're six and you play this game, you'll learn that you might not have the right amount of money for what you want to buy. You just have to cross your fingers and hope. It's a good challenge, and I enjoyed adding the coins up to see how much they made. But I don't like the name. Money Match Cafe is not a good name for a cafe. I would have liked it more if it had been Flora's Cafe or Fantastically Baked Lunches."

“如果你六岁玩这个游戏，你会学到自己想要买的东西可能钱不够。你只能祈祷走运。这是个很好的挑战，我很喜欢把硬币加起来看看一共是多少。但我不喜欢这个名字。Money Match Cafe 不是个好咖啡馆的名字。如果叫‘弗洛拉咖啡馆’或者‘奇妙烘焙午餐’，我会更喜欢。”

N

Seven out of ten teachers think children are now faced with financial decisions earlier.

十分之七的教师认为，如今孩子们更早就要面临与财务相关的决定。

On Wednesday, Lifesavers—a new project which promotes financial education in primary schools—is hosting a conference to encourage teachers to join its programme and get free classroom resources and training.

周三，Lifesavers——一个在小学推广金融教育的新项目——将举办一场会议，鼓励教师加入其计划，免费获取课堂教学资源和培训。

Almost two-thirds of millennials wish they had received more money advice at a younger age, according to a Santander survey.

桑坦德银行（Santander）的一项调查显示，近三分之二的千禧一代希望在更年轻的时候能得到更多理财方面的建议。

O

Only a third of parents involve their children in discussions about their household finances, even though less than half of young people aged seven to seventeen receive financial education at school, another survey shows.

另一项调查显示，只有三分之一的父母会让孩子参与家庭财务的讨论，尽管不到一半的七至十七岁年轻人在学校接受过金融教育。

P

Most adults believe their parents have had the biggest influence on their money behaviour.

大多数成年人认为，父母对自己理财行为的影响最大。

So when should they start trying to instil good habits?

那么，他们应该从什么时候开始培养良好的理财习惯呢？

As early as possible before the age of seven, research suggests.

研究表明，越早越好，最迟不要晚于七岁。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 1444 词 · 12 句

P1

Three artists have brought a lawsuit against Stability AI, DeviantArt, and Midjourney, alleging that the text-to-image AI tools have infringed the rights of thousands of artists.

三名艺术家已对Stability AI、DeviantArt和Midjourney提起诉讼，指控这些文生图AI工具侵犯了数千名艺术家的权利。

P2

The lawsuit claims that the Stable Diffusion tool used by Stability AI, DeviantArt, and Midjourney was trained on billions of copyrighted images downloaded from the internet and used by the companies "without compensation or consent from the artists."

诉讼称，Stability AI、DeviantArt和Midjourney所使用的Stable Diffusion工具，是用从互联网上下载的数十亿张受版权保护的图片训练而成，这些公司“未经艺术家补偿或同意”便使用了这些图片。

P3

The artists are represented by The Joseph Saveri Law Firm and Matthew Butterick.

这些艺术家由Joseph Saveri律师事务所和Matthew Butterick代理。

P4

"If Stable Diffusion and similar products are allowed to continue to operate as they do now, the foreseeable result is they will replace the very artists whose stolen works power these AI products with whom they are competing," the law firm wrote in a press release.

该律师事务所在新闻稿中写道：“如果Stable Diffusion及类似产品被允许继续照常运营，可以预见的结果是，它们将取代那些被盗作品为这些AI产品提供动力、并与之竞争的艺术家本身。”

"AI image products are not just an infringement of artists' rights; they will eliminate 'artist' as a viable career path. In addition to obtaining compensation for the wrongful conduct, this lawsuit seeks to prevent that outcome and ensure these products follow the same rules as any other new technology that involves the use of massive amounts of intellectual property. If streaming music can be accomplished within the law, so can AI products."

“AI图像产品不仅是对艺术家权利的侵犯，它们还将使‘艺术家’这一职业不再可行。除了为不当行为获得赔偿外，本诉讼还旨在防止这一结果，并确保这些产品遵守与任何其他涉及大量知识产权使用的新技术相同的规则。如果流媒体音乐能够在法律框架内实现，AI产品同样可以。”

P5

Since text-to-image generators have increased in popularity in the past year, many artists have been vocalizing their opposition to AI art after seeing their own distorted signatures appear in AI art and finding out that their work was used to train the tools without their permission.

过去一年里，文生图生成器日益流行，许多艺术家在看到自己的签名被扭曲地出现在AI艺术作品中、并得知自己的作品未经许可就被用于训练这些工具后，纷纷公开表达对AI艺术的反对。

P6

Not only are many artists furious at the possible copyright violations of AI art, but they have also been frustrated by how AI tools are able to sidestep the labor and art processes they are required to go through.

许多艺术家不仅对AI艺术可能侵犯版权感到愤怒，也对AI工具能够绕过他们必须经历的劳动和艺术创作过程感到沮丧。

As a result, many artists have been trying to ban AI art from art-sharing platforms.

因此，许多艺术家一直在努力推动在艺术分享平台上封禁AI艺术。

P7

As AI innovation continues at a rapid speed, it seems that artists and lawmakers are trying to put a brake on further developments before it's too late.

随着AI创新以迅猛的速度持续推进，艺术家和立法者似乎正试图在为时已晚之前踩下刹车。

P8

Many tech enthusiasts are still rallying around the promise of AI tools to create detailed images cheaply and quickly—anonymous tech supporters have already published a response to Butterick and Joseph Saveri's lawsuit on a website, using the "fair use" clause as the basis for why copyrighted images could be used by the tool.

许多科技爱好者仍然拥护AI工具的前景，即能够廉价、快速地生成精细图像——匿名的科技支持者已在一个网站上发布了对Butterick和Joseph Saveri诉讼的回应，以“合理使用”条款为依据，主张该工具可以使用受版权保护的图片。

P9

Fair use, a legal doctrine that allows for a limited usage of copyrighted materials without permission by the original creators for purposes such as teaching and news sharing, is what a lot of generative model creators claim that their training data is covered by.

合理使用是一项法律原则，允许在未经原作者许可的情况下，为教学和新闻分享等目的有限度地使用受版权保护的材料，许多生成式模型的创建者声称其训练数据受此条款保护。

However, since fair use applies differently to each case, it's hard to make a blanket statement about all works in a dataset as being applicable.

然而，由于合理使用在每个案件中的适用情况各不相同，很难对数据集中的所有作品是否适用一概而论。

Section C · 仔细阅读 Passage 2448 词 · 26 句

P1

On a warm October morning, Daniel Agok woke up to another disappointing day at his school in Wanyjok, South Sudan.

十月里一个温暖的早晨，丹尼尔·阿戈克醒来，又将在南苏丹万约克的学校迎来令人失望的一天。

The classes he had planned teaching computer skills to aspiring young professionals were canceled, due to an unreliable diesel generator and the absence of a municipal power grid in his area.

他原计划为有志成为专业人才的年轻人教授计算机技能，但由于柴油发电机时断时续，当地又没有市政电网，这些课程只能取消。

Now, he would be losing frustrated customers as well as income.

这下，他不仅会流失那些失望的客户，还会损失收入。

Agok is just one of the hundreds of millions of people in sub-Saharan Africa, many of whom are business owners, who still do not have dependable electricity.

在撒哈拉以南非洲，数亿人至今仍用不上可靠的电力，其中不乏企业主，阿戈克只是其中之一。

This inequitable access to energy continues to put their livelihood and future at risk.

这种能源获取上的不平等，持续威胁着他们的生计与未来。

P2

The current system keeps a lid on the economic talents of people like Agok and his students.

现行体制压制了阿戈克及其学生这类人的经济才能。

We must instead unleash their abilities and let Africa reach its true economic potential.

我们应当转而释放他们的潜能，让非洲充分发挥其经济潜力。

That means wiring Africa with clean, dependable energy.

这就意味着要用清洁、可靠的能源为非洲通电。

P3

Historically, national governments on the continent have led electrification efforts, but fewer than half of the people surveyed say that the national grid is providing a reliable supply of electricity.

从历史上看，非洲大陆各国政府一直是电气化建设的主导者，但受访者中不到一半的人表示国家电网提供了可靠的电力供应。

P4

This has led to expensive and harmful temporary "solutions".

这催生了代价高昂且有害的临时“解决方案”。

In Nigeria alone experts estimate people spend $13 billion every year on fuel for toxic, polluting generators.

仅在尼日利亚，专家估计人们每年就要花130亿美元购买燃油，为有毒、污染环境的发电机供能。

That number will only grow as the continent's population hits 2.5 billion people by 2050.

随着非洲大陆人口到2050年将增至25亿，这一数字还只会继续增长。

Deploying modern grid infrastructure with speed and at scale is essential to replacing these temporary solutions with reliable and affordable alternatives.

要以可靠且价格可负担的替代方案取代这些临时解决方案，就必须快速、大规模地部署现代化电网基础设施。

P5

The rapidly growing private power sector in Africa holds the key to achieving the speed and scale necessary to close the continent's energy access gap.

非洲快速增长的私营电力部门掌握着关键，唯有凭借其所需的速度与规模，才能弥合非洲大陆的能源获取缺口。

Private companies today are building world-class infrastructure and driving forward economic growth with sustainable business models.

如今，私营企业正在建设世界一流基础设施，并以可持续的商业模式推动经济增长。

In fact, many governments agree and have begun to embrace the private sector as partners in providing reliable power at scale.

事实上，许多政府对此表示认同，并已开始把私营部门视为合作伙伴，共同提供大规模的可靠电力。

In South Africa, President Cyril Ramaphosa has streamlined licensing requirements and enlisted the private sector to play a key role.

在南非，总统西里尔·拉马福萨简化了许可要求，并让私营部门发挥关键作用。

P6

South Africa is just one example.

南非只是其中一个例子。

Across the continent, the private sector has delivered power faster and more reliably, and governments are increasingly turning to private partners.

在整个非洲大陆，私营部门供电更快也更可靠，各国政府正越来越多地寻求与私营伙伴合作。

P7

With the right funding and structure in place, the private sector will come together to provide this essential service.

只要资金到位、架构得当，私营部门就会汇聚起来，提供这项必不可少的服务。

After all, electrifying sub-Saharan Africa is a $350 billion opportunity.

毕竟，让撒哈拉以南非洲实现电气化是一个价值3500亿美元的机遇。

P8

Today, Daniel Agok is steadily growing his business in South Sudan.

如今，丹尼尔·阿戈克正在南苏丹稳步发展自己的生意。

His town of Wanyjok was electrified by a private company, providing clean, reliable, and affordable power to his business and his neighbors.

他所在的万尼乔克镇由一家私营公司通了电，为其生意和邻居们提供了清洁、可靠且价格可负担的电力。

Agok now operates his growing computer skills training business smoothly.

阿戈克如今经营着他不断壮大的计算机技能培训业务，运转顺利。

His classes are no longer affected by the smell and the constant noise of the diesel generator, and he saves money with more affordable power.

他的课堂不再受柴油发电机的气味和持续噪声的影响，而且电费更加实惠，帮他省下了开支。

His students are developing hardware and software skills.

他的学生们正在锻炼硬件和软件方面的技能。
